Chapter 601: The Current Situation of the Necromancers

During the Weeping Pine Sea Battle, Qi Ling betrayed the group led by Jian Gui.

Yet upon reflecting on the entire incident, Jian Gui resolved to save Qi Ling.

Qi Ling herself was an undead life form created by Jian Gui through his Soul Refinement Spell. For a time afterward, he trained her as his alchemy assistant. She proved exceptionally capable, and Jian Gui imparted more of his undead legacy to her.

Their relationship remained strong, so much so that when Qi Ling begged for freedom, Jian Gui let her go.

In life, Qi Ling had been a mermaid, deeply devoted to her kind. Even as a necromancer, she returned to protect her people.

Yet her clan regarded her as a mortal enemy, repeatedly placing Qi Ling in mortal peril.

Jian Gui intervened several times, rescuing Qi Ling from danger.

Qi Ling was truly “unrepentant in death,” continuing to aid her clan time and again.

Lady Sea Snake, having obtained intelligence from an unknown source, learned Qi Ling's weakness. She then seized control of Qi Ling's fishman clan, forcing her to become her mole.

Qi Ling had no choice but to comply, yet she constantly plotted her counterattack, striving to regain her freedom.

On Weeping Pine Island, she openly revealed her traitorous status and held back against Cang Xu and the others—a clear declaration of her intentions to Jian Gui and his crew.

On the ghost ship.

Jian Gui and his crew stood at the bow.

Jian Gui's tone was grave: “Qi Ling was forced into becoming an informant. Her clan is her greatest vulnerability.”

“Therefore, we must not only locate Qi Ling and her kin but also attempt to save both simultaneously.”

Both objectives required simultaneous execution.

Advancing either alone would alert the enemy, triggering countermeasures.

This would severely hinder the impending rescue operation.

“We must proceed with caution from here on,” Xiao Wang Zi voiced his concern, “We may very well encounter Lady Sea Snake, or even that gold level assassin.”

He was referring to Wei Ying.

Wei Ying had nearly wiped out the entire group of Jian Gui and Cang Xu. Had Doctor Sui Shi not arrived in time to scare him off, Cang Xu and the others would have faced dire consequences.

Wei Ying also possessed the Shadow Walker's sacred emblem, allowing him to swiftly escape and retreat into the Shadow Plane.

He was a formidable and dangerous adversary.

“But now, we don't have Doctor Sui Shi with us,” Xiao Wang Zi sighed bitterly.

Though he understood Qi Ling's desperate circumstances and the pressure forcing her betrayal, he still felt profound disgust toward her.

Had Jian Gui not insisted on rescuing her, he personally would never have engaged in such actions.

Cang Xu reassured Xiao Wang Zi: “We may not encounter Wei Ying.”

“They won't anticipate our next move.”

"Even if Lady Sea Snake controls Qi Ling's kin, she likely won't oversee them personally. In fact, I suspect her situation is far from favorable."

Lady Sea Snake's familiar was a gold level sea serpent.

It perished in the Underworld River.

As a mere silver level magician, having lost her most potent trump card, she must be under immense pressure. Perhaps she's already struggling to manage her own affairs.

After all, fierce competition exists among pirates themselves.

Even as a member of the legendary Fire Beard Pirates, she navigates a complex, cutthroat environment within this legendary pirate group.

Xiao Wang Zi glanced at Cang Xu and sighed again: “Let's hope so.”

Beside him, the undead ronin Ku Feng adopted a different stance: "I, for one, would relish seeing Lady Sea Snake. The grudge from the Weeping Pine Sea Battle is etched into my memory."

Ku Feng had always considered Weeping Pine Island his home.

During the battle, the island suffered immense destruction. Now, he couldn't return home. As the former gathering place for the undead, it had been completely exposed.

Thus, Ku Feng harbored deep hatred toward both Lady Sea Snake and Wei Ying.

"Alright, I'll now fully merge with the ghost ship to dive beneath the waves. Prepare yourselves." After giving this order, Ship Ghost merged completely with the vessel.

Only in this “merged” state could he unleash the ghost ship's full potential.

The ghost ship swiftly submerged, the surface mist gradually dissipating.

As it descended deeper, the water pressure intensified dramatically.

Cang Xu and the others had already cast protective spells to shield themselves against the pressure.

“The ghost ship is quite impressive, but it's not a conventional magic ship. Its functionality is far from comprehensive.”

“Essentially, it's an undead life form.”

“Of course, it possesses tremendous potential to become an exceptional magic ship!”

Cang Xu now understood the ghost ship quite well.

The ghost ship contained almost no alchemy components, but it possessed a magic pool.

This was because the ghost ship itself carries the magic pool, unlike the Deep Sea Monster Fish, which was constructed by outsiders.

In a sense, the ghost ship could be considered a necromancer.

However, its intelligence was extremely low, and it could only act on instinct.

Therefore, its abilities could not be fully unleashed. Only when Jian Gui served as its “spirit,” merging his intellect with the ghost ship's power, could its full might be unleashed.

Take the Deep Sea Monster Fish, for instance - would Cang Xu and his crew need to cast spells themselves to counteract the water pressure?

Cang Xu had thus proposed to Jian Gui: could they first upgrade the ghost ship, transforming it into a fully functional magic ship?

Jian Gui replied with a resigned tone: Forging the ghost ship into a complete magic ship is a long and arduous journey. The ghost ship's magic pool operated on negative energy, rendering nearly all commercially available alchemy components incompatible. Even powerful figures within the necromancer orthodoxy had sought to enhance their ghost ships by commissioning alchemists for custom designs, yet the results had been disappointing.

A qualified alchemy artifact required extensive practical testing.

Even if alchemy components suitable for ghost ships were designed, they still demanded extensive practical application. Through use, flaws were discovered, necessitating continuous refinement and iteration before a relatively mature alchemy component could be finalized.

This process demanded immense time, resources, and manpower.

A single necromancer could not independently sustain such an endeavor.

Conventional alchemy artifacts involved countless individuals and factions collaborating—designers, users, traders, and more—forming a vast, interconnected system of investment and return.

Necromancers themselves were scarce.

This school of magic had existed for a relatively short time.

Few necromancers possessed ghost ships.

Therefore, ghost ships were even rarer.

Among those who did possess them, only a fraction conceived of enhancing their ghost ships into fully functional magic ships.

These individuals were scattered across the world, not gathered together.

Their resolve to pursue this goal was also unstable, as they each faced their own troubles - external threats or more compelling interests.

Such was the reality of ghost ships.

After receiving Jian Gui's response, Cang Xu gained deeper insight into the survival state of the necromancer community.

He now understood more fully why You Shen had issued his directive, urging his subordinates to actively spread necromancy inheritances.

The number of necromancers was truly too small.

Many endeavors simply could not proceed without sufficient personnel.

Take the monumental project of upgrading the ghost ship into a fully-fledged magic ship—it demanded vast manpower, immense time, and colossal resources to truly develop.

From this, Cang Xu also grasped Jian Gui's intent in rescuing Qi Ling.

“Qi Ling's necromancer aptitude isn't particularly strong; silver level is the absolute limit.”

“But for necromancers, even silver level talent is precious.”

“This might explain why Jian Gui chose to sponsor me when he first encountered me.”

Of course, Cang Xu would never forget how, during the battle in the Underworld River, Jian Gui had used him as bait to draw Wei Ying's fire, leading to his death!

“Jian Gui represents the orthodox path of the undead, a direct descendant of You Shen.”

“Therefore, he willingly sponsors undead seeds to expand and strengthen the undead faction.”

“Yet at the same time, when survival is at stake, he will exploit the necromancers around him to ensure his own survival.”

“That is Jian Gui.”

Cang Xu's understanding of Jian Gui was profound.

Thus, he silently warned himself: In this rescue of Qi Ling, he must assess the situation carefully. If things turn sour, retreat immediately.

After all, he wasn't part of the orthodox undead faction!

Moreover, if they encountered a formidable foe, he'd have to guard against Jian Gui.

Wasn't being used once enough already?

On the other hand, Cang Xu also coveted the advanced inheritance held by Jian Gui. He had to demonstrate his worth, striving to obtain what he desired from Jian Gui, things the survivors desperately needed.

"So, this is White Hair Trench? Wow, it's truly massive!" Xiao Wang Zi exclaimed through psychic communication, his admiration genuine.

Jian Gui replied, “This trench plunges 15,000 meters deep, stretches roughly 3,100 kilometers long, and varies in width—70 kilometers at its broadest, narrowing to about 30 kilometers at its narrowest points.”

He knew this trench quite well.

Jian Gui continued, “A unique species of coral polyps thrives within the White Hair Trench. They parasitize the surfaces of living organisms, multiplying and expanding their colonies.”

“These polyps absorb nutrients from their hosts while secreting waste products that accumulate, eventually forming white coral formations.”

"This coral differs from ordinary varieties in that it is nearly soft-bodied."

“So, when creatures in the trench swim, the white coral on their surfaces moves with them, appearing from afar like long white hair drifting through the water.”

“That's how White Hair Trench got its name.”

Cang Xu felt a surge of excitement. He was suddenly driven by an urge to collect, dissect, and study this peculiar coral polyp.

He knew his corals.

Coral was both a secretion of the polyp and its structural framework.

Coral polyps typically fed on planktonic microorganisms, but the white hair coral polyps parasitize the surfaces of their hosts, absorbing nutrients from the host's body.

“I really want to study them.” Though Cang Xu was a necromancer, he had always harbored a strong curiosity and desire to explore knowledge related to life forms.

This was evident from his actions on Mystifying Monster Island.

"Too bad this isn't the Deep Sea Monster Fish. Besides, I have important duties to fulfill. When will I ever have the leisure to freely study these mysteries of life?" Cang Xu sighed deeply.

“Marvelous! Absolutely marvelous!” At the same moment, the fishman Zhong Yong exclaimed excitedly, staring at the mysterious pearl before him.

He had been awakened by the dragonman youngster.

The research environment he possessed aboard the Deep Sea Monster Fish was precisely what Cang Xu yearned for.

What he was studying was a pearl taken from the mysterious cave wall!

“Seems you've made a discovery?” The dragonman youngster stood nearby.

“Indeed, I believe I understand where we are now, Commander,” Zhong Yong replied excitedly.

Chapter 602: Subduing a Saint Level?

After absorbing Hai Ping's saint corpse, the dragonman youngster pondered for a moment before imbuing several alchemy puppets with the Anti-investigation and Prophecy spell and Deceptive Disguise spell. He even transformed one into his own dragonman form.

Only then did he rouse Zhong Yong, briefly informing him of certain matters.

It was then that Zhong Yong realized he was aboard a mobile dock capable of sailing the seas!

Before the shock had fully subsided, the dragonman youngster informed him they had apparently been teleported by some mysterious force to a mysterious location.

Zhong Yong was stunned again.

Then, when he witnessed the wondrous sights within the mysterious cavern with his own eyes, his astonishment reached new heights.

In what felt like mere moments, the shock he experienced far surpassed the accumulated awe of previous years.

Then, the dragonman youngster took a risk, deploying an alchemy puppet to attempt harvesting pearls from the cave walls.

Empowered with the Anti-investigation and Prophecy spell and Deceptive Disguise spell, the puppet smoothly and safely plucked a small pearl without incident.

The spells were remarkably effective - the saint level colossal beast showed no reaction whatsoever.

Of course, it could also be because the target chosen this time was very small. The harvested pearl was only about the size of an adult's index finger, being at the iron level.

“If we were to target those saint level, legend level, or even the sole god level pearl, it might trigger widespread repercussions.”

For the time being, the dragonman youngster harbored reservations and would not take such risks.

It proved wise to awaken Zhong Yong.

Though this action revealed some secrets of the Deep Sea Monster Fish, he swiftly studied the pearl and conveyed his findings to the dragonman youngster.

“While not entirely certain, this mysterious location bears a striking resemblance to a certain historical account!”

Noticing the dragonman youngster's urging gaze, Zhong Yong continued, “This place is likely an Ocean Mother Nest.”

“What is an Ocean Mother Nest?” the dragonman youngster pressed immediately.

"It is an extraordinary location; a convergence point for the essence of all things. Across the vast oceans, such nests exist, almost always taking the form of sealed caverns."

“Within a vast radius around the Mother Nest, immense quantities of material essence gather, vast amounts of elements are absorbed, and boundless spirit power also coalesces here.”

“Therefore, high-quality alchemy materials often emerge within the Mother Nest, in enormous quantities and scale. It can even nurture life.”

“However, the life forms nurtured within the Mother Nest are typically elemental beings.”

"The colossal sea beast we observe now, however, is clearly flesh and blood. It must have emerged when the Mother Nest inadvertently absorbed the seeds of surrounding sea creatures, nurturing and growing them continuously."

The dragonman youngster pondered, “So that's how it is. No wonder this completely sealed place can sustain a saint level colossal beast growing naturally.”

“Though it lacks food, everything here is condensed, providing ample nutrients for its growth.”

“Precisely,” Zhong Yong nodded, “In fact, certain records mention wounded deities finding such Mother Nests to hide within, recuperating and healing their divine bodies.”

The dragonman youngster's eyes lit up, “Then how did the deities enter the Mother Nest?”

Zhong Yong shook his head, “The books offer no account. Even if they did, it's likely beyond our ability to replicate, Commander.”

“Then we'll have to breach the cavern wall and force our way through,” the dragonman youngster sighed.

His lament infected Zhong Yong: “Ah, truthfully, I wouldn't advise breaking it open just yet, Commander.”

"Such a Mother Nest is exceedingly rare, built upon an incredibly delicate equilibrium. The slightest disruption could trigger a chain reaction, causing the entire cavern to collapse."

"The most advantageous approach is to stay here as long as possible, gathering resources without disrupting the balance."

"The Mother Nest concentrates the essence of all things within the surrounding heavens and earth. Its resources are virtually inexhaustible, flowing endlessly."

The dragonman youngster's heart stirred, “You mean even after we harvest these materials, they'll keep growing back?”

“If my assessment is correct, and this truly is the Ocean Mother Nest, then yes,” Zhong Yong replied.

The dragonman youngster's breathing grew heavier, but soon regret clouded his expression, “What a pity. We cannot linger here long.”

“Too many urgent matters demand my attention.”

“Does the book mention any method to transport us out?”

“Were we teleported in because the Mother Nest is gathering everything around it?”

Zhong Yong shook his head, “I have no idea why we were transported here.”

“But I can say for certain: the Mother Nest doesn't gather things through teleportation. It shatters and absorbs them down to their finest components.”

“Much like coral polyps feeding on plankton.”

"Therefore, unique resources likely form within the Ocean Mother Nest. Take these pearls before us—they must be a unique resource here, with no equivalent outside. Because this cavern concentrates the essence of all things, it unexpectedly gave birth to them."

“As for this colossal beast, it likely began as something akin to fish roe, possessing exceptional bloodlines. That's why it was treated as essence and absorbed into the Mother Nest.”

“Our teleportation here... in my view, it's utterly bizarre, no, a miracle!”

The dragonman youngster nodded. "Precisely. If the cavern directly forcibly teleports external entities by gathering all things, this saint beast must have endured countless battles. Remember, the ocean depths teem with innumerable sea beasts."

“As for being transported in... could it be that the divine artifact protected us?” The last thought remained unspoken, murmured only within the dragonman youngster's mind.

After a moment of contemplation, the dragonman youngster sighed again, "What a pity. With ample time, we could have gained immensely here. But now, we must find a way to leave this place swiftly."

“First, we need to find a way to deal with this saint level sea beast.”

“Zhong Yong, can you discern this sea beast's origins?”

The fishman Zhong Yong shook his head repeatedly, “Commander, I'm afraid I cannot.”

"It's curled into a ball now—its true form is impossible to identify. There are simply too many lifeforms resembling its appearance."

"Truthfully, just like these unique pearls, perhaps it too is one of a kind. "

"Everything I've suggested about it so far is mere conjecture. Perhaps the Mother Nest, gathering the essence of all things, unexpectedly gave birth to some unique life form. That too is possible!"

The dragonman youngster clenched his teeth slightly.

If it was a peculiar life form created by the Mother Nest, that would be troublesome.

He had to uncover the exact nature of this colossal sea beast's abilities; otherwise, escaping this place would be difficult.

“If only there were no saint level sea beast, just vast treasures!” The dragonman youngster sighed deeply, his voice tinged with resignation.

Zhong Yong's expression flickered with surprise before clearing. He replied, “Commander, you may be overlooking something.”

“We don't necessarily have to fight this colossal.”

"Consider this: the Mother Nest is sealed off, isolated from the outside world. This beast has lived here its entire life, from infancy to adulthood. It's utterly ignorant and naive."

“Without enemies to teach it combat, it retains an infant-like curiosity toward everything.”

“We can easily tame it!”

“Is there a more suitable target for taming than this creature?”

Zhong Yong's words seemed to open a new window for the dragonman youngster to view the world.

"You mean... we can tame it? Tame a saint level combat force?" The dragonman youngster's eyes widened.

Zhong Yong's eyes widened too—fishman eyes were far larger than the youngster's—his gaze now intense, “Indeed, the possibility of taming it exists.”

“There's one more thing I must tell you, Commander.”

“The area surrounding the Ocean Mother Nest cannot be barren.”

“Only a place with a favorable environment and abundant resources could give birth to an Ocean Mother Nest.”

The dragonman youngster's expression instantly grew grim, “So... if we breach the Mother Nest, powerful beasts might be lurking outside?”

Zhong Yong nodded.

This made subduing the saint colossal beast crucial.

But how?

The ghost ship glided through the depths.

Pitch darkness surrounded them, so thick one couldn't see their hand in front of their face.

Only by looking up could one glimpse a vast, azure band of light stretching across the sky.

The ghost ship had penetrated deep into the White Hair Trench, flanked by towering walls of rock, while above lay the translucent sea surface.

The mist surrounding the ghost ship had completely vanished, and even the vessel's own undead aura seemed to have retreated into obscurity.

Negative energy was repelled and abhorred by life.

The deeper into the trench they ventured, the more positive energy they encountered, and marine life thrived in abundance. If the ghost ship pressed forward recklessly, it risked being surrounded and attacked by a horde of sea beasts.

“A pack of sharp-clawed water monkeys has spotted us.”

Barely three seconds after Jian Gui spoke, he called out again: “Prepare for battle! The water monkeys are closing in!”

The sharp-clawed water monkeys were roughly the size of an adult human, covered entirely in silver-gray fur.

They possessed sharp claws and teeth; when attacking, their talons emitted a faint blue glow in the pitch-black depths.

Their faces resembled monkeys, yet their noses were bird-like—hard and pointed.

This troop dwelled on the trench walls, carving caves into their habitat.

The ghost ship's approach triggered their coordinated assault.

Cang Xu and his companions, alerted by Jian Gui, prepared for battle.

As the water monkeys entered striking range, Xiao Wang Zi led the charge, casting necromancy spells to unleash volleys of bone spears.

Qing Xin joined the fray, summoning wind blades.

The bone spears inflicted significant damage, while the wind blades proved largely ineffective in combat.

As a living being, Qing Xin had to constantly maintain her spells to survive in the deep sea, leaving her no spare energy for combat.

In this regard, the undead held a distinct advantage. At the very least, they didn't need to breathe—only withstand the water pressure.

The water monkeys swarmed in dense, countless numbers.

Though some were felled by bone spears and wind blades, the majority still surged forward.

Chapter 603: Ghost Seed Head, Head-Binding Spell

Cang Xu was also casting spells.

Under his command, a squadron of sailfish charged into battle from behind the ghost ship.

The sailfish launched their assault, skewering water monkeys one after another.

When colliding with stronger water monkeys, many sailfish crashed like ships against reefs, dying instantly.

The scene was brutal. The sailfish fought recklessly, stemming the tide of the water monkey horde.

When the enraged leader of the water monkey pack stormed onto the deck, the undead ronin Ku Feng immediately drew his blade to fight.

Ku Feng quickly found himself at a disadvantage, forced into retreat.

The water monkey pack leaders were all gold level, while Ku Feng's combat effectiveness was already diminished by fighting in the deep sea. Being only silver level and lacking the extensive gear of the dragonman youngster, merely holding off the leader was already commendable.

At the critical moment, Jian Gui intervened.

Gold level undead magic, amplified by the ghost ship itself, unleashed devastating power.

Soon, the water monkeys storming the deck were slaughtered to the last, including their leader.

After the battle, the group swiftly cleaned the battlefield, assessed losses, and divided the spoils.

Ship Ghost, Cang Xu, and Xiao Wang Zi all cast the Soul Summoning Spell, coalescing water monkey spirits from the corpses.

“These spirits are perfect for transforming into water ghosts,” Ship Ghost remarked with satisfaction as he took two gold level water monkey spirits.

Cang Xu and Xiao Wang Zi took only a small portion.

The finest spirits went to Jian Gui, while the vast majority were assigned to the ghost ship.

The more spirits parasitizing the ghost ship, the stronger the ship itself became. Simultaneously, the ghost ship provided protection and enhancements to the spirits aboard.

It was a mutually beneficial relationship.

Next, Xiao Wang Zi cast another necromancy magic—Summon Skeletons.

The corpses of the sharp-clawed water monkeys convulsed violently. Their bones split through the flesh, burrowing out.

“Water monkey skeletons... Hmm, passable,” Xiao Wang Zi appraised.

Finally, the remaining flesh and blood were divided.

The distribution followed strict rules based on combat contributions.

Qing Xin had also participated, so she received some water monkey flesh as compensation.

Cang Xu's primary objective was the water monkey heads.

The heads had to be intact—soul, bones, flesh, everything—preferably without a single part missing. Only then could the highest probability of creating death heads be achieved.

In the end, Cang Xu obtained five silver level water monkey skulls, several iron level ones, and a large quantity of bronze level skulls.

“The success rate of the Head Creation Spell is low, but this haul should guarantee me at least one water monkey death head, right?” After a period of cultivation, Cang Xu had roughly assessed his own success probability for performing the Head Creation Spell.

This time, to gather a large quantity of water monkey skulls, he spared no expense in commanding his sailfish fleet.

Consequently, after this battle, the sailfish fleet was nearly annihilated, suffering devastating casualties.

Thus, the sailfish carcasses met the same fate as the water monkeys, swiftly divided among the necromancers.

The specific distribution rules had been thoroughly discussed with others long before this battle.

Sacrificing the sailfish school was part of the plan.

Cang Xu had discovered that the sailfish death heads they produced were already severely damaged and difficult to preserve.

This damage occurred during an undersea hunt when Cang Xu's command was inadvertently mishandled, causing the death heads to sustain injuries.

“Though the techniques for preserving the death head were passed down to me by Doctor Sui Shi, I struggle to master them.” Cang Xu's own alchemy expertise limited his ability to fully harness the true power of the Flying Head Spell's legacy.

He had discerned the essence of this inheritance: alchemy was its core, while necromancy served merely as its foundation. In many aspects, profound alchemy mastery was required to achieve optimal results.

Returning to his assigned cabin, Cang Xu summoned the death head of the sailfish leader before him.

The next instant, he flicked his finger.

The death head exploded with a loud bang.

The explosion's force was minimal, shattering the decayed flesh and bones before leaving behind a ghostly essence.

This essence was compact, roughly spherical in shape. Within the gold gray mist enveloping the sphere, the form of a sailfish head occasionally materialized.

A wisp of ghostly fog rose from the top of the sphere.

This ghostly mist wasn't loose like long hair, but rather denser, resembling young rice shoots.

According to the Flying Head Spell tradition, after a modified death head shattered, what remained was the Ghost Seed Head.

Cang Xu opened his mouth and sucked the Ghost Seed Head into his mouth.

Necromancy - Head-Binding Spell.

He proceeded with the incantation. The sailfish Ghost Seed Head temporarily fused with his own, transforming into a purplish-blue sailfish mark. Like a tattoo, it appeared on Cang Xu's right cheek.

This represented another application of the death head.

When a death head was damaged beyond repair or intentionally shattered, it would leave behind a Ghost Seed Head.

Upon binding the Ghost Seed Head, the necromancer gained a certain probability of mastering all its abilities.

However, Cang Xu gained zero abilities from the sailfish Ghost Seed Head.

This was because he failed to invest resources into its modification. Consequently, the gold sailfish death head possessed no necromancy magic whatsoever, merely retaining the ability to command sailfish schools.

Now that the sailfish's form was gone, Cang Xu could no longer command them.

Typically, necromancers cultivated exceptional death heads, imbuing them with necromancy magic.

These spells were engraved within the Ghost Seed Head's body.

Upon binding, the necromancer effectively gained a built-in spellbook - boosting casting success rates, accelerating incantations, and reducing costs.

Cang Xu wasn't without this ambition; he had made attempts, all ending in failure.

As always, the true barrier to this inheritance lies in alchemy mastery.

For Cang Xu, merely crafting the death head of a gold sailfish was already a minor miracle.

Not only was he constrained by this limitation, but he also lacked sufficient undead resources, making further cultivation of death heads genuinely impossible.

“Even so, this gold level Ghost Seed Head remains my most valuable asset today.” Cang Xu touched his right cheek, feeling nothing unusual.

It was worth noting that the Ghost Seed Head offered another advantage.

Should Cang Xu ever desire a gold level sailfish death head in the future, he could release this Ghost Seed Head to seek out a gold level sailfish head and refine it.

The success rate of such refinement far surpassed starting from scratch!

“This too is a process of accumulation.”

“Hopefully, I can refine several silver level water monkey death heads next.”

"Since the highest material level is only silver level, one is too few, two barely sufficient, and three are just enough to handle minor situations."

Cang Xu took a moment to regroup before beginning work on the water monkey skull.

His combat strength was weak. This deep dive into the White Hair Trench had instilled a sense of urgency within him—he absolutely had to focus all his efforts on boosting his own fighting power.

Chapter 604: Vigorous Preparation for Battle

Head Creation Spell.

Head Creation Spell.

Head Creation Spell…

Cang Xu entered the ship's hold and remained there for the next two days, never stepping outside as he processed the undead materials in his hands.

Frequent invocations of the Head Creation Spell produced one death head after another in his grasp.

These skulls were all monkey heads, sourced from the battle before. In that engagement, the necromancers had joined forces to wipe out a pack of sharp-clawed water monkeys. Everyone had reaped substantial rewards.

To secure sufficient necromancy materials, Cang Xu had exhausted his entire stock of sailfish skulls.

It was a gamble he had no choice but to take.

The reason was that his gold level sailfish death head was already beyond use, on the verge of destruction.

“Sigh, I lost the gamble...”

Cang Xu's face was pale, his spirit exhausted. Staring at the newly crafted death heads, his heart was filled with bitterness.

He had obtained a total of five silver level water monkey skulls, over thirty iron level ones, and more than sixty bronze level ones.

The death head now produced contained only one silver level water monkey skull, seventeen iron level ones, and fifty six bronze level ones.

While bronze level and iron level were plentiful, silver level was scarce—only one.

Two were barely sufficient, but one was truly too few!

This was the world of the strong.

Bronze level and iron level were expendable; only gold level served as the true pillars.

Unfortunately, Cang Xu no longer possessed any gold level death heads. Only one gold level Ghost Seed Head remained, devoid of any recorded undead spells.

“Sacrificing a gold level skull and an entire sailfish school for this meager return—my combat strength has diminished rather than increased.”

As Cang Xu sighed, he examined the quantities of skulls across tiers and discerned certain patterns.

“The success rate of skull crafting decreases with each level.”

“Bronze level water monkey death head has over 80% success rate. Iron level approaches 50%. By silver level, the success rate dropped to just 20%.”

The success rate of the Head Creation Spell was intrinsically linked to one's alchemy prowess.

Cang Xu suffered from this limitation, squandering vast quantities of undead materials for meager returns.

“Alchemy... alchemy...”

Cang Xu muttered incessantly to himself.

He felt utterly helpless.

His innate talent was lacking, his alchemy skill rudimentary—he was practically a novice.

Fortunately, he wasn't alone; companions stood by his side.

“Xu Jia, you finished too?” Xiao Wang Zi greeted Cang Xu as he stepped onto the deck.

A large group of water monkey skeletons followed Xiao Wang Zi.

Ku Feng practiced elsewhere, relentlessly swinging his blade.

Qing Xin approached and informed Cang Xu he was the last to finish post-battle recuperation.

Among this group, Qing Xin and Cang Xu shared the closest bond.

Cang Xu hadn't spotted Jian Gui, but moments later, his voice echoed: “Since Xu Jia is ready, we shall proceed.”

Though the ghost ship hadn't launched any attacks during this period, Jian Gui had continuously scouted the surroundings, identifying numerous viable targets.

Moments later, the ghost ship dove deep, intruding into the territory of a beast pack and once again facing an assault from a water monkey horde.

Jian Gui's decision to target the water monkeys was a prudent choice.

Such foes were no strangers to the necromancers.

Skeletons clustered around the ship's sides, unleashing continuous volleys upon the water monkeys.

The undead ronin charged from the deck into the depths, single-handedly breaking through the ranks and swiftly slaying numerous sharp-clawed water monkeys.

Over time, he had grown accustomed to the underwater environment, developing considerable proficiency in aquatic combat.

It must be said that undead, needing no air to breathe, faced no significant barriers in developing aquatic combat skills.

Cang Xu commanded the death heads to divide the water monkey horde.

The leader of this group was only silver level. Cang Xu deployed a silver level death head to successfully disrupt the leader's commands, leaving a portion of the water monkeys disoriented and confused.

Iron level and bronze level death heads drifted freely, colliding with or gnawing at the flesh of healthy water monkeys.

During their charge, many water monkeys were already felled by the water monkey skeleton archers.

Ku Feng grew bolder with each clash, charging directly at the water monkey leader. This left the latter overwhelmed and unable to command the horde.

Cang Xu seized the moment, deploying a silver level death head to intimidate the horde, forcibly recruiting a contingent of living water monkeys to his cause.

Finally, Jian Gui intervened once more through the ghost ship, unleashing a gold level spell that instantly killed 70% of the water monkeys.

Shattered by this devastating blow, the horde immediately disintegrated.

The water monkey leader, sensing the enemy's gold level aura, shrieked in terror and fled first.

Ku Feng couldn't pursue it. Though he possessed some naval combat skills, his speed couldn't match that of the water monkey leader desperately fleeing for its life.

“Watch this!” Xiao Wang Zi shouted, snapping off his index finger bone and hurling it forward.

Mid-air, the bone instantly reassembled into a bone spear.

The spear flew with blinding speed, piercing the water monkey leader with deadly precision and ending its life.

The necromancers began clearing the battlefield.

This water monkey pack proved far weaker than any they'd encountered before. Only one silver level leader existed—no gold level.

Victory came easily to the necromancers, though their spoils were significantly reduced.

Jian Gui cast a Soul Summoning Spell, replenishing the ranks of water monkey spirits.

Ku Feng remained unsatisfied.

Xiao Wang Zi reaped the greatest rewards, having slain the water monkey leader, whose head became his spoils.

Of course, Ku Feng also claimed a small portion.

Cang Xu approached Xiao Wang Zi, proposing to purchase the water monkey leader's head.

“Sure,” Xiao Wang Zi countered, “I'm quite interested in your Flying Head Spell.”

Cang Xu shook his head with a bitter smile. "The Flying Head Spell isn't available for trade anytime soon."

Beyond that, however, he possessed little else that might catch Xiao Wang Zi's eye.

Nearly all his wealth lay within his storage pouch, lost along with his body.

His current body was provided free of charge by other necromancers.

Even his basic materials were acquired on credit.

Ship Ghost and others, including Qing Xin, were his creditors.

Nearly everything he'd accumulated along the way had been squandered—a few became death heads, most simply consumed.

Cang Xu had demonstrated some of Flying Head Spell's power, tempting Xiao Wang Zi.

But since Cang Xu refused to trade it, Xiao Wang Zi could only decline.

When Cang Xu attempted negotiation, he met with Xiao Wang Zi's contempt: “You still owe me money. Pay up first, then we'll talk.”

Even among comrades, aid had its limits.

Past support for Cang Xu had been charity; now that he could stand on his own, Xiao Wang Zi and his crew preferred Cang Xu to earn resources and settle his debts first.

After a brief rest and clearing the battlefield, the ghost ship pressed onward.

Soon, they clashed again with a horde of sea beasts.

Over the course of the day, they engaged in no fewer than ten battles, large and small. Though the necromancers suffered losses, their gains were greater, and their combat strength grew significantly.

Sustaining themselves through battle, their fighting prowess increased. The necromancer faction's advantage was now undeniably evident.

The necromancers had yet to discover Qi Ling or even traces of her anywhere. They continued reconnaissance while actively preparing for battle, ready to face any unexpected or dire situation.

Chapter 605: Investigating Lan Zao

The Divine Wind.

Several people gathered inside the cabin.

Lan Zao lay on the bed, still unconscious.

By the bedside stood Bing Xiao, the human ringleader, and a priest.

The priest carefully examined Lan Zao's entire body.

Most pirate crews lacked priests, but the Divine Wind Pirates were an exception. Their ships carried a small contingent of priests of the Sea Wind God.

After completing the examination, the priest wiped sweat from his brow, “For now, his condition is remarkably stable!”

The human ringleader looked astonished. "How is that possible? Priest, does he look well to you?"

At that moment, Lan Zao's entire body was swollen, and his normally healthy skin tone had changed, tinged with a hint of blue, as if he were poisoned.

The priest of the Sea Wind God shook his head and said, “I know what you're thinking. These symptoms he's showing now are precisely signs that his body is improving.”

His original injuries were minor and have already healed through previous treatments."

“His current unconscious state and these symptoms are solely due to his bloodline awakening!”

“Bloodline... awakening?”

Bing Xiao and the human ringleader exchanged a swift glance, both seeing astonishment on each other's faces.

The Sea Wind God's priest looked at them: “Surely you know what bloodline awakening is?”

Bing Xiao and the human ringleader shook their heads simultaneously. The latter replied, “Of course we know!”

“We just never imagined the legendary phenomenon of bloodline awakening would occur in one of our own faction members.”

Bloodline awakening referred to a qualitative transformation in the concentration of a specific bloodline within a life form.

This process was often accompanied by visible physical changes. The root cause lay in the bloodline's intense restructuring of the organism. For instance, beastman bloodlines manifested tails, while demon bloodlines sprouted horns.

Among the offspring of great nobles, those with exceptional bloodlines might exhibit signs of awakening during their growth.

Marquis Jin Mang Ke of the Sheng Ming Empire, whose cultivation of the battle qi technique perfectly aligned with his bloodline, exemplified this. When unleashing his full power, battle qi surged through his body, enveloping him in a swarm of golden lightning sparks. Simultaneously, his original black hair transformed entirely to gold, the strands swelling and coalescing into dense, block-like masses resembling engorged muscles.

These were manifestations of bloodline awakening.

Some manifestations were passive, others active.

Including the dragonman youngster—whenever he activated his Bloodcore, forming the ring mounds of a smoke-spewing dragon upon his body, people interpreted it as a physical mutation signifying bloodline awakening.

The Sea Wind Priest shifted his gaze to Lan Zao and remarked speculatively, " In my view, he likely experienced a stimulus during the escape battle at the dock earlier. Under the pressure of death, his bloodline was triggered, leading to this awakening."

"Of course, that was merely an external catalyst. The true accumulation came from his constant growth in daily life, where the concentration of his bloodline within him steadily increased."

“Finally, at this moment, the quantitative change brought about a qualitative leap, reaching the minimum threshold for bloodline awakening.”

Bing Xiao couldn't help but ask curiously, “Then, what exactly is his bloodline?”

The priest of the Sea Wind God chuckled and shrugged, “How should I know?”

"I'm just a priest of the Sea Wind God, not someone from the Life Church. I don't possess the relevant divine spell to detect the truth of a bloodline."

“However, you can make some educated guesses based on his physical traits.”

"Of course, he's still in the midst of awakening. His bloodline characteristics haven't fully solidified yet and aren't particularly pronounced. From what we can observe now, he seems to be awakening some sort of water-based bloodline."

The human ringleader nodded slightly, silently agreeing.

In his eyes, Lan Zao appeared as a human. As a member of the gang who has lived in Snow Bird Harbor for years, where water elements abound, awakening water-based bloodlines had a solid foundation in reality.

Bing Xiao furrowed his brow slightly: “Priest, in a case like his, does he need to take bloodline stabilizers?”

The priest shook his head without hesitation: "Given his current state, he's actually doing quite well and doesn't require bloodline stabilizers. Such potions are used to manage conflicts between bloodlines. But you see, he's only in the process of awakening—it's not complete yet. Perhaps in the future, the awakened bloodline within him might clash with the residual impure bloodline. In that case, bloodline stabilizers would be necessary."

Bing Xiao pressed further: “Does the Divine Wind Pirates' stockpile contain anything like bloodline stabilizers?”

The priest shook his head. "Certainly not. Bloodline awakenings are exceedingly rare. We don't stock such specialized supplies."

"You're heading to Flower Skirt Island next. Perhaps you could procure the necessary potions there?"

Flower Skirt Island was small, and the priest couldn't say for certain.

Bing Xiao and the human ringleader saw the priest off before returning to the cabin.

Gazing at the unconscious Lan Zao, both fell silent for a moment.

The human ringleader spoke up, "Bloodline stabilizers don't come cheap. Lord Bing Xiao, it seems you hold high hopes for this lad. Do you intend to nurture him?"

Bing Xiao made no attempt to conceal his thoughts, nodding frankly, “Indeed, that is precisely my intention.”

The old gang leader paused before elaborating to the human ringleader, “This boy performed admirably during the battle at the previous dock. He saved my life and deserves commendation.”

“I'm quite impressed with this youngster.”

“He's full of drive, isn't he?”

"He showed no fear toward the Dragon Lion Mercenary Corps or the Snow Bird Harbor City Lord. His heart burns with vengeance. Even during our escape, he wanted to bombard the dock. Though it was reckless and naive, such courage is rare and valuable."

“After all, the gap between us and our enemies is starkly evident.”

Bing Xiao had deeply misunderstood the situation.

In truth, Lan Zao had sought to incite conflict between the two sides, hoping to create chaos. This would have given him more opportunities to slip back into the Dragon Lion Mercenary Corps.

Ultimately, his suggestion was rejected.

But Bing Xiao had witnessed it all.

Bing Xiao sighed deeply: “The Bayonet Gang suffered too severe a blow. The surviving members are of poor quality, their fighting spirit completely gone.”

“The true grassroots backbone was wiped out by the Snow Bird Harbor City Lord. His preparations were simply too thorough.”

“Our current group is at best marginal - the Snow Bird Harbor City Lord doesn't even consider us worth his attention.”

"Therefore, we must recruit new members, select promising individuals from this group, cultivate them, and forge them into new pillars. At the very least, their loyalty is plain for all to see."

The human ringleader voiced his concern: “Could it be that Mu Si is truly the only mole?”

Bing Xiao chuckled, a cold gleam flashing in his eyes: “There might be others, or there might not. Either way, it won't hinder our rebuilding of the Bayonet Gang.”

“In my view, this lad's suspicion remains minimal.”

“He's been unconscious the entire time, and his gear is remarkably ‘clean’.”

“We cannot allow the fear of potential traitors to paralyze us or slow our progress.”

The human ringleader nodded, “The gang leader speaks wisely.”

“I also doubt this lad is the mole.”

"If I'd planted a spy, I wouldn't have let him openly kill our own men. Instead, he'd save a group on the battlefield to cover his tracks."

Lan Zao had been harassed aboard the Divine Wind after killing several Bayonet Gang members during the dock battle.

Bing Xiao frowned slightly and snorted coldly: “Those guys picking on the kid? Besides losing some close associates, it's mostly about power struggles.”

“What's the name of that iron level troublemaker?”

“Zhao San,” the human ringleader replied.

"Hmph. Zhao San and his crew lack the guts to fight enemies, but they're experts at internal strife. They barely escaped death and already scheme to seize power within the gang, trying to bring this kid down." Bing Xiao understood their nature all too well.

With his extensive experience leading gangs, he'd seen plenty of such characters.

Bing Xiao turned to Lan Zao, his tone softening slightly. "Even without his bloodline awakening, I would have brought him close as my personal attendant. Now that his bloodline has awakened, all the better."

"His achievement shouldn't be limited to the bronze level. If he advances to iron level, he can become a basic group leader. Let that iron level Zhao San who's been giving him trouble be his opponent."

If he can still reach silver level, he'll be one of the Bayonet Gang's ringleaders.

“What do you think of this arrangement?”

Bing Xiao sought the human ringleader's opinion, instantly causing the latter's expression to shift from panic to excitement. He hastily declared the boss's arrangement perfectly reasonable and pledged full cooperation.

This attitude immediately satisfied Bing Xiao, who nodded approvingly.

Only three leaders remained in the Bayonet Gang.

Xiong Ju was a brute, while Spring Boxer was still too young. The human ringleader's combat prowess fell far short of Xiong Ju's, and his potential couldn't match Spring Boxer's. Yet at this stage, he was Bing Xiao's most capable assistant.

It was because he possessed basic political acumen, foundational management skills, and could work in tandem with Bing Xiao.

This was precisely why Bing Xiao patiently explained his views and plans regarding Lan Zao to the human ringleader. On this matter, Bing Xiao and the human ringleader reached a consensus. Lan Zao would become Bing Xiao's future confidant.

“Next, investigate this kid's background,” Bing Xiao ordered, “Come to think of it, I haven't even figured out what this little guy's name is yet.”

The human ringleader nodded promptly: “Yes.”

“There are quite a few gang members aboard the ship. We should be able to learn something from them.”

Snow Bird Harbor.

A certain dueling arena.

Zong Ge and Yue Qian's weapons clashed fiercely, unable to withstand the strain, snapping clean in two.

“Hahahaha! Long Fu, I never expected you to wield the twin-handed war hammer with such skill!” Yue Qian paused the fight; his admiring gaze fixed on Zong Ge.

Not a single spectator watched the arena; this was their third private duel.

Yue Qian studied Zong Ge, “If I'm not mistaken, you served in the military, didn't you?”

Zong Ge nodded. "Let bygones be bygones. I'd rather not speak of it. How about we test our two-handed greatswords next?"

Yue Qian shook his head. "Your greatsword proficiency is clearly formidable. But I yearn for a true battle with you."

"Long Fu, draw your short spears. Unleash your full combat prowess. Use your most potent battle skills."

“Today, we shall settle this once and for all!”

Chapter 606: Ramhorn Turtles

White Hair Trench.

“I'm in position,” Xiao Wang Zi relayed through magic.

“Then begin,” Jian Gui commanded, striking first.

A powerful surge surged forward, engulfing a group of turtles.

Struck by the necromancy magic, some young turtles died instantly. The silver level turtles staggered, while the gold level leader, enraged, charged toward the ghost ship.

Under Jian Gui's command, the vessel slowly retreated, attacking while drawing the turtle herd toward it.

The leader relentlessly pursued the enemy, paying no heed to what lay behind. The gap between the sea turtles widened, stretching their formation into a chaotic, single-file line.

The ghost ship lured the turtle pack into narrow terrain. As the turtle leader advanced, ambushes sprang up on both sides.

Hordes of sharp-clawed water monkey skeletons pounced upon the turtles, while numerous skeleton archers rained arrows from elevated positions.

Skeletons raised from sharp-clawed water monkey corpses rarely included skeleton magicians. Most were unarmed melee fighters, relying solely on their claws as weapons. A few were skeleton archers.

The sharp-clawed water monkey skeletons swarmed over the sea turtles like a vast ant colony. Yet their claws, capable of easily piercing reefs, made little impact against the turtle's shell.

These turtles possessed a distinctly aggressive appearance. Their shells were covered in black, hard spines, and a triangular horn protruded from the front of their heads.

These were ramhorn turtles.

In the early days, before magic ships existed, people hunted ramhorn turtles for the horns on their heads, using them as ship rams.

During the age of magic ships, these horns became the primary material for crafting alchemy components of the Ramhorn type.

The turtle shells of ramhorn turtles remained a material for shields. With minimal processing, they could be transformed into massive defensive shields covered in spikes.

During the great war against the demon invasion of the main world, the legendary Minotaur, Great Herculean, single-handedly poisoned the largest group of ramhorn turtles dwelling in the depths of the ocean at that time.

The acquired turtle shells were immediately dispatched to the desperately embattled city of Fan City.

The giants within Fan City wielded the shells directly as shields, ultimately repelling the demon hordes' frenzied assault and safeguarding the city. This victory laid the groundwork for the subsequent major counteroffensive on the main world.

The sea turtles were in a frenzy.

They shook their bodies relentlessly yet couldn't dislodge the water monkey skeletons clinging to them.

They then swung their fin-like limbs with all their might, thrusting their heads forward to slam violently against the surrounding cliffs.

Deep craters pockmarked the undersea cliffs. The intense pressure and friction between the shells and rock crushed many of the sharp-clawed water monkey skeletons into dust.

The bone arrows fired by skeleton archers struck the turtles like mosquito bites—barely causing a scratch.

Xiao Wang Zi remained utterly composed, having anticipated this outcome.

His primary role in this engagement was to distract and provide cover. The water monkey skeletons sacrificed were either substandard units slated for disposal or common fodder whose loss carried no weight. His true elite forces—the iron level and silver level skeletons—remained deployed elsewhere.

Necromancy magic - Soul Capture!

Jian Gui channeled his full power, the spell's potency amplified severalfold by the ghost ship itself.

The gold level ramhorn turtle leader, struck by this spell, froze instantly, rendered immobile as if turned to stone.

Instead of advancing, the ghost ship retreated, sailing right up to the ramhorn turtle leader, who remained frozen in place.

As the two passed each other, a turtle-shaped soul was extracted by the ghost ship, becoming one of its many specters.

Then, an invisible force seized the ramhorn turtle leader's corpse.

The gold level turtle corpse was tossed into a gaping hole in the ghost ship's hull, vanishing into the darkness.

The ghost ship charged back across the battlefield, its swarm of wailing spirits darting swiftly through the fray.

Ramhorn turtles possessed formidable physical defenses, their unique shell design granting a built-in damage reflection effect. However, their resistance to magic attacks was notably weak.

Ghosts could freely pass through solid objects like rocks, rendering the ramhorn turtles' prized spiked armor useless.

Only by casting racial magic could they offer any resistance against the ghosts.

Yet the ghosts were numerous, forming the initial makings of an army.

Jian Gui didn't even need to cast spells; the sheer impact of the ghosts alone threw the turtle horde into chaos.

Soon the turtle herd collapsed and turned to flee.

But this battlefield had been meticulously chosen by the necromancers - narrow terrain flanked by large numbers of water monkey skeletons.

As the turtles fled, they lost their lives one by one.

The underwater trench fell into dead silence.

The necromancers began clearing the battlefield.

“This haul is truly substantial!” Xiao Wang Zi exclaimed joyfully.

His contributions were immense, having sacrificed countless skeletons, and the spoils of war would be rich indeed.

Necromancy spell - Summon Skeletons.

Necromancy spell - Summon Skeletons.

Necromancy spell - Summon Skeletons...

Xiao Wang Zi cast the spell repeatedly, and one by one, massive turtle skeletons emerged from the corpses.

Their white shells were covered in sharp bone spines, and the striking horns atop their turtle heads remained intact, commanding attention.

"Now I have a heavy cavalry unit. This is fantastic! Generalissimo, you truly live up to your name." Xiao Wang Zi called out to his mount.

His mount was a three-headed sea serpent, also an undead creature, named Generalissimo by Xiao Wang Zi.

Jian Gui revealed his true form.

He began casting the Soul Summoning Spell.

The sea turtles had just fallen in battle, and their souls still lingering on the battlefield.

Soon, ghostly sea turtles began appearing across the field.

“These turtle spirits are prime material for crafting Shield Bearer Ghosts,” Xiao Wang Zi beamed.

The soul aspect faction within the undead realm excelled at modifying spirits, and Shield Bearer Ghosts were one such application. The spirits thus created could grant necromancers formidable defense.

Jian Gui set aside some turtle spirits for Cang Xu and the undead ronin, Ku Feng.

In this battle, Cang Xu's death heads served only as scouts. The undead ronin Ku Feng remained stationed on the ghost ship's deck without engaging, contributing the least.

Within mere days, the necromancers' combat power had multiplied several times over.

They eradicated over a dozen sea beast swarms, including sharp-clawed water monkeys, Ramhorn Turtles, and schools of oceanic fish.

As their strength grew, the necromancers' actions became bolder and their coordination sharper.

Like in this recent engagement: no longer confined to the ghost ship, they preemptively divided forces, selected battlefields, and set up ambushes.

They employed tactics to lure the turtle swarm into the ambush site before striking with full force.

Clearly, sound strategy greatly enhanced their efficiency in hunting down turtle swarms.

Cang Xu stowed the newly acquired ghosts without immediate processing.

He returned to his cabin, continuously casting spells.

Necromancy spell - Burden Alleviation.

Necromancy spell - Karma Reduction Spell.

The former eased Cang Xu's mental strain, while the latter mitigated the erosion of his secondary abilities.

Currently, his collection of death heads was substantial.

He possessed 4 silver level, 27 iron level, and a staggering 86 bronze level specimens.

Commanding this multitude of death heads had already imposed a tremendous mental burden on Cang Xu.

“My innate talent is limited. Even transformed into an undead life form, I can only reach iron level.”

“It's only because the Flying Head Spell inheritance imposes less burden than ordinary ghosts, skeletons, or zombies that I can control so many.”

Cang Xu appeared to be silver level, but in truth, he was merely iron.

Even using the Burden Alleviation Spell and Karma Reduction Spell daily, he was nearing his control limit.

Only after halting these two spells did Cang Xu begin processing the newly acquired spirits.

“This battle yielded only these.”

“Far too few.”

“This situation is unfavorable for me.”

Cang Xu's low contribution naturally meant meager spoils.

It wasn't that he lacked willingness, but his inherent weakness placed him low in the tactical hierarchy.

In every battle, Jian Gui claimed the largest share of spoils. The ghost fleet aboard the ghost ship had swelled nearly tenfold!

Xiao Wang Zi's haul usually ranked second, his skeletal army now forming a formidable force.

Cang Xu came third, his death heads increasing in number. But constrained by his innate talent, he was nearing his personal limit.

Among the necromancers, Ku Feng ranked lowest. Just like this time, where his involvement was scarcely needed anymore.

As for Qing Xin, she needn't be mentioned.

In the deep sea, she had to constantly maintain her spells to sustain her own respiration.

She scarcely left her quarters.

Each day consumed vast amounts of time in meditation, replenishing her magic power.

Yet in the ocean depths, water elements abounded while wind elements were scarce, making her meditation highly inefficient.

She had been completely marginalized....

…

The Divine Wind.

The human ringleader's brow furrowed deeply.

After thorough inquiries, he discovered no one recognized Lan Zao.

“How is that possible?”

“Could he not be part of the Bayonet Gang?”

The human ringleader fell into deep thought: “The people on the ship are merely remnants of the gang. Perhaps none of them ever interacted with him.”

“But that possibility is too low.”

“Could he be a spy, deliberately rescuing Bing Xiao to gain trust?”

“Everything had been going smoothly until the bloodline awakening became an accident, knocking him unconscious.”

At this thought, the human ringleader shook his head again.

“If I'd planted a spy, it wouldn't have been so crude and sloppy.”

"At the very least, I'd have arranged a proper cover identity. The rocket assault boat's teleportation capability manifested unexpectedly. Him rescuing Bing Xiao was also accidental."

“Regardless, I should inform the old gang leader of this news first.”

The human ringleader approached Bing Xiao's cabin door, about to knock.

Boom!

A sudden explosion rocked the entire ship violently.

Boom! Boom! Boom!

Explosions erupted one after another inside the Divine Wind, immediately causing significant casualties.

“What's happening?!” The human ringleader exclaimed in shock.

With a crash, the cabin door burst open, nearly colliding with the human ringleader.

Bing Xiao had just burst out when he and the human ringleader heard the Divine Wind's first mate shout: "Enemy attack!"

"Enemies again?"

“Where?”

Feng Lian, wielding his long-handled scythe, immediately prepared for battle, but could find no sign of the enemy.

“In the sea!” the lookout on the mast shouted the warning.

Chapter 607: Mian Li Cang

The lookout scanned the sea below and caught glimpses of giant turtle shadows flickering in and out of view.

Bang, bang, bang...

They kept slamming their horns into the Divine Wind's hull, sending the gold level magic ship reeling violently.

“It's a ramhorn turtle swarm!”

“At least three gold level ones.”

"How is that possible? We scanned this area—where did this swarm come from?"

“At this rate, the Divine Wind's hull will be breached!”

Feng Lian shot forward like an arrow.

Hovering midair, he swung his scythe, unleashing wind blades that sliced toward the ramhorn turtles below.

But in the next instant, dense spatial ripples erupted around each turtle.

One by one, the ramhorn turtles vanished into the distance, perfectly evading every wind blade.

Simultaneously, similar spatial ripples manifested within the Divine Wind's hold.

This time, bundles of alchemy bombs materialized out of thin air.

Boom! Boom! Boom!

The alchemy bombs detonated in rapid succession, wounding countless pirates and destroying vast sections of the ship's interior, causing immense destruction.

“Damn it!” Feng Lian had no choice but to dive back into the Divine Wind, taking control of the situation.

Though his scythe was razor-sharp and his personal combat prowess formidable, he found himself unable to act in this situation, his strength rendered useless.

Feng Lian nearly ground his teeth to dust.

He couldn't find the enemy!

"This style of attack... Mian Li Cang! You are the honorable leader of the Li Jian Clan! Have the courage to show yourself and face me in battle! Hiding elsewhere, where is your honor as a noble?!"

The next moment, Mian Li Cang's voice pierced the air, responding to Feng Lian.

“Captain of the Divine Wind Pirates, you struck while my clan tested its new naval warships during a vulnerable period, reaping immense gains. Did you truly believe you and your men could depart unscathed?”

“Now I shall make you and your crew pay a heavy price!”

Mian Li Cang possessed no noble honor, only utter shamelessness. Though the navy was ambusher, he spun it as if he were the victim.

As Mian Li Cang finished speaking, waves of spatial disturbances, varying in size, erupted once more.

This time, however, the disturbances were far more numerous. Their reach extended beyond the Divine Wind, engulfing nearly every pirate vessel.

Beasts materialized from thin air, launching frenzied assaults on all nearby people and objects.

Ice wolves, snow monsters, white tigers, and wyverns bearing the bloodline of white dragons appeared.

Chaos erupted throughout the Divine Wind Pirates.

Countless pirates were slaughtered.

They were forced to fight individually.

Bing Xiao and the human ringleader also found themselves in trouble.

“Run!”

Both were only silver level, now under attack by three silver level snow spiders.

Among the Bayonet Gang leaders, the human ringleader was already the weakest combatant.

Bing Xiao was ancient and specialized in breeding giant slaves. His personal combat prowess had never been exceptional.

Most critically, the beasts summoned by Mian Li Cang were utterly fearless.

They even turned on each other!

For Bing Xiao and the human ringleader, the most rational approach was to fight while retreating.

To confront them head-on was suicide!

This was the ship of the Divine Wind Pirates. Both Bing Xiao and the human ringleader agreed: Feng Lian would ultimately resolve all these troubles.

“Can anyone help me?” Bing Xiao shouted for help as he ran.

But everyone they encountered, whether members of the Bayonet Gang or the Divine Wind Pirates, was too preoccupied with their own survival.

Throughout their perilous escape, Bing Xiao witnessed countless corpses, blood staining the ship's holds and corridors crimson.

Every passing second felt like torture.

Finally, the pair succeeded in diverting the pursuers' attention by using others as decoys, temporarily escaping the crisis.

But the cost was immense.

A sharp ice spike pierced Bing Xiao's right shoulder. Frost elements were violently corroding his body.

The human ringleader brought up the rear, shielding Bing Xiao. Now he was covered in blood, a massive wound gushing uncontrollably from his midsection. His condition was critical.

As Bing Xiao passed by Lan Zao's cabin, he saw a giant ice wolf bursting through the door, poised to end Lan Zao's life.

"Save him!" Bing Xiao roared at the crucial moment.

The giant ice wolf was only bronze level. It was swiftly slain by the two. If it were a silver level, or even an iron level beast, Bing Xiao might have made a different choice.

“Quick, block the door with whatever you can find!” After slaying the wolf, Bing Xiao burst into the cabin and shouted.

The human ringleader hurriedly moved tables and chairs to seal the gaping hole in the cabin door, braced his body firmly against it, and pushed with all his might.

Outside, the sounds of pirates clashing with beasts echoed relentlessly—death throes, desperate cries for help...

Bing Xiao and the human ringleader remained tense and alert for a while before gradually relaxing.

During this time, though beasts approached the cabin with heavy footsteps, they all passed by without entering, drawn instead to the pirates putting up resistance.

“Phew, safe for now!” The human ringleader exhaled a cloud of stale breath before collapsing exhausted onto the floor.

“You've lost too much blood—you need bandages immediately!” Bing Xiao scanned the room, then strode swiftly to Lan Zao's bed. Bending low, he tore a strip from the bedsheet and tossed it to the human ringleader.

“You brat, I saved your life this time. We're even now,” Bing Xiao said, gazing at the unconscious Lan Zao.

“The bloodlust here is too strong. It'll draw beasts. We should move to a safer place,” Bing Xiao turned to the human ringleader.

The human ringleader's eyes were closed.

Bing Xiao's expression darkened instantly. “Damn it!” he cursed aloud.

He rushed back to the human ringleader, checking his condition.

The human ringleader wasn't dead—there was still a faint breath—but he lay unconscious from his severe injuries.

“Mian Li Cang is hiding within a sub-space. As long as he doesn't appear in the Main World, I can't threaten him.” Feng Lian pieced together the intelligence and grasped the situation.

Feng Lian felt his resolve waver.

He had provoked Mian Li Cang with everything he had, yet the man refused to show himself.

“Launch the ship! Full speed ahead! Get us far from here!” Feng Lian suddenly ordered.

The effect was immediate.

As they gradually distanced themselves, spatial fluctuations diminished sharply, and the number of beasts or alchemy bombs teleported by Mian Li Cang decreased dramatically.

Once the Divine Wind Pirates had pulled away to a safe distance, no more beasts or alchemy bombs appeared.

Mian Li Cang, residing within the suppressed space, relied on spatial positioning to teleport objects and creatures.

Once the Divine Wind pirate ships were no longer at their original location, pinpointing their new position became a cumbersome task.

For Mian Li Cang, separated by an entire plane, it required a series of complex recalculations to obtain new precise coordinates for accurate teleportation.

The Divine Wind Pirates fled in disarray.

Feng Lian's face was as cold as frost.

His combat prowess far surpassed that of Mian Li Cang, the current head of the Li Jian Clan. Yet, the enemy refused to engage him directly, instead continuously deploying forces across dimensions.

The frenzied, death-defying beasts served as perfect suicide troops..

The Divine Wind Pirates suffered devastating losses, all because of Mian Li Cang alone.

Feng Lian etched this humiliation deep into his heart.

“The Li Jian Clan... Mian Li Cang!” Feng Lian's eyes blazed with seething fury.

“They ambushed us again. How did they pinpoint our fleet's exact location?”

“There must be a mole aboard!”

"Investigate!!"

Chapter 608: The Fishman Youngster Appears!

Deep Sea Monster Fish.

The alchemy cabin.

Zhong Yong tilted his head back and downed a bottle of potion, instantly feeling a surge of energy. After days of relentless alchemy, his exhausted body was infused with fresh vitality.

The glow from the alchemy formation behind him gradually faded, revealing the fruits of his labor over the past days.

“Commander, the dual-eared detection puppet is complete,” he relayed to the dragonman youngster via communication.

Shortly after, the hatch slid open, and the “dragonman youngster” stepped inside.

Zhong Yong hurried forward to greet him and introduced, “These dual-eared alchemy puppets utilize sound wave transmission alchemy. They can emit sound waves across a wide range of frequencies.”

“Using them to probe the Ocean Mother Nest not only allows us to detect conditions within the nest, but the sound waves can also penetrate the cavern walls to gather some intelligence on the external environment.”

The “dragonman youngster” nodded slightly, “And what are the drawbacks of these puppets?”

Zhong Yong replied, "The main drawback is their energy consumption. They consume a tremendous amount of energy and require substantial replenishment at regular intervals."

“Additionally, they're quite fragile. Designed primarily for reconnaissance, their combat capabilities are weak.”

"Finally, their reconnaissance takes considerable time, largely due to how the sonic waves are utilized. We must deploy them covertly, allowing them to swim around the Mother Nest's perimeter, scatter, and emit sonic waves for continuous surveillance. It takes at least 24 hours to gather phased results."

The “dragonman youngster” pressed further: “Then when might we detect conditions beyond the Mother Nest?”

“Well...” Zhong Yong looked troubled, shaking his head slightly. "Commander, I've only heard of the Ocean Mother Nest—its secrets remain unknown to me. I cannot estimate how long the sonic waves would take to penetrate the Mother Nest's cavern walls. The composition and thickness of those walls are critical variables affecting the outcome."

The dragonman youngster pressed further, "Then, if we deploy these reconnaissance puppets, will the saint level sea beast react? Could the emitted sonic waves awaken it—or even provoke its wrath?"

Zhong Yong shook his head, “I cannot say, my lord.”

“This saint level sea beast remains curled in a ball. Its true nature remains unknown.”

“Every living being has a limited hearing range. If the sonic waves fall outside the saint level sea beast's audible spectrum, they might have no effect.”

“If within its hearing range, it would depend on the specific volume.”

"My suggestion is we test it first. Adjust the power from low to high, probing incrementally."

"I still believe there's risk involved. Even a pure-minded saint level sea beast, untouched by the outside world, would likely feel some irritation upon being roused from slumber, wouldn't it?"

"Commander, we're somewhere deep beneath the sea. This combat environment doesn't allow you to fully unleash your strength, and my own combat power is rather weak..."

Noticing Zhong Yong hesitating, the “dragonman youngster” smiled reassuringly, “Rest easy. This mobile alchemy vessel possesses combat capabilities.”

“I won't be fighting either; I'm only silver level.”

“Nor will I risk you in battle. Your value lies in alchemy.”

“I have confidence in the ship's camouflage capabilities. Moreover, my trusted confidant and capable assistant is aboard!”

"Come in. Hou Lang, you're a fishman too. Meet Zhong Yong."

Zhong Yong's interest was instantly piqued.

From the “dragonman youngster's” words, he gathered there were others aboard the alchemy ship.

This man was no ordinary figure—a high-ranking member of the Dragon Lion Mercenary Corps, deeply trusted by Commander Long Fu. And his identity? A fishman!

The next moment, the alchemy cabin door slid open again, revealing a fishman stepping inside.

This fishman stood a towering 1.6 meters tall.

Among his kind, that height was far from short.

His form was robust, his belly, thighs, and thick arms bulging with visible subcutaneous fat. His entire body was covered in deep blue fish scales, adorned with black, wavy patterns.

His eyes were large and pure black, giving off an intense, profound aura.

The life force emanating from him told Zhong Yong that this was a silver level transcendent fishman.

“Hello, my name is Zhong Yong. I recently joined the Dragon Lion Mercenary Corps,” Zhong Yong hurriedly introduced himself.

The dragonman youngster nodded in response. "I am Hou Lang, guardian of this submersible alchemy vessel. I know you. Though this is our first meeting, it is a pleasure to make your acquaintance."

The entity conversing with Zhong Yong was, in truth, an alchemy puppet equipped with a communication device.

Through the Anti-investigation and Prophecy spell, coupled with the Deceptive Disguise spell, the dragonman youngster had transformed it into his own dragonman form. His true form, meanwhile, had transformed into that of a fishman.

This allowed him to conceal many secrets from Zhong Yong.

For instance, that only the dragonman youngster and Zhong Yong were currently aboard the Deep Sea Monster Fish.

Or, for example, Commander Long Fu could effortlessly alter his form!

“You two talk. I have business to attend to.” The “dragonman youngster” departed first.

Hou Lang and Zhong Yong bowed in farewell.

Inside the alchemy cabin, only the two fishmen remained.

The dragonman youngster, or more accurate, the fishman youngster, now examined the reconnaissance puppets within the alchemy formation.

These puppets resembled rabbits, each sporting two long ears. One ear emitted sonic waves, while the other received them.

"Zhong Yong, you joining us has greatly enhanced our alchemy capabilities. We couldn't have crafted these before," the fishman youngster praised.

Zhong Yong chuckled: "This model of alchemy puppet is indeed ingenious. When I first got the alchemy blueprints, I studied them for quite some time."

“I've discovered our corps' alchemy knowledge is exceptionally refined.”

"During my entrance exam, I encountered a wondrous alchemy formation. Theoretically analyzing it, I discovered it actually affected bloodlines.

“This dual-eared reconnaissance puppet is a rare masterpiece, even by the standards of the Ice Sculpture Kingdom's Alchemy Guild.”

"I plan to craft other reconnaissance puppet models next. These puppets will be no weaker than the dual-eared detection puppets!"

The fishman youngster appraised Zhong Yong: “Seems you're quite enthusiastic about your work here. That's commendable.”

“I was a bit worried you might struggle adapting to this environment.”

“What's the big deal!” Zhong Yong shook his head, “Back in the Ice Sculpture Kingdom, I barely left my lab either. Every day was either alchemy experiments or alchemy itself.”

“The realm of alchemy is so vast, I could spend my entire life without fully grasping all its mysteries.”

“What's even more wonderful is that this realm expands every single moment.”

"In this world, there are alchemy geniuses who stand at the forefront of their specialized fields. Every tiny breakthrough they make is unprecedented. Thus, our alchemy domain ceaselessly expands, ever renewing itself."

“I would gladly dedicate my entire life to alchemy!”

Zhong Yong's expression softened with emotion, his eyes brimming with sincere passion.

"Ah, my apologies. Lord Hou Lang, it's rude to share such personal thoughts upon our first meeting." The next moment, Zhong Yong realized his error and apologized to the fishman youngster.

By normal standards, expressing such heartfelt sentiments to someone you've just met, even within the same race, would be considered abrupt.

But the fishman youngster had anticipated this.

He patted Zhong Yong's shoulder, “I see your magician's heart, Zhong Yong. I'm glad our corps has gained another fishman.”

“It's good you can adapt here.”

“If the chance arises, I'll speak to the commander about giving you opportunities for alchemy experiments.”

"Really? That's fantastic!" Zhong Yong's face lit up instantly.

Currently, he could only perform alchemy and work tirelessly, lacking the authority to independently design experiments, explore, or verify theories aboard the Deep Sea Monster Fish.

“Honestly, seeing a fellow fishman here feels really good.”

Zhong Yong admitted, “I've always had this worry. You know, the commander is a dragonman, and most members of the Dragon Lion Mercenary Corps are human.”

Racial barriers were everywhere.

Zhong Yong couldn't help but share such worries.

The fishman youngster desperately wanted to tell him, 'Your worries are completely unnecessary. What does fishman even mean in this team? We even have necromancers.'

After offering more comfort and encouragement to Zhong Yong, he left the alchemy cabin.

After their conversation, Zhong Yong felt rejuvenated, a powerful surge of motivation welling up from within as he threw himself into another round of alchemy.

Meanwhile, the fishman youngster reflected on their meeting: “Is this the effect of the Affinity Aura?”

It was one of the transcendent abilities granted by his fishman form.

Chapter 609: Mental Secret

After the dragonman youngster devoured Hai Ping's saint corpse with his Bloodcore, he unexpectedly gained two types of fishman bloodlines.

This outcome surprised him.

The concentrations of the two fishman bloodlines were 52% and 11%, respectively.

He experimented with mutations one after another. The 52% concentration of fishman blood transformed him into a yellow green fishman—tall, burly, with bulging muscles and sturdy bones.

Due to its distinct characteristics, the dragonman youngster easily identified it through the tower spirit's records: this was the Barbarian Fish bloodline.

The Barbarian Fish bloodline was a gold level lineage.

Currently, it possesses only one innate talent: racial magic—Berserk.

The Berserk talent was commonly found among barbarian tribes.

The Frost Continent was even larger than the Sheng Ming Continent and the domain of the barbarians in the main world.

Once a barbarian entered Berserk, their individual combat power surged severalfold. The more formidable the barbarian warrior, the greater the destructive force of their Berserk state. This led to the legendary prestige of elite barbarian warriors spreading far and wide.

In ancient times, barbarians and dragons were equally renowned, reigning as the world's dominant races. The Berserk talent played a pivotal role in this.

Had it not been for the critical drawback of losing rationality during the Berserk state, the course of world history and the status of the barbarians would have been utterly transformed.

Of course, certain barbarian prodigies could maintain composure during their Berserk state.

This proved that the drawback of Berserk could be overcome.
