Yet the grouper school drawn by the head showed no less fury, charging en masse into the white hair forest.

The white coral polyps' revolt showed no signs of ceasing. Driven by biological instinct, they couldn't obey orders to stop.

Each white, soft coral lashed viciously at the grouper, immediately provoking a counterattack from the school.

Thus, a fierce battle erupted.

The white forest lashed at the grouper as if they were enemies, brutally whipping and killing large numbers of them.

The vast grouper school counterattacked with racial magic.

Their primary racial magic was petrification.

Strand after strand of white coral hair was turned to stone. Amidst the chaos, they were either snapped by other strands or shattered by colliding groupers.

The fierce battle raged for over ten minutes.

The final outcome was the complete annihilation of the grouper swarm. The white hair forest also suffered heavy losses. Its former lush splendor vanished, leaving the white hair sparse and thin, with some areas completely bald.

Though the white hair coral colony emerged victoriously, it was clearly a pyrrhic victory.

A ghost ship silently surfaced.

On it were Jian Gui, Cang Xu, and the others.

The grouper head from earlier was actually the deceased skull of a grouper controlled by Cang Xu.

By exploiting living creatures' aversion and rage toward the undead, he had successfully lured a grouper swarm into the white hair forest, igniting the clash between the two colonies.

Now came the cleanup.

Over a dozen ghosts leapt from the ship, most of them gold level!

They charged directly into the white hair forest.

The forest erupted again, lashing out at the ghosts.

White hair corals dealt physical blows, but these ghosts were immune to physical attacks, suffering no harm.

Only racial magic could truly harm these specters.

But the white hair coral polyps lacked such innate abilities. Their greatest combat capability was manipulating the white hair coral to lash out.

More seasoned polyps could also control soft corals to entangle and strangle enemies.

Considering that white soft corals were essentially coral polyps' excretions, in a sense, these polyps were fighting with their own feces.

A group of “shit-manipulating” little experts.

Beyond this, the white hair coral polyps possessed no other combat abilities.

Coral polyps were a type of insectoid and lacked any racial magic.

This lack was not a disadvantage but rather an advantage.

As parasites, these polyps relied on inhabiting other life forms to survive and reproduce.

Their hosts were typically massive sea beasts possessing racial magic. They could passively absorb elements from nature to form magic crystals, using various racial magic against enemies to dominate the food chain.

If white hair coral polyps could cast racial magic spells, it proved they possess magic crystals within their bodies. Regardless of the quantity of magic crystals, they inevitably clashed with the sea beasts they parasitize, causing issues like magic interference and magic repulsion.

Under such circumstances, the host sea beast prioritized purging the coral polyps from its body to ensure its own safety and survival.

This effectively eliminated the coral polyps' primary habitat for survival.

Thus, mastering racial magic becomes a catastrophe for these white hair coral polyps.

Watching the ghosts effortlessly kill the last coral, the undead ronin Ku Feng stood at the bow of the ghost ship and sighed, "If it were the grouper school's petrification spell, it would be much harder to deal with. Ghosts or skeletons hit by petrification would likely not survive. But these white hair corals? They let us slaughter them without any resistance at all."

This wasn't to say Jian Gui's crew lacked means to break petrification.

Where there's petrification, there's naturally a spell like “Dispel Petrification.”

Even though most were necromancers, they always carried generic spell scrolls for contingencies.

Cang Xu, being a penniless wretch, naturally possessed none. But Jian Gui most likely had scrolls for dispelling petrification. He even carried a spare spellbook!

Yet the reality was this: for the vast majority of skeletons and ghosts afflicted by petrification, the necromancers chose not to rescue them.

The reason was simple - cost.

While casting a spell like Dispel Petrification wasn't particularly costly, the magic power and spirit expended were renewable resources. But for necromancers, they could create more skeletons and ghosts at a much lower cost.

They would only intervene to save high-quality skeletons or ghosts if they were petrified.

Xiao Wang Zi now stood beside the undead ronin Ku Feng, his voice tinged with regret. "This flat seabed terrain would be perfect for my heavy cavalry to charge across. Alas, in this battle, Lord Jian Gui forbade me from joining the fray."

Since eradicating that herd of ramhorn turtles, Xiao Wang Zi had amassed a vast number of ramhorn turtle skeletons. These skeletons were natural heavy cavalry. Xiao Wang Zi particularly cherished this small cavalry unit, longing daily to deploy them.

Yet the complex and unique underwater environment rarely offered a stage where these ramhorn turtle skeletons could truly showcase their strength.

Cang Xu offered reassurance from the side: "Xiao Wang Zi, when we rescue Qi Ling, we'll likely face pirates. Then, will your ramhorn turtle squad really lack a place to prove itself?"

“Before that, we should focus on leveraging our strengths, gathering as many resources as possible to bolster our own combat power and increase the success rate of this rescue mission.”

“Xu Jia is absolutely right.” Jian Gui materialized as well.

Over these days, the necromancer's crew had launched numerous raids across the White Hair Trench, significantly bolstering their combat strength.

Initially, Jian Gui had to merge with the ghost ship to control the battlefield.

Now, the frequency of such merges has noticeably decreased. Relying on their vast legions of ghosts and skeletons, they could now confront most undersea beast swarms head-on.

In truth, many engagements didn't even require direct confrontation.

Take the recent battle, for instance. Deploying just one of Cang Xu's silver level death heads had secured a massive advantage.

Most sea beasts possessed little intelligence.

In this regard, the advantage of sentient races was starkly evident.

After brief communication, the necromancers began cleanup.

Though numerous, skeletons and ghosts possessed low intelligence and were unsuited for this task. Thus, such meticulous work requires the necromancers' personal attention.

Several summoning spells coalesced a swarm of grouper ghosts onto the battlefield. Alongside them were countless, teeming little ghosts of coral polyps.

These tiny spirits were so feeble that many dissolved into nothingness the moment they materialized. Like bubbles bursting, they vanished without a sound.

Insect souls were inherently fragile, and after death, the success rate of summoning them was far lower than for other lifeforms.

However, Jian Gui soon intervened personally, carefully preserving and gathering these insect ghosts.

“I want all these coral polyp ghosts,” Jian Gui declared.

This request puzzled Cang Xu. But he quickly heard Xiao Wang Zi explain: “In the advanced soul aspect inheritance, there exists a specialized insect soul transformation spell capable of altering insect swarms into ghost hordes... This ghost horde is like an elemental conglomerate, capable of coalescing and dispersing at will. They're far more troublesome than ordinary modified ghosts."

“Advanced soul aspect inheritance...” Cang Xu sighed inwardly.

He yearned to possess such inherited knowledge. Yet, inheritance of this caliber had always been guarded by the orthodox undead factions.

With his lowly bloodline, Cang Xu was utterly unworthy of Jian Gui's recognition, let alone obtaining such inheritance.

He could only drool with envy.

Still, in this hunt, Cang Xu's spoils were second only to Jian Gui's.

This was mainly because he had sacrificed a silver level grouper death head.

After clearing the battlefield, the necromancers worked together to properly neutralize all traces of undead energy in the area.

Finally, they boarded the ghost ship and vanished silently into the dark depths of the ocean.

On the ghost ship, Cang Xu returned to his cabin and began processing the newly acquired grouper heads.

He didn't use the Head Creation Spell to directly create death heads, opting instead for some basic preliminary processing. Then, he placed these processed grouper heads in a corner of the cabin.

The cabin corner was already piled high with undead materials.

These undead materials shared a common trait: they were all heads of some marine creature.

None of them had been refined yet.

Cang Xu selected several heads from the pile and placed them before him.

After a moment of meditation, he cast the Head Creation Spell upon them.

After several attempts, the silver level undead materials were depleted, yet Cang Xu gained nothing.

No matter, plenty of silver level skulls remained in the hold's corner.

After two rounds, Cang Xu finally obtained a brand-new silver level death head.

Higher level death heads carried lower success rates.

But Cang Xu had plenty to spare. He charged ahead relentlessly, knowing that persistence would eventually yield success.

The White Hair Trench teemed with sea beasts and schools of fish, yet Cang Xu's haul remained meager. Xiao Wang Zi and Jian Gui fared much better than him.

Thus, Cang Xu's stock of silver level death heads returned to its peak, reaching five.

Beyond that, he possessed 48 iron level death heads and 122 bronze level ones.

This represented the absolute limit Cang Xu could currently command.

This burdened him with immense mental strain.

The strain from death heads was far less than that from ghosts or skeletons. But remember, Cang Xu was only an iron level magician.

His mental fortitude was weaker than that of a silver level magician.

The relief spells, Burden Alleviation Spell and Karma Reduction Spell, offered him little help now.

Since acquiring these spells, Cang Xu had used them nearly every day.

The benefits gained from them grew increasingly faint.

The Karma Reduction Spell lessens the erosion of negative energy upon him, preventing the extinction of his humanity. This spell was most effective when used at intervals, as the erosion from negative energy was cumulative.

The Burden Alleviation Spell lessened the mental strain his undead minions imposed upon the necromancer. This was the cornerstone of a necromancer commanding an entire army single-handedly.

Without the Burden Alleviation Spell, the number of death heads Cang Xu could control would plummet by at least two-thirds.

But now, even the Burden Alleviation Spell's effect on him had become barely perceptible.

During these days deep beneath the sea, Cang Xu's personal combat prowess had also expanded tremendously. But having reached this point today, he understood he had hit his limit.

His bloodline firmly constrained him.

“I've already touched the ceiling. Unless my bloodline becomes superior...”

Though his bloodline mutation was still ongoing, Cang Xu had abandoned all expectations for his own bloodline.

He could distinctly sense the bloodline mutation was nearing its final stage.

His cultivation efficiency showed no significant change, having even weakened slightly.

The trajectory of the bloodline mutation was clear. Cang Xu had already foreseen its outcome, neither good nor bad, merely maintaining the status quo. Strictly speaking, it had slightly diminished his innate talent, though the reduction was so minimal it was almost imperceptible. It could be considered on par with his previous state.

Cang Xu accepted this outcome swiftly.

Bloodline mutations were inherently unpredictable. Most resulted in deterioration; only a rare few brought improvement.

This differed from bloodline awakening.

Bloodline awakening typically yielded positive results. Essentially, it involved the bloodline concentration achieving a quantitative accumulation, reaching a qualitative leap through gradual change.

Chapter 622: My Wretched Alchemy Attainment

Numerous factors could trigger bloodline mutations.

Take Cang Xu, for instance. His body was submerged in the Underworld River, then dismembered by Doctor Sui Shi, who used his flesh as alchemy ingredients. These combined circumstances forced his bloodline to mutate. This process was utterly irreplicable.

“Maintaining nearly all my original aptitude is already a blessing.”

“My life-changing opportunity has appeared; it lies within the commander himself!”

"What I truly need is a spell to test my necromancer aptitude, one capable of discerning my undead bloodline. Alas, such spells exist only within intermediate soul aspect inheritances, held firmly in the hands of Jian Gui."

As a necromancer, Cang Xu couldn't test his bloodline through potion-based blood analysis like other survivors.

This lay beyond Zi Di's knowledge.

Only by obtaining the necromancer aptitude detection spell could he determine the true nature of his bloodline. Then he could hunt vast numbers of such undead beings, purify his own bloodline through the Bloodline Purification formation, and achieve bloodline awakening.

Yet, having journeyed alongside Jian Gui, Cang Xu had yet to find an opportunity to acquire this crucial spell.

Jian Gui adhered to strict principles in this matter. Cang Xu remained cautious, refraining from rash communication. He possessed no other bargaining chips and knew full well the outcome of such an approach.

“If all else fails, I'll simply wait until the bloodline mutation concludes before requesting Jian Gui perform this spell to identify my undead lineage.”

Cang Xu naturally had contingency plans.

But this plan had many drawbacks, hindering Cang Xu's chances of gaining Jian Gui's recognition.

Jian Gui possessed many valuable resources.

Cang Xu desperately wanted to use this connection to infiltrate the inner circle of the undead orthodoxy. That was the sound long-term strategy!

If Jian Gui detected Cang Xu's bloodline now, the results would be clear, he would be utterly rejected from entering the exclusive circle of the undead orthodoxy.

This was not what Cang Xu desired.

“Now, the only thing offering me significant room for improvement is the Ghost Seed Head.” Though Cang Xu had reached his bloodline's limit, there remained untapped potential in his combat prowess.

The Ghost Seed Head emerged from the self-destruction of a death head. When combined with the necromancy magic “Head-Binding Spell,” it could merge with its user. In this form, the Ghost Seed Head functioned as a unique spellbook. All necromancy magic recorded within could be rapidly cast by the necromancer.

A silver level Ghost Seed Head was equivalent to a silver level spellbook. The spells contained depend entirely on how many the Ghost Seed Head's original death head had mastered.

Of course, there was an inherent decay.

The best outcome was 100% inheritance. However, according to the records of Flying Head Spell, such a complete inheritance was extremely rare.

The exact degree of decay depended on specific circumstances.

The Flying Head Spell texts were rather vague on this point, leaving Cang Xu to figure it out through trial and error.

His current combat power had reached a plateau, leaving the Ghost Seed Head as the most promising avenue for advancement.

With the ship's hold piled high with undead materials, the conditions for Cang Xu to mass-produce Ghost Seed Heads were now ripe.

“So, which necromancy magic would be best suited for me?”

Cang Xu fell deep in thought.

He possessed numerous necromancy magic spells.

He possessed the foundational inheritance of the soul aspect by birth. Later, at the necromancer gathering on Weeping Pine Island, he traded with figures like Jian Gui to acquire the foundational inheritance of the two other aspects, along with the elementary inheritance of the soul aspect.

He had also duplicated the content and entrusted them to Zi Di.

The three necromancy aspects were corpse, bone, and soul.

All the spells Cang Xu mastered belonged to these three aspects.

For example: soul aspect spells like the Soul Summoning, Soul Control, Soul Capture, Soul Devourer, and Soul Send.

Bone aspect spells like Bone Spear and Flying Bone Shield.

Corpse aspect spells like Corpse Explosion and Summon Zombie.

Cang Xu faced many choices, but after deliberation, he first eliminated all ghost modification spells from the soul aspect inheritance.

These modification spells included Ghost Horse Refinement, Ghost Elf Refinement, Hungry Ghost Refinement, and Frost Ghost Refinement...

Cang Xu currently primarily controlled death heads.

The mental burden brought by these ghosts far exceeded that of death heads. If modified, the mental strain from the ghosts would only increase further.

Cang Xu's mental burden had reached its limit.

Of course, he could sacrifice some death heads to gain space and control certain ghosts.

But after deliberation, Cang Xu rejected this plan.

Even though modified ghosts were often more powerful than death heads, his greatest concern was that manipulating these ghosts would create vulnerabilities, allowing Jian Gui, Xiao Wang Zi, and others to uncover his tricks.

Jian Gui and Xiao Wang Zi knew nothing of the Flying Head Spell, so they couldn't discern Cang Xu's mental state from the scale of his death heads.

The truth was that Cang Xu's death heads could expand no further. If he switched to ghosts, it would be easy for Jian Gui and others to pick up on crucial intelligence.

Though Cang Xu had integrated into this necromancer group, he understood clearly: this environment was no match for the Dragon Lion Mercenary Corps.

Especially during the battle at the Underworld River, where Jian Gui used him as bait to draw enemy fire. Cang Xu nearly died because of it!

For the same reasons he ruled out the ghost modification spells, Cang Xu also eliminated the Soul Control Spell, Soul Summoning Spell, and other similar ones.

The Burden Alleviation Spell and Karma Reduction Spell were core techniques in the elementary soul aspect inheritance, practices a necromancer cultivated throughout their life.

Yet Cang Xu didn't ponder long before dismissing these two as well. For now, their utility was negligible.

The Soul's Eye spell allowed necromancers to share vision with their subordinate ghosts. This served as a highly practical reconnaissance tool. Yet Cang Xu reasoned: currently stationed with other necromancers aboard the ghost ship, whose inherent surveillance capabilities far exceeded his own, his personal reconnaissance abilities were redundant. Thus, it too was discarded.

Soul Healing proved exceptionally suitable. This spell consumed ghosts to grant healing effects. Cang Xu promptly added it to his list of potential choices.

Ghost Fog was also decent. Primarily used for camouflage, it could also create negative energy environments to temporarily bind souls, facilitating follow-up spells like Soul Control or Summoning. Advanced ghost magic would be battlefield-class spells capable of altering the environment, similar to the effect Lava Hell had when the dragonman youngster used it.

Ghost Fog suited Cang Xu well.

Since the death head was also an undead lifeform, it benefited within the ghost fog. Cang Xu selected it as a candidate.

The final candidate was a rapid-movement soul aspect spell called Ghost Slip. It consumed ghosts to gain mobility boosts.

Cang Xu was more interested in Soul Flight. It allows the necromancer to fully phantomize. Advanced Soul Flight could even spectralize the necromancer's equipment, granting immunity to physical attacks.

Jian Gui had deeper experience with this spell. Soul Flight was one of the foundations for his fusion with the ghost ship.

Unfortunately, Soul Flight was only recorded in intermediate soul aspect inheritances.

Cang Xu's aptitude only allowed him to obtain an elementary soul aspect inheritance at best.

Beyond soul aspect inheritance, the Corpse Explosion from the corpse aspect was also promising. Cang Xu considered the Bone Spear and Flying Bone Shield spells from the bone aspect as potential choices.

After the first round of selection, Cang Xu proceeded to a second round of screening.

Only three options remained: Flying Bone Shield, Ghost Slip, and Soul Healing.

All three spellbooks were essential choices Cang Xu needed to acquire. The only question was which one would be his first.

After careful consideration, Cang Xu placed Ghost Slip first.

Though this spell belonged to the elementary soul aspect inheritance, Cang Xu had practiced it extensively.

He conceived the magic model within his mind, then cradled a death head in both hands, imprinting the model onto it.

Plop.

The bronze level death head emitted a soft pop as it detonated.

The explosion was minor, yet Cang Xu's face paled. The magic model was constructed with his spirit; its destruction inflicted damage upon him.

Several consecutive failures followed, leaving Cang Xu's complexion as white as paper.

Suddenly, he realized: "Wait, isn't this exactly how one crafts spell scrolls or spell books? The only difference is that normal magicians use paper, while I use death heads."

A sense of foreboding washed over Cang Xu as he rolled his eyes and sighed deeply, “My wretched alchemy attainment...”

Ever since acquiring the Flying Head Spell, Cang Xu's lamentations over his alchemy attainment had skyrocketed.

In a foul mood, he chose to rest. After recuperating his spirit, he resumed his experiments.

Cang Xu holed up in his cabin, cradling skulls in his palms. He carved runes relentlessly, day and night, enduring the grueling process.

Soon, a bright, sunny day arrived.

Blood Kin City.

Zi Di visited a medicine shop to investigate the market.

The dragon rider potion was already on sale at the medicine shop, and it sold quite well. Zi Di stood there for over ten minutes and witnessed more than a dozen transactions for the dragon rider potion.

“May I assist you, honored customer? What potion might you be seeking?” the shop attendant approached.

Zi Di produced her contract to verify her identity, eliciting a low gasp from the attendant.

Soon after, the shopkeeper hurried over: “My Lord, you've arrived!”

This medicine shop belonged to the Li Jian Clan, but through Zi Di and Kong Pan's partnership, it had been leased to the Dragon Lion Mercenary Corps.

The lease term was one year.

Zi Di could certainly pay an additional price to outright purchase the shop instead of renting it.

For the wealthy Li Jian Clan, this was negotiable.

But considering the survivors' precarious, uncertain future, renting was safer than buying.

“Let me see the ledgers first,” Zi Di requested.

Soon after, the merchant lady set down the ledgers, her thoughts turning: “With Bu Quan and others mass-producing potions, I've already successfully captured the mount potion market in Blood Kin City ahead of schedule.”

“At this production level, we can supply all key towns near Snow Bird Harbor.”

“Still, I must personally investigate user feedback.”

Her thoughts drifting, Zi Di glanced back at the ledger: “The largest customer group here is... the Axe Gang?”

Zi Di paused, slightly taken aback.

Chapter 623: Blood Vengeance Axe and Fire Axe Cavalry

Ice Sculpture Kingdom.

Blood Kin City.

After reviewing the ledgers, Zi Di discovered something peculiar: the largest purchaser of dragon rider potions in the city was a single gang.

The shopkeeper explained, "Lord Magician, you may not be aware, but the Axe Gang is the largest gang in Blood Kin City, owning a vast herd of long-haired horses. These horses are exceptionally cold-resistant. Though their charge distance is short, they possess remarkable endurance, allowing them to gallop freely in icy, frigid weather."

“Some superior long-haired horses even possess racial magic that provide warmth to their riders.”

“The Axe Gang has formed a cavalry unit with these horses, known as the Fire Axe Cavalry.”

Zong Ge, who had accompanied Zi Di, initially paid little attention. But upon hearing the shopkeeper's words, his keen interest was piqued, and he turned his gaze toward them.

Noticing Commander Long Fu's attention, the shopkeeper elaborated further: "The Fire Axe Cavalry wields long-handled battle axes as weapons and cultivates the Blood Rage battle qi technique. Only a small number are snow elves; most are humans and dwarves."

"When fighting at full strength, the Blood Rage battle qi erupts, often igniting the warriors themselves. Their long-haired horses catch fire too. In such moments, both rider and mount are bathed in crimson flames, their attack power surging severalfold!"

Zong Ge listened intently, encountering this type of squadron for the first time.

He envisioned countless scenarios in his mind, pondering how such a unit could be deployed for optimal battlefield performance.

Zi Di, however, focused on another point and asked the shopkeeper, “What exactly does a local gang accomplish by maintaining a cavalry unit?”

The shopkeeper hastened to reply, "The Axe Gang's biggest business is escorting merchant caravans. They protect goods for caravans traveling to and from Blood Kin City. The Fire Axe Cavalry is their biggest selling point."

“Of course,” the shopkeeper lowered his voice at this point, "if any merchant alliance refuses to cooperate with them, the Axe Gang members turn into bandits and carry out robberies. Very few caravans can defeat the Fire Axe Cavalry, so by now, most merchant alliances in Blood Kin City basically cooperate with the Axe Gang."

“Even major merchant alliances like the Clam Cloth Merchant Alliance, despite having their own formidable guards, maintain ties with the Axe Gang.”

Zi Di furrowed her brow slightly, her expression growing grave. "The Axe Gang is merely a local gang, yet they control the trade routes outside Blood Kin City, extorting protection fees from caravans large and small. Does the Blood Kin City Lord not intervene in this?"

“The Axe Gang leader is gold level, but the Blood Kin City Lord is also gold level—and he's a magician.”

Before setting out, Zi Di had already purchased extensive intelligence on the towns along their route through the Thieves' Guild.

The shopkeeper lowered his voice further. "Our Blood Kin City Lord is indeed gold level and a magician, but he hasn't been seen in ages. Rumor has it he's been training in the royal capital, preparing to win this year's Warm Snow Cup tournament. His disciple, merely a silver level magician, has been handling Blood Kin City's affairs in his stead."

“Moreover, the Axe Gang leader isn't just a gold level fighter, he wields a gold level weapon - the Blood Vengeance Axe.”

"The Blood Vengeance Axe isn't an ordinary gold level weapon. Far surpassing ordinary gold level weapons, it long served as the symbol of authority for Blood Kin City's rulers."

“However, over forty years ago, a rebellion erupted in the Ice Sculpture Kingdom.”

"At that time, Blood Kin City was still known as Snow Fall City. The ruler of Snow Fall City, a member of the royal family, suffered defeat and, fearing death, shamefully surrendered to the rebels."

"The king successfully quelled the rebellion and reclaimed Snow Fall City. He intended to execute the Snow Fall City Lord, but the king's mother interceded on his behalf. After all, he was of royal blood."

"The king thus kept the city lord's position, merely changing the city's name. It was only after this renaming that it became known as Blood Kin City."

Upon learning the city's new name, the city lord was overcome with guilt and took his own life that very night.

From that day forward, the Blood Vengeance Axe fell into the hands of outsiders, changing masters many times before finally landing with a powerful snow elf warrior. Armed with this axe, he carved out a domain within the city and founded the Axe Gang.

“The Axe Gang's leadership has passed down to the current generation, and the gang's influence has grown immensely.”

"Our acting city lord has attempted several times to retrieve the Blood Vengeance Axe from the Axe Gang. Each attempt was thwarted by the current Axe Gang leader, costing our acting city lord dearly."

“Sigh, she's still too young.”

"After several clashes between the two sides, the Axe Gang has consistently gained the upper hand. The city lord's faction has grown increasingly weak. All other factions witnessed this, powerless to challenge the Axe Gang."

"So now, though the acting lord's faction holds nominal authority in Blood Kin City and commands the city guard, the Axe Gang remains the true dominant force.

The shopkeeper paused here, his expression shifting to one of satisfaction and delight: “Truth be told, the dragon rider potion is truly remarkable.”

Thanks to this potion, our shop has established connections with the Axe Gang."

“Before, some competitors tried to squeeze us out, but since the Axe Gang started bulk-buying dragon rider potions, that kind of harassment and exclusion has dropped significantly.”

After hearing the shopkeeper's words, Zong Ge and Zi Di exchanged silent glances.

They hadn't imagined the true state of affairs in Blood Kin City would be like this.

In Snow Bird Harbor, the Snow Bird Harbor City Lord was undoubtedly the most powerful figure. Yet here, the acting city Blood Kin City Lord was being dominated by a local gang.

Though the acting city lord held the title, it was clear she was powerless against the Axe Gang. When it came to the power struggles of local factions, one had to look first to the leaders themselves.

After a moment's thought, Zi Di made a swift decision: “Prepare gifts. It seems we must pay a proper visit to the Axe Gang's leader.”

“Is that really necessary?” Zong Ge frowned at the suggestion.

Coming from a military background, he harbored little regard for pirates or local gangs.

Zi Di nodded slightly, “Our alchemy workshop in Snow Bird Harbor focuses almost exclusively on one thing: brewing dragon rider potions.”

“Our shipments will only grow larger.”

“If the Axe Gang acts against us, Blood Kin City will struggle to distribute our goods.”

"And Blood Kin City is one of the closest cities to Snow Bird Harbor. For nationwide distribution, this is a crucial transportation hub. The Axe Gang controls the nearby trade routes, which is a major factor."

“Very well,” Zong Ge nodded, “I'll go with you.”

Despite their serious disagreements and even arguments, when problems arose, he would set aside their differences and cooperate with Zi Di to handle them first.

The Axe Gang was the most powerful faction in Blood Kin City. With their Fire Axe Cavalry, they now control the trade routes surrounding the city.

The acting city lord has been powerless against them. She hasn't even deployed the city guard.

Zi Di immediately suspected: The Axe Gang has likely corrupted many officers. If the acting city lord ordered their eradication, the city guard would likely defect.

She felt no motivation whatsoever to restore order for the acting city lord. Her purpose in this journey, aside from entering the Ice Sculpture Capital, was to conduct smooth reconnaissance along the way and execute her business plans.

Sales of dragon rider potions were booming, yet Zi Di remained unsatisfied. The mount potion market in Blood Kin City still held vast untapped potential.

The Dragon Lion Mercenary Corps had significantly increased potion production, meaning more dragon rider potions would soon flood the city's market.

Only by nearly monopolizing this mount potion niche could she achieve relative security.

Even if similar potions emerged later, they'd struggle to gain traction in Blood Kin City. The market space would be almost entirely occupied by dragon rider potions.

Although a direct teleportation formation connects Blood Kin City and Snow Bird Harbor, the Dragon Lion Mercenary Corps cannot possibly use teleportation for shipping due to cost.

Generally speaking, this mode of transport was reserved for major military operations.

Using teleportation formations for cargo shipment was prohibitively expensive. Even Zong Ge knew such a venture would incur massive losses.

The most cost-effective transport method was caravan delivery.

If the Axe Gang interfered, it would inevitably cause Zi Di immense trouble. She aimed to seize the market with dragon rider potions, and timing was crucial.

Any delay would mean achieving the same market impact later would require more than double the effort. It might even become impossible to ever achieve equivalent results.

Chapter 624: Fake Medicine Plan

Snow Bird Harbor.

The alchemy workshop was thick with smoke and heavy with the pungent scent of herbs.

Magician Zhong Bei from the Alchemists' Guild covered his mouth as he stepped inside, immediately engulfed by the acrid fumes.

The same fate befell the gold level alchemist, Da Bei.

With graying temples and a weathered face, Da Bei swiftly covered his nostrils with a handkerchief amidst the thick fumes.

“Lord Magicians, this way please.” Guided by an alchemy apprentice, they were led into a small, separate compartment.

The smoke here was finally less oppressive.

Here, Zhong Bei and Da Bei finally encountered the person they sought - Magician Bu Quan.

Magician Bu Quan was conducting an alchemy experiment.

Before her, a table held a small crucible, steaming as it was baked by magic flames. In the glass beaker she held, swaying gently, blue and red potions rippled.

Bu Quan was experimenting with refining the alchemy process for the dragon rider potion.

The formula for the dragon rider potion came from the War Merchant's database and was quite sophisticated. Even given Bu Quan's alchemy expertise, significantly altering the formula itself would prove challenging in such a short timeframe.

However, she could refine the alchemy process itself, making large-scale, streamlined production feasible.

Once successful, the Dragon Lion Mercenary Corps' output of this potion would increase at least threefold!

“Magician Bu Quan, it's been two years since we last met, hasn't it?” Magician Da Bei initiated the greeting.

Bu Quan glanced up at him before quickly returning her gaze to her beaker, “Lord Da Bei,” she replied, "you've traveled all the way from the capital specifically to see me. Is this about me joining the Dragon Lion Mercenary Corps? If you're here to persuade me to leave the mercenary group and return to the Alchemy Guild, then there's no need to say more."

“As you can see, I'm quite busy right now.”

"Why not skip the small talk and get straight to the point? It saves time and benefits us both, wouldn't you agree?"

“So, my answer is - I'm staying here.”

Magician Da Bei's mouth opened slightly. His carefully prepared words of persuasion were completely derailed by Bu Quan's blunt response.

Taking a deep breath, Magician Da Bei clutched his chest, “Magician Bu Quan, seeing you like this truly pains me.”

“As your senior, I cannot bear to watch a rising star of alchemy fade away before my eyes.”

“Look at yourself now. What are you doing?”

“You're refining processes for mass-producing potions? Such mundane, uninspired work, is it truly worthy of a genius like you?”

“Is this how the Dragon Lion Mercenary Corps treats you?”

“You're wasting your time, squandering your talent, and expending your future.”

Bu Quan didn't even bother to lift her head this time. She simply issued her dismissal: “The door is over there. Gentlemen, you're welcome to leave now.”

Magician Zhong Bei and Magician Da Bei: ...

Moments later.

The two magicians were escorted out by the alchemy apprentice.

“Ahem, ahem, ahem.” Magician Zhong Bei coughed loudly.

Magician Da Bei lowered his handkerchief, greedily inhaling the fresh air outside the alchemy workshop.

Magician Zhong Bei looked deeply concerned: "Father, what are we to do now? Magician Bu Quan's resolve is too strong. You offered such generous terms just now, yet she remained unmoved. It seems I'm in for trouble this time."

Magician Da Bei sighed deeply, “This is my fault. I never imagined such a task would lead to such complications. Remember how much I sacrificed and owed favors to secure this assignment for you?”

“Father, I've let you down.” Magician Zhong Bei bowed his head; his voice filled with regret.

Though born into the Alchemy Guild, his innate talent was limited by bloodline—far weaker than his father's.

He was destined to remain confined to the silver level.

Magician Da Bei had only this one son, pinning his hopes on him to inherit the family legacy. Thus, Magician Da Bei had mobilized his connections and resources, going to great lengths to secure this position for him - to secretly protect Bu Quan and monitor him for the Alchemy Guild.

This role not only offered generous rewards in guild contributions but also provided an opportunity to establish ties with Bu Quan. Should Bu Quan advance to the gold level or achieve greater alchemy feats, Magician Zhong Bei could profit from this early connection.

It could be said Magician Da Bei spared no effort for his son.

Alas, man proposes, heaven disposes. The Bayonet Gang was utterly annihilated within a single day. After successfully exacting her revenge, Bu Quan actually joined an outsider mercenary group!

If this news reached the Alchemy Guild, it'd definitely cause an uproar. As the one responsible for his covert surveillance, Magician Zhong Bei would surely bear the blame. Given his talent and alchemy prowess, he'd essentially reached the pinnacle of his career within the Alchemy Guild.

After failing to persuade Bu Quan, Zhong Bei hurriedly relayed the news to his father.

As a gold level Alchemy Great Master, Da Bei rushed straight to Snow Bird Harbor.

Yet his attempt to reason with Bu Quan also ended in failure.

“Father, what should we do now?”

“Calm down. Think carefully, there must be a way.” Da Bei's breathing gradually steadied.

He deliberately tested Zhong Bei: “Have you thought of any solutions?”

Magician Zhong Bei pondered: "If we can't bribe Bu Quan, perhaps we could bribe the Dragon Lion Mercenary Corps directly? If the temptation is high enough, I believe Commander Long Fu will voluntarily expel Bu Quan. That way, Magician Bu Quan would have no choice but to return to the Alchemy Guild."

Magician Da Bei shook his head. "You underestimate the value of a silver level Alchemy Great Master! Bu Quan is already indispensable to the Dragon Lion Mercenary Corps' production plan. Her worth will only grow. If they were just ordinary mercenaries, heavy bribes might work. But they're backed by arms dealers. Think about it, would a power capable of transnational arms smuggling be swayed by our petty interests?"

Magician Zhong Bei fell silent.

Magician Da Bei encouraged, “Think again. Is there a better approach?”

Magician Zhong Bei pondered deeply before finally saying, "I have an idea. Our target isn't the Dragon Lion Mercenary Corps, but the arms dealer behind them. The arms dealer came here solely to penetrate the Ice Sculpture Kingdom's arms market. We can leverage our connections within the Alchemy Guild to help them expand their market reach, using that leverage in negotiations."

Magician Da Bei shook his head again. "Now I'll give you some bad news. The Dragon Lion Mercenary Corps has already secured a commercial partnership with the Li Jian Clan. The dragon rider potions produced by Bu Quan are now being distributed throughout the Ice Sculpture Kingdom via the Li Jian Clan's commercial channels."

“Can our connections and influence possibly rival the Li Jian Clan's?”

Magician Zhong Bei paled and shook his head. The Li Jian Clan was a major noble clan within the Ice Sculpture Kingdom. Their clan's influence was vast, ranking either second or third in power.

Magician Zhong Bei felt like crying: “In that case, my mission is truly doomed to fail.”

“Not necessarily.” Magician Da Bei patted his son's shoulder, “We can't bribe the arms dealer backing the Dragon Lion Mercenary Corps, but destruction is always far easier than construction.”

Magician Zhong Bei's eyes lit up, “Father, are you suggesting we eliminate the Dragon Lion Mercenary Corps outright?”

Magician Da Bei tapped his son's head in exasperation. "What exactly is in your head? We're alchemists, combat isn't our forte. How much combat strength do we even have? You want us to take on a mercenary group battle-hardened through countless conflicts?"

Magician Zhong Bei retorted defiantly: “We could hire mercenaries, gangs, or other factions to do the dirty work for us!”

"How much money do you plan to spend? Even if we spent our entire fortune, could we guarantee the hired forces would eliminate the Dragon Lion Mercenary Corps? And if they were wiped out, how would Bu Quan view us? What would the arms dealers backing the Dragon Lion Mercenary Corps do? Do you truly believe we could keep the secret of paying others to do our dirty work?"

Magician Zhong Bei fell silent once more.

At a loss for solutions, he spread his hands in resignation. "Father, just tell me what you propose. I'll follow your lead."

Magician Da Bei was so furious he wanted to slap him again.

Magician Zhong Bei failed to grasp his father's earnest intentions. Lacking his father's alchemy prowess, he would forever be confined to the silver level. Achieving his father's standing within the Alchemy Guild was virtually impossible.

Deprived of alchemy talent and lacking gold level potential, Magician Da Bei could only cultivate his son's political acumen. Yet the son proved unfit for the task, choosing instead to follow instructions.

“What can I do? You're my only son,” Magician Da Bei sighed, finally revealing his plan.

“We're going to cause destruction. The goal is to make the Dragon Lion Mercenary Corps recognize our destructive power. Use conflict to force peace, compelling them to voluntarily abandon Bu Quan.”

“They're at a critical juncture right now.”

"You saw it yourself just now, Bu Quan and her crew are frantically mass-producing potions. I have to admit; the Dragon Lion Mercenary Corps has some talented individuals. They aim to break into the market with mount potions and establish stable sales channels. Once they start distributing all kinds of alchemy military supplies nationwide, it'll be twice the result with half the effort.

Magician Zhong Bei was inspired: “If we just destroy their alchemy workshop...”

Before he could finish, Magician Da Bei delivered another sharp blow to his head.

"Don't openly antagonize them! Don't provoke Bu Quan's hostility. If you act so directly, even if Bu Quan returns to the Alchemy Guild, she'll become our enemy. By the time she rises to the guild's leadership, I'll be old, retired, or even dead. But you? You'll have a very hard life."

“Now, shut your mouth and listen to me properly.”

Magician Da Bei, worn down by his son's torment, lost patience and laid out his plan: “We can sell counterfeit potions!”

“The Dragon Lion Mercenary Corps aims to use dragon rider potions to break into the market, filling the gap for mount potions across the kingdom.”

"We can produce potions that closely mimic the dragon rider potion, but with weak efficacy and severe side effects, potions that could even harm the mounts themselves. We needn’t brew large quantities. Just a fraction entering the market will utterly ruin their reputation, preventing them from achieving this crucial step."

“During this process, we can initiate negotiations.”

“Naturally, we won't reveal to the Dragon Lion Mercenary Corps that we're the masterminds behind the counterfeit potions.”

"I will step forward, using my authority as an elder, to help the Dragon Lion Mercenary Corps clarify the truth and prove their innocence. The condition for this assistance? Bu Quan must return to the guild!"

"Their time is limited; they need my help. Otherwise, if this drags on, other nobles or the Alchemy Guild will develop mount potions, seize the market, and ruin their business plans."

"Brilliant! This plan is truly brilliant! Producing counterfeit potions requires minimal cost from us, and crucially, it plays to our strengths. There's no need to risk combat!" exclaimed Magician Zhong Bei enthusiastically.

Magician Da Bei shook his head: “Executing this won't be easy. We don't possess the exact formula for the dragon rider potion, making it difficult to craft convincing counterfeits.”

“Should we steal it?” asked Magician Zhong Bei.

“No.” Magician Da Bei shook his head. "I have a better option. We buy several dragon rider potions and pay a visit to Master Cai Jing."

“Father, you mean the forger, Master Cai Jing?”

“That's right. Creating counterfeit potions ourselves would be quite difficult in such a short time. But if Master Cai Jing takes it on, success is assured. Ever since he was expelled from the Alchemy Guild, he's been living on Flower Skirt Island."

“Given the friendship between us, I'll just swallow my pride and ask him for help this time. He will assist us.”

Chapter 625: Lan Zao – I have a Fish Bladder Inside Me

Flower Skirt Island.

In the underground chamber of Shuang Tu's clinic.

Lan Zao lay suspended in midair, encased within a three-dimensional magic formation.

Beams of light emanating from the formation continuously scanned his entire body.

After a moment, the formation slowly ceased its scanning.

Lan Zao's suspended height steadily lowered until he touched the floor.

Shuang Tu's voice, controlling the magic formation, came through the chamber door: “All done, Hen Fa. You may come out now.”

Lan Zao stood up, pushed open the chamber door, and stepped out.

He entered an underground hall.

Bright lights illuminated the space, filled with numerous alchemy components. Lan Zao recognized none of them, but he could discern the magic aura emanating from these alchemy components. The vast majority were silver level, with two or three being gold level.

The magic formation in the chamber had been controlled by a gold level alchemy component.

Shuang Tu stood before that very component, manipulating the conjured magic interface.

“100%100%”

Spotting Lan Zao approach, he greeted him with evident delight. "Good news, your bloodline has fully stabilized. Your awakening was remarkably successful. You now possess 100% Fish Bladder bloodline within you. This is the best possible outcome for bloodline awakening; not all awakenings achieve 100% purity."

Lan Zao nodded, then his gaze was drawn to the gold level alchemy component before him.

Its metallic surface was smooth and flawless, like a giant mirror.

Lan Zao saw his current appearance reflected in it.

The most striking feature was his entirely blue skin.

To be precise, it was pale blue.

His complexion was no longer that of a human.

“At least I didn't grow fish scales,” Lan Zao consoled himself.

His physique had also changed.

First, he had shrunk in height. From his previous stature, he now stood at just 1.7 meters, yet he had become significantly more muscular.

His upper body formed an inverted triangle. His shoulders broadened considerably, while his waist and abdomen showcased eight sharply defined abdominal muscles. His arms were thick and resilient.

Lan Zao's upper body was significantly longer than before his awakening. Due to the compression of his height, his lower body had become shorter.

Yet both his thighs and calves were bulging with muscle. One glance revealed the immense power stored within his legs.

His palms and soles had broadened significantly, with webbing-like membranes growing between his fingers and toes.

These membranes didn't fully connect to the fingertips, reaching only to the middle of each finger at most.

His head shape had also changed. What was once a normal human skull had become narrower at the top and wider at the bottom, forming a shape close to an inverted triangle. His jaw was at least twice as broad as before, and the top of his head had a slight peak.

His hair had become extremely sparse, yet far denser than before.

Normal human hair consists of fine, soft strands. But Lan Zao's hair was now a deep blue, long and thick like noodles or seaweed, curling and coiling in its natural state.

Lan Zao's eyes had also grown larger. Nearly twice their previous size, their pupils were a deep, abyssal black.

Three “black lines” now ran along each cheek.

These “lines” could unfold, for they were actually gills.

Lan Zao had already experimented with this new organ - he could now breathe through his gills.

Staring at his reflection on the metal surface, Lan Zao's gaze grew complex.

While lying in the hospital bed, he had already noticed these changes occurring in his body.

At first, he found it difficult to accept.

But with Shuang Tu's encouragement, he quickly adjusted his mindset. After all, the awakening of bloodline was a blessing others could only dream of.

“100%”

Noticing Lan Zao staring intently at his reflection, Shuang Tu chuckled softly. "Your Fish Bladder bloodline has reached 100% concentration. Therefore, after awakening, all characteristics tied to the Fish Bladder bloodline have been permanently unlocked."

"Hen Fa, you're incredibly fortunate. After awakening, your life is utterly different from before. You've won a new life!"

In their daily conversations, Shuang Tu always addressed him as “Hen Fa,” aiming to help him quickly grow accustomed to the name and heighten his sensitivity to it.

“Come on, stand over there. Next, we'll teleport away from Flower Skirt Island.”

Shuang Tu led Lan Zao to another alchemy component.

This alchemy component was also gold level.

Upon activation, a beam of light instantly shot down from the hall's ceiling.

Following Shuang Tu's instructions, Lan Zao positioned himself beneath the beam.

The teleportation initiated swiftly. He vanished from his original spot, reappearing in a narrow, dimly lit chamber.

This chamber was considerably smaller than the one where he'd undergone inspection earlier, barely spacious enough for two or three people.

Five or six seconds later, another beam of light shot down from above Lan Zao's head.

Lan Zao hurriedly retreated to a corner of the room.

The next second, Shuang Tu's figure appeared within the beam.

The beam vanished swiftly.

“Where is this?” Lan Zao asked Shuang Tu, who had teleported in.

Shuang Tu replied simply, "It's an uninhabited deserted island near Flower Skirt Island. We carved out a secret chamber beneath the surface of this island and installed part of the alchemy components for teleportation."

“This is one of our escape routes. If anything goes wrong, we can immediately evacuate from Flower Skirt Island.”

"Alright, now we'll begin training. Since this is your first formal training session, the content will be relatively simple, just getting you as familiar as possible with your own body."

Shuang Tu looked at Lan Zao and inquired, "The awakening of your bloodline has transformed your body, granting you new organs. You should be able to sense it, right?"

Lan Zao nodded, “That's right. I can now breathe through my gills.”

“But it doesn't feel very good.”

"I've tried it a couple times. After just one breath through my gills, my entire jaw aches like I'm caught in a desert storm, sand pouring into my mouth."

Shuang Tu nodded slightly. "The Fish Bladder bloodline is related to the fishman. Hen Fa, it's highly likely one of your ancestors was a fishman."

“And such gills, that life-sustaining organ, are typically designed for underwater respiration. They aren't suited for breathing on land, or directly in air.”

"However, the Fish Bladder bloodline didn't just grant you gills. It also gave you a fish bladder! The latter is the most defining feature of this bloodline."

“A fish bladder?” Lan Zao asked in surprise.

He knew what a fish bladder was, of course.

Lan Zao shook his head. "Where is this fish bladder? Why can't I sense it?"

“I've checked. It's correct. Your newly formed fish bladder organ is located beneath your lungs,” Shuang Tu stated.

"It's perfectly normal that you haven't sensed it yet. Your current state is like an infant born with disabilities—missing arms or legs. It's only after reaching adulthood and receiving treatment that the limbs grow back, yet you won't know how to use them."

“Next, I'll open the valve and push this door. We'll immediately enter the sea.”

“When you continuously breathe through your gills in seawater, you'll be able to sense the fish bladder within your body.”

After speaking, Shuang Tu cast a spell upon himself.

Spell - Air Bubble.

A relatively dense sphere of air formed, enveloping Shuang Tu's entire body.

He then approached the chamber's door. Near the chamber entrance, he pried open several bricks, revealing a bronze handle beneath.

As Shuang Tu turned the bronze handle, Lan Zao immediately heard the clanking and grinding of chains and mechanisms shifting.

After a series of clattering and creaking noises, seawater suddenly surged in through the four corners of the chamber's floor tiles.

The water level rose steadily. Within about thirty seconds, it reached Lan Zao's chin. Another thirty-odd seconds passed before the chamber was completely submerged.

As Shuang Tu completed the final turn of the bronze handle, the chamber door swung open of its own accord.

The two swam out into the open sea.

Lan Zao scanned his surroundings.

His newly awakened black pupils captured the undersea light more effectively than before his bloodline awakening, allowing him to see clearly.

He discovered, just as Shuang Tu had said, that this chamber was a modified underwater foundation of a deserted island.

It was a crude chamber carved out beneath the island's surface.

“Now, breathe continuously through your gills.” Shuang Tu approached from behind Lan Zao.

Thanks to the Air Bubble Spell, he wasn't submerged in water. Instead, a sphere of air enveloped him like a protective dome.

This air bubble not only shielded him from the seawater pressure but also supplied breathable air.

Of course, with Shuang Tu's continuous breathing, the air bubble gradually shrank.

Lan Zao had only briefly tried breathing through his gills in the infirmary ward before stopping due to the pain. But this time was different. He abandoned his mouth and nose, persistently using his gills instead.

Breathing underwater felt entirely different from breathing in the hospital room.

Cold seawater poured in through the gills and was expelled, while filtered air flowed through the trachea to fill the newly formed fish bladder within Lan Zao.

The fish bladder steadily expanded.

Air continuously filled it, causing Lan Zao's chest to gradually swell.

Lan Zao felt a buoyant force, growing stronger, urging him toward the surface.

“You should now sense the air bladder within you,” Shuang Tu spoke.

Lan Zao nodded immediately. Perhaps earlier, with insufficient air, the air bladder remained minimally inflated, making it hard to perceive.

But now, as it expanded, the sensation became distinctly clear.

Shuang Tu continued, “Now, try compressing your fish bladder to squeeze the air out.”

Lan Zao attempted it. The next moment, an intense urge to vomit surged through him.

His Adam's apple rolled involuntarily. His mouth snapped open wide, expelling a massive bubble. As he reflexively closed his lips, a string of smaller bubbles followed.

Gurgle... gurgle... gurgle...

As the tiny bubbles escaped, a torrent of seawater rushed back into his mouth.

Lan Zao quickly clamped his mouth shut, but in his flustered state, he forgot to breathe through his gills. Instead, he instinctively used his mouth and nose, immediately choking on water and becoming even more flustered.

Seeing this, Shuang Tu swiftly cast the Air Bubble Spell.

Enveloped in a sphere of air, Lan Zao coughed violently for a long while before finally catching his breath.

“My bloodline has awakened. My body has changed drastically.”

"I must undergo extensive training to master these changes. Otherwise, this new body will become a massive vulnerability."

Lan Zao fully grasped his situation.

However, just as he was about to resume training, Shuang Tu's expression shifted.

“Bing Xiao is approaching. He must have learned you've awakened.”

"Hen Fa, your first trial has arrived. Do you remember what you should do?"

Lan Zao nodded and replied, “If Bing Xiao attempts to recruit me, I will refuse him outright.”

Chapter 626: Investigating “Hen Fa”

Before Bing Xiao arrived, Lan Zao and Shuang Tu had successfully returned to the clinic ahead of schedule.

Lan Zao had barely lain down on the hospital bed when he heard footsteps approaching.

Soon, the door swung open.

Shuang Tu entered first, followed by Bing Xiao, with the human ringleader trailing behind.

Lan Zao hurriedly sat up, his heart pounding, his face stiff.

“Don't be nervous, young man. Lie back down,” Bing Xiao waved his hand, smiling warmly and gently.

“Gang Leader Bing Xiao,” Lan Zao spoke, his voice dry.

Bing Xiao approached the bedside: "No need to be so formal, young man. I should be thanking you for saving my life."

But Lan Zao shook his head. "I heard about it. You saved me too. Back on the ship, when I was still unconscious."

As he spoke, Lan Zao instinctively glanced at the human ringleader.

He didn't know the human ringleader was actually part of his own faction, but he had indeed learned this information from the latter.

The human ringleader's ward was separated from his by just a single wall.

For the sake of Tranquil Hill's mission, though the human ringleader didn't wish to reveal his true identity to Lan Zao, maintaining contact with him was beneficial.

Thus, the human ringleader often visited, chatting about everything under the sun with Lan Zao while probing into his background.

It was during these visits that Lan Zao shared the story behind his name, “Hen Fa,” and its origins.

Lan Zao exchanged a few words with Bing Xiao.

For the most part, Bing Xiao offered condolences while Lan Zao responded.

Finally, Lan Zao suddenly said, "Lord Bing Xiao, thank you for bringing me here to recover. I will repay you for the medical expenses."

Hearing this, and noticing Lan Zao's stiff expression, Bing Xiao recalled what the human ringleader had previously told him. He instantly understood Lan Zao's intentions.

By insisting on repaying the medical fees, Lan Zao sought to sever ties with him, Bing Xiao, and the Bayonet Gang.

Bing Xiao's expression remained unchanged, maintaining his gentle demeanor as he controlled the pace of the conversation.

He was already quite aged, having stepped down from his position as leader of the Bayonet Gang. Now, he found himself forced back into a position of authority—an extraordinary twist in his life's journey.

The human ringleader standing behind him watched Bing Xiao's back, his gaze flickering slightly.

In some ways, Bing Xiao was even more difficult to deal with than Teng Dong Lang. The vigorous Teng Dong Lang disliked concealing his intentions, whereas Bing Xiao was an old fox, outsiders could never discern his inner thoughts from his words, actions, or expressions.

Lan Zao remained taciturn, his expression rigid throughout.

Bing Xiao, however, seemed to have endless topics to discuss.

Finally, he uttered the question: “So, young man, are you interested in working under me?”

Lan Zao shook his head immediately, inwardly sighing with relief. Finally, the question was asked. This conversation had been agonizing.

He hastily apologized, explaining his intentions: Saving Bing Xiao earlier had been a coincidence, and he'd also hoped to use the Bayonet Gang's influence to escape Snow Bird Harbor. Now that he'd arrived at Flower Skirt Island and his bloodline had awakened, everything could start anew. He wanted to forge his own path!

Hearing this, Bing Xiao remained smiling, but the human ringleader snorted coldly, rebuking Lan Zao for being ungrateful.

Bing Xiao stopped the human ringleader's scolding, offered a few words of reassurance to Lan Zao, and as he left, tossed out a parting remark: Lan Zao needn't repay the medical costs, it was all fate.

After leaving Lan Zao's hospital room, the human ringleader escorted Bing Xiao downstairs.

“Chief, that kid Hen Fa really doesn't know a good thing when he sees it!” the human ringleader was still fuming.

Bing Xiao shook his head and chuckled softly, "It's understandable. He's young, and with his bloodline awakening, he wants to carve out his own path. Having that kind of spirit is a good thing. That's precisely why I see potential in him."

At this, Bing Xiao sighed, “It's a pity we can barely protect ourselves now. Back in the heyday of the Bayonet Gang, I wouldn't have needed to recruit him, Hen Fa would have offered to join on his own.”

The human ringleader hurriedly reassured Bing Xiao, insisting the Bayonet Gang would surely regain its former glory.

As they reached the first-floor lobby, the human ringleader added, "Gang Leader, that Hen Fa fellow truly is promising talent. I heard Doctor Shuang Tu mention the examination results, his bloodline is completely purified, 100% pure concentration."

“He's a guaranteed silver level, and we rescued him. Are we just going to let him go?”

Bayonet Gang shot the human ringleader a glance. "The Bayonet Gang is rebuilding from scratch. Hen Fa is a talent worth recruiting. But now isn't the right time. Let's get to know him first. Over the next few days, keep building rapport with him, maintain the relationship, and find out more about him."

The human ringleader hastily replied, “Understood.”

Yet he casually pressed on, "But our treatment will eventually end. Once I'm fully recovered, I'll have no reason to stay here. And it's not just me, Hen Fa faces the same situation. Though he's only bronze level now, the island's factions will surely covet a future silver level talent."

Bing Xiao nodded, keeping his next plans to himself. He only reminded the human ringleader that his primary focus remained healing, recruiting Hen Fa was merely a secondary task.

The human ringleader immediately voiced his concerns about the Bayonet Gang's future, his inner urgency palpable. He pledged that upon returning, he would thoroughly discuss the gang's foundations and bright prospects with Hen Fa.

“That's enough. No need to see me off.” Bing Xiao stood at the clinic's entrance, waving the human ringleader away.

Spring Boxer, leaning against the wall beside the door, now approached to escort Bing Xiao.

Though Bing Xiao was also silver level, age had taken its toll. His true strength lay in his gold level giant slave. Unfortunately, his sole gold level giant slave had been beheaded by Snow Bird Harbor City Lord.

Flower Skirt Island presented yet another unfamiliar environment.

Thus, when Bing Xiao ventured out, he was typically escorted by either Spring Boxer or Xiong Ju.

Spring Boxer exchanged greetings with the human ringleader before gradually departing the clinic grounds with Bing Xiao.

Along the way, Spring Boxer remarked, "I see the human ringleader's injuries have healed considerably. He should be discharged now. For daily gang affairs, he's the most suitable person to handle them. He's also the most adept at managing these minor matters."

Bing Xiao smiled faintly, “What's this? You've only been handling gang affairs for a few days, and you're already growing impatient?”

“Gang affairs may seem trivial, but they're actually crucial.”

"I know your past ways. Though you were a ringleader, you'd almost always delegate your responsibilities to your deputy. But that won't do now. The gang needs you to handle these matters."

Bing Xiao stopped walking, turning to look at Spring Boxer with solemn earnestness, “I'm getting old.”

“The next leader of the Bayonet Gang will be you.”

“If you can't even handle gang affairs, how can you shoulder such a heavy responsibility?”

Spring Boxer gasped, his face clouding with distress.

He'd never shown interest in this position.

Bing Xiao knew his temperament well and pressed on, "You also know there might be a mole among us now. Do you want the leader's position to fall into the hands of a traitor?"

“Of course not!” Spring Boxer blurted out, “But...”

"In my view, Xiong Ju isn't the mole. He's a formidable fighter, but his character isn't suited to treachery."

“And the human ringleader, he's always been loyal to the gang.”

Bing Xiao shook his head and continued walking forward.

Spring Boxer hurried to catch up, hearing Bing Xiao say, “If we can't confirm the traitor, we must maintain cautious suspicion toward everyone else.”

“Xiong Ju, the human ringleader, either could be it. Even Hen Fa is a possibility.”

“Hen Fa?” Spring Boxer was taken aback. He knew some information about Hen Fa, “He shouldn't be, right?”

“He's not one of us Bayonet Gang members.”

“He's not even from Snow Bird Harbor, he comes from Blood Kin City.”

“If I were the Snow Bird Harbor City Lord, I'd definitely plant former Bayonet Gang members among us.”

“Besides, Hen Fa never wanted to join us, did he?”

Leaning against the wall, Spring Boxer overheard the conversation between the human ringleader and Bing Xiao in the hall.

“But we can't rule him out as a suspect,” Bing Xiao shook his head slightly, sighing again, “Fortunately, we can investigate him.”

“Investigate?” Spring Boxer was puzzled.

Bing Xiao explained, "He fled from Blood Kin City to Snow Bird Harbor. We happen to have people inside Blood Kin City."

“Does our Bayonet Gang still have a surviving branch?” Spring Boxer grew more curious.

Bing Xiao shook his head, “To be precise, it's connections, through Teng Dong Lang.”

“You actually know this person.”

“Who?” Spring Boxer pressed urgently.

Bing Xiao uttered a name.

Spring Boxer gasped. "What? The bald uncle? He's now the Axe Gang's leader?"

Blood Kin City and Snow Bird Harbor were close in proximity, and Spring Boxer knew the Axe Gang was the largest syndicate in Blood Kin City.

But he never imagined the Axe Gang leader would be the bald uncle from his memories.

That was Teng Dong Lang's comrade-in-arms. Though the two usually couldn't stand each other, each finding the other unbearable, when it came to combat, they'd risk their lives to save one another, trusting each other implicitly with their backs. Their bond as comrades-in-arms ran deep.

“Didn't the bald uncle go back to farming?”

Bing Xiao sighed. "Veterans like us can't return to ordinary lives. On the battlefield, the roar of explosions lulled us to sleep. Lying in a soft bed, quietly passing a peaceful day, that’s our nightmare. The habits of combat are etched into our very bones. We've grown accustomed to relying on force to survive. Like Teng Dong Lang, he's also gold level. His temper is even more volatile than Teng Dong Lang's—he simply can't endure a life bound by rules. His current situation is actually quite natural."

Spring Boxer blinked, nodding repeatedly in agreement with Bing Xiao's words.

Bing Xiao: “Next, I'll send a message requesting the Axe Gang leader to investigate Hen Fa's background.”

“Flower Skirt Island has numerous communication beasts.”

“If all goes well, we should receive a reply within days.”

Blood Kin City.

Zi Di, bearing a heavy gift, arrived at the Axe Gang's headquarters alongside Zong Ge.

After explaining the purpose of their visit, they were ushered inside.

“Long Fu... He actually came looking for us himself!” The Axe Gang leader's face darkened.

"Father, we failed to eliminate him in Cold Wind Valley. Now we can activate the base's magic formation and try again!" The Axe Gang leader's son, Fu Zhao, seethed with murderous intent.

Chapter 627: The Guest Acts as the Host

Upon hearing his son's suggestion to use the magic formation within their stronghold against Long Fu's group, the Axe Gang leader didn't hesitate before shaking his head.

“The magic formation in our stronghold is indeed powerful and could increase our chances of victory.”

“But we cannot do this!”

“If we kill both Long Fu and Magician Yao Ma within the compound - that would be the best outcome, but even then, it would be troublesome.”

"The Dragon Lion Mercenary Corps is renowned throughout the kingdom, and Long Fu is at the height of his fame. To openly murder them within the city would cause extremely negative repercussions. We are no longer soldiers, merely a local gang, this would be a crime! Even if the Dragon Lion Mercenary Corps isn't native to the kingdom."

“Then, even if the Axe Gang survives, we will have to flee for our lives.”

"There's also the possibility we fail to kill Long Fu and let him escape. Don't forget, they're no ordinary mercenaries, they're backed by transnational arms dealers. During his battle with Teng Dong Lang, Long Fu used a gold level magic scroll. Do you think he might have more scrolls of that caliber?"

"Our base's magic formation is powerful, but it doesn't suppress magic. If he uses a battlefield-class spell scroll, our entire base will be destroyed."

"There's another concern: if the fight becomes deadlocked and Long Fu drags out the battle, what reaction might the acting city lord have?"

“Therefore, to deal with them, we must disguise ourselves and ambush them.”

"Last time in Cold Wind Valley, I underestimated him. I truly didn't expect Long Fu's strength to be so formidable, far surpassing what he displayed in his heroic duel with Teng Dong Lang. That's why I didn't use my full strength or deploy the Blood Vengeance Axe."

"Once they leave Snow Fall City, I'll strike again. This time, I won't just give it my all, I'll also invite allies of equal level. Even if Long Fu can fight above his rank as a silver level against gold level opponents, I don't believe he can withstand two gold levels working together. Two gold level experts might still be risky; there's a chance he could escape. That's why I plan to recruit two gold level reinforcements simultaneously."

“With three gold level experts united, I don't believe a single silver level has any chance!”

Upon hearing his father's plan, Fu Zhao's mouth fell open slightly, his face frozen in utter astonishment.

He hadn't anticipated the Axe Gang leader's ambitions reaching such heights this time.

Recruiting a gold level expert for battle would demand a hefty price. This time, the Axe Gang leader intended to recruit two simultaneously.

All to deal with a single silver level?

Even with the Axe Gang controlling most of Snow Fall City's power, recruiting two gold level experts would drain nearly their entire fortune.

This was no exaggeration.

For an ambush like the one on Long Fu, exposure was unthinkable and extremely risky.

Should Long Fu survive and leak the news, the Axe Gang would face devastating repercussions. Take the Axe Gang leader himself. Even if the gang survived, he would certainly lose his position.

This was essentially staking his entire future on the outcome of the ambush against Long Fu.

The Axe Gang leader had his reasons for doing this. But persuading two gold level helpers to stake their own futures on this? That was far more difficult.

He had to offer a massive sum of money, incurring enormous costs, just to make them consider it.

"Father, is this really necessary? Hiring two gold levels help at the same time, three including you, just to ambush a silver level?"

The Axe Gang leader shot his son a glance and spoke frankly: "If I had more liquid funds, I'd even recruit a third. With the enemy's strength unknown, victory demands maximizing our odds. Never underestimate any foe. To guarantee success, you must pay the price. I'd wager my entire fortune without hesitation."

“You must understand the trouble we'll face if our identities are exposed!”

The depth of the Axe Gang leader's camaraderie with Teng Dong Lang mirrored his hatred for Long Fu.

"However, since Long Fu and Yao Ma have chosen to visit me today, this presents an opportunity we cannot pass up. Go challenge him later to probe his capabilities and trump cards." The Axe Gang leader instructed Fu Zhao.

Fu Zhao immediately looked troubled: “Father, though I'm also silver level like Long Fu, you know well that silver level cultivators vary greatly in strength.”

The Axe Gang leader patted Fu Zhao on the shoulder: “Of course I know. That's why for this battle, I'll allow you to wield the Blood Vengeance Axe!”

Fu Zhao's eyes lit up instantly, his expression brimming with excitement: “With the Blood Vengeance Axe, I might just stand a chance against Long Fu.”

Moments later.

Gang members ushered Zong Ge and Zi Di into the reception hall. Here, the two met the Axe Gang leader.

"Long Fu, I've long admired your deeds. I am the Axe Gang leader." The Axe Gang leader greeted them warmly.

“I am Long Fu. This is our corps’ Magician Yao Ma,” Zong Ge introduced briefly while sizing up the man before him.

Clearly, the Axe Gang leader was a snow elf. But unlike typical elves, he was exceptionally stocky and towering. Zong Ge was already quite tall, yet the Axe Gang leader stood a full head taller.

The Axe Gang leader was aged, his chin sporting freshly shaved white stubble. The stubble extended outward, reaching the base of his earlobe.

Though his face bore a smile and his demeanor was warm, the sharp gleam that flashed unconsciously in his eyes, along with his shaven head, conveyed his formidable strength.

Intelligence on the Axe Gang leader instantly replayed in Zong Ge's mind.

According to the reports, the leader's ancestry was traced back to an Iceborne Troll.

His bloodline traits closely resembled those of a troll. He possessed the Blood War bloodline. Though its concentration was low, it still granted him extraordinary regenerative abilities.

This extraordinary regenerative ability proved highly practical, greatly enhancing his recovery capabilities. Many injuries could heal completely on their own given enough time, even without healing from a priest.

After exchanging a few pleasantries, Zong Ge stated directly, “Honorable Axe Gang leader, the purpose of our visit is to propose a partnership with your gang.”

The Axe Gang leader inquired with curiosity, "Oh? How might we collaborate?"

Zong Ge shifted slightly, turning his head to signal Zi Di beside him with a glance.

Zi Di stepped forward, bowed respectfully to the Axe Gang leader, then began speaking eloquently.

First, she expressed gratitude for the Axe Gang becoming the largest purchaser of the dragon rider potion. Therefore, Zi Di proactively offered that for any dragon rider potions purchased by the Axe Gang at her potion shops over the next three years, they would receive a 10% discount.

Secondly, Zi Di wished to recruit several members from the Axe Gang. After brief training, these individuals would become shop assistants at her medicine shops.

Finally, Zi Di proposed a three-year escort contract with the Axe Gang. When merchant caravans departed Snow Bird Harbor and approached Blood Kin City, the Axe Gang would provide security.

After listening carefully, the Axe Gang leader broke into a smile, “So you're the ones producing the dragon rider potion.”

"Your group's capabilities are truly impressive. I hadn't realized you had this capability. I must say, the dragon rider potion is remarkably effective."

"I am, of course, very open to such a partnership. In fact, the entire Axe Gang welcomes it. However, the specific details require careful consideration."

Following this, the Axe Gang leader and Zi Di proceeded to negotiate the specifics of the collaboration.

What surprised Zong Ge and Zi Di slightly was that the Axe Gang leader proved far more articulate than they had anticipated.

Though outwardly rough-hewn, he possessed a shrewd side, often spotting crucial points within the partnership terms.

Moreover, he possessed a formidable personality. Once he latched onto certain terms, he wouldn't budge, not even an inch.

"I'm a gangster, not a merchant. I don't haggle. What I insist on is my bottom line!" He frequently used this line to pressure Zi Di into concessions.

However, Zi Di didn't find herself at a disadvantage.

Her business acumen was remarkable, and she possessed extensive negotiation experience, having faced opponents like this before.

Whenever the Axe Gang leader dug his heels in on certain terms, Zi Di would feign concession. She would then find ways to modify other clauses or introduce new conditions, deliberately framing these terms in complex language.

How could the Axe Gang leader possibly grasp these new clauses as well as their creator, Zi Di?

After all, he was a retired soldier, no match for someone like Zi Di, born into a merchant family.

He needed more time to grasp them, and soon his attention became fully absorbed, his brow furrowing deeper and deeper.

He was forced to ask Zi Di direct questions to help him understand these new clauses. But this was precisely what Zi Di wanted to see. Under her verbal tactics, the Axe Gang leader gradually lost his negotiating leverage.

Zong Ge might lack diplomatic skills, but he possessed keen battlefield intuition. After listening for a while, he gradually realized: the Axe Gang leader resembled the defender, while Zi Di was the attacker.

Earlier, the defender had stubbornly held the line, making it difficult for the attacker to achieve any gains. But soon, the attacker feigned retreat to create space on the battlefield. She then continuously split her forces into left and right wings, drawing the defender's attention. The defender's lines were severely disrupted, allowing the main attacking force to advance layer by layer. The defender had already lost ground without even realizing it!

“That's impressive.” Fu Zhao, hiding behind the scenes, shared the same observation as Zong Ge.

As an observer, Fu Zhao had been eavesdropping on his father's conversation with Long Fu.

The Axe Gang leader was already thoroughly confused. Fu Zhao couldn't bear to listen any longer. He stormed into the reception hall, shouting impatiently, “Father! I heard Commander Long Fu has arrived!”

The Axe Gang leader immediately glared at his son, “What's with all the shouting? What do you want?”

After scolding him, he finally snapped to attention, glancing at Zi Di with lingering fear.

“To find him, of course!” Fu Zhao's face was twisted with impatience. His burning gaze locked onto Zong Ge. "You're Commander Long Fu! I've seen your battle footage, it's incredible. Come on, let's have a heroic duel!"

The Axe Gang leader's face darkened as he scolded, "Scoundrel! Commander Long Fu and Magician Yao Ma are guests of our Axe Gang. Get out of here, you brat."

But Fu Zhao didn't even glance at his own father. He waved him off dismissively: “Don't stop my heroic duel! Not even you, my own father, can stop me!”

“You brat...” The Axe Gang leader stood up, clearly furious.

He was about to continue his scolding, then put on a show with Fu Zhao, ultimately resigning himself to it. But the next moment, he heard Zong Ge say: “I accept your challenge.”

Father and son froze, both turning to stare at Zong Ge.

Zong Ge added, “Because I've been wanting to challenge the leader myself.”

The Axe Gang duo looked utterly stunned.

The ghost girl was equally bewildered, inwardly groaning: "Again?"

This wasn't part of their plan before arriving.

It was Zong Ge's own initiative.
