Earlier, in the Cold Wind Valley, he'd acted similarly, setting off without hesitation, never wasting words.

This time, accompanying Zi Di to visit the Axe Gang was also a spur-of-the-moment decision, driven by his desire to challenge the Axe Gang leader, a gold level expert.

According to the customs of the Ice Sculpture Kingdom, a heroic duel was nearly impossible for outsiders to intervene in. Such a challenge was sacred and inviolable.

As long as both parties consented, not even a father could stop it.

However, Zong Ge's acceptance came with conditions.

He would accept Fu Zhao's challenge, but only on the premise that the Axe Gang leader would also accept his!

Chapter 628: Changes to the Divine Artifact

The Axe Gang leader felt troubled.

He wanted to use Fu Zhao to probe Zong Ge's true intentions—a sound plan, but he never imagined facing "Long Fu" head-on just yet.

“Does he suspect I'm the one behind the attack in Cold Wind Valley?” the Axe Gang leader couldn't help but wonder inwardly.

Guilty conscience.

The Axe Gang leader didn't want to fight Zong Ge, for fear of exposing himself.

Even holding back, he'd already clashed with Zong Ge and confirmed firsthand that this man was no ordinary silver level fighter. Such an opponent demanded his full strength!

The Axe Gang leader also intended to give his all. He had resolved to spare no expense in recruiting two allies of equal strength to properly kill “Long Fu.”

Small impatience ruined great plans.

Having decided to eliminate Long Fu, the Axe Gang leader naturally wished to avoid provoking trouble before the operation.

He sternly reprimanded Fu Zhao, even locking his son in confinement on the spot!

In the exchange of glances with the Axe Gang leader, Fu Zhao sensed his father had changed his mind. He had no choice but to play along, feigning deep resentment while accepting the punishment, then left the scene.

The Axe Gang leader repeatedly apologized, admitting his lax discipline of his son and stressing his desire to avoid conflict with business partners. Most crucially, he acknowledged the Dragon Lion Mercenary Corps' strength, especially Long Fu's combat prowess, stating they were equal partners.

“Since we're equals, then please, Gang Leader, don't cling rigidly to certain clauses. Recognize our sincerity in seeking cooperation.” Zong Ge's final remark stunned the Axe Gang leader.

Even Zi Di was taken aback.

It was a stroke of genius!

After a moment of silence, the Axe Gang leader indeed shifted his negotiating stance, becoming far more accommodating. Many terms were swiftly agreed upon with Zi Di, and he even made concessions in certain areas.

Soon after, both parties smoothly signed the magic contract.

This signified the preliminary establishment of their cooperation. The moment the contract took effect, the atmosphere in the reception hall visibly grew much more harmonious.

The Axe Gang leader wished to host a banquet in honor of Zong Ge and Zi Di.

However, Zong Ge and Zi Di did not wish to waste any more time here and took their leave.

The Axe Gang leader personally escorted them out, watching until their carriage disappeared around the street corner before turning back toward the gang headquarters.

Upon returning to the reception hall, Fu Zhao had already emerged.

The future Axe Gang leader looked uneasy. "Father. Long Fu suddenly challenged you. Could it be that he recognized you as the one who attacked him?"

The Axe Gang leader shook his head. "Even if he suspects something, he has no proof. That's not the point."

Fu Zhao pressed further, “Did signing a magic contract with them interfere with your plan to kill Long Fu later?”

“There's interference, but minimal,” the Axe Gang leader replied. "I considered that, of course. There are no absolute guarantees in this world. Any magic contract can potentially be broken. Before I act, I will certainly break this contract. If I can't manage it, the two gold level individuals I invited will possess that ability."

“Moreover, when I signed the contract with them, I had already anticipated this. The terms were designed with great caution.”

Fu Zhao set aside his bold concerns; his expression tinged with regret. "It's truly a pity. To be honest, I'd have loved to challenge Long Fu and see this man's prowess for myself. If I can use the Blood Vengeance Axe, even in defeat, I’ll give him a lesson he'll never forget!"

The Axe Gang leader's face grew solemn. "I believe that. Your strength is formidable, but the Blood Vengeance Axe is the crucial factor. Its power isn't something Long Fu can withstand."

"But I must tell you this: dealing with an enemy isn't just about teaching them a lesson. The best way to deal with an enemy is to kill them outright!"

"Teng Dong Lang once said it best. What makes the best enemy? An enemy lying dead is the best."

At this point, the Axe Gang leader sighed heavily, his voice tinged with nostalgia. "What a pity... that fellow of yours... actually died. But rest assured, your enemy will soon join you."

Zong Ge and Zi Di departed the Axe Gang, seated in the carriage for their return journey.

Zi Di activated her alchemy device first, creating a temporary private space for their conversation. She then looked at Zong Ge with a complex expression: "I never expected such a big payoff this time. I have to say, you were a huge help."

Zong Ge glanced at her, his expression calm: “It's what I should do. Even though we have differences in how we see many things, we're still on the same team.”

This realization brought relief to Zi Di. Truth be told, Zong Ge's sudden, unprovoked challenge to the Axe Gang leader had genuinely startled her.

Her inner voice had screamed, “Zong Ge, don't forget you're only silver level! Do you really think you're the commander?”

But the reality was, Zong Ge's challenge had turned the tables. Not only had it resolved his own predicament, but it had also helped Zi Di.

Combined with the outcome in Cold Wind Valley, which exposed the ambushers' hostility, it proved Zong Ge's decision had been correct.

Zi Di's tone softened. "You're right. We're on the same side. Hearing you say that puts my mind at ease."

She pressed further, “How could you be so certain the Axe Gang leader would reject you?”

"Also, did you suspect the Axe Gang father and son? After all, the leader's primary weapon is the Blood Vengeance Axe. I recall you mentioning during reconnaissance in Cold Wind Valley that the gold level fighter held back his power. His true primary weapon wasn't a war hammer, but rather a blade or axe suited for slashing."

Yet Zong Ge shook his head, speaking plainly: “I never suspected them.”

"The father and son are gold level and silver level respectively, which matches the ambushers' levels. But isn't his son also a fighter?"

Zi Di shook her head. "He likely practices both magic and battle qi, and magic aura can be concealed. There are many methods for that. Especially without real combat, it's hard to detect any clues."

She was puzzled, “If you weren't suspicious, why challenge them to probe their strength?”

Zong Ge remained composed. "I intended to challenge the Axe Gang leader anyway. On one hand, I wanted to gauge my true gap against gold level opponents. Could I achieve what a team battle could accomplish? On the other hand, actively challenging gold level opponents aligns with my upcoming plans."

“What do you mean?” Zi Di sensed trouble.

“Exactly what it sounds like,” Zong Ge replied, “Rather than passively accepting challenges, I'd rather go out and challenge them myself.”

“Most silver level cultivators want to step on me to indirectly gain glory from defeating a stronger opponent.”

"Fighting these guys is such a pain, they hold nowhere near the combat value of gold level opponents! Once I actively challenge gold level opponents, they'll have no reason to bother me."

Zi Di listened, utterly dumbfounded.

Zong Ge concluded, "On the battlefield, you must seize the initiative by any means necessary. Taking the offensive is often an excellent tactical strategy."

Zi Di rolled her eyes inwardly.

She fully realized that Zong Ge's previous cooperation with her had been nothing but a fluke.

As for his plan to actively seek trouble? Zi Di was a million percent against it!

But she was powerless.

She knew she couldn't stop Zong Ge.

That guy just loved doing things his own way!

His past experiences had forged his habits. Like his true appearance, he was a lion, fierce and relentless.

Ironically, his choices often yielded favorable outcomes.

Zong Ge was stubbornly independent; Zi Di couldn't sway him.

Even with her keen insight, she sensed a faint disdain for her lurking beneath his words and expressions.

Military men often looked down on merchants.

"Sigh, Commander, where are you now? If only you were here." Zi Di began muttering about the dragonman youngster again.

Among all the survivors, only the dragonman youngster could keep Zong Ge in check.

The youngster was no longer a dragonman; he had become a fishman.

Bam!

Inside the landing pod of the Deep Sea Monster Fish, the fishman youngster slammed his head against the metal hull once more.

He had no choice but to pause his training.

“Bursting Explosion battle qi really isn't suited for the Swift Swim battle skill.”

It was the same old problem.

The optimal use of Bursting Explosion battle qi was full-power output, unleashing its explosive properties.

But when applied to the Swift Swim battle skill, it caused the fishman youngster's swimming speed to fluctuate wildly, making it extremely difficult to control.

The youngster floated effortlessly on the water's surface, extending a hand to press against his forehead.

The slightest touch intensified the stinging pain on his head.

That collision had delivered a significant blow.

Racial magic - Healing.

In the next instant, a flash of white light erupted as the fishman youngster successfully healed himself.

The throbbing pain above his head vanished completely.

The Surging Wave fishman bloodline he possessed granted him four racial magic spells. He was now most proficient in using Healing.

The use of Mental Flogging, Mind's Eye, and Affinity Aura was relatively infrequent.

After all, within the Ocean Mother Nest, only he, Zhong Yong, and the saint level sea beast remained living beings. (The tide snake eggs and Zi Di's corpse were not counted for now.)

Compared to these racial magics, the fishman youngster had mastered the fishman battle skills to perfection.

The initial awkwardness upon first using them had completely vanished.

Though he hadn't taken shortcuts like using the Grand Arena Medal for training, Zong Ge's eye for talent proved accurate, the fishman youngster truly possessed exceptional fighter potential.

Fishman battle skills were often straightforward, allowing him to grasp them quickly and master them with ease.

Not only that, but the fishman youngster also trained in the hybrid fishman form.

He had absorbed two bloodlines from Hai Ping's saint corpse. The first remained unknown to him, but the second, the Barbarian Fish bloodline, was unmistakably distinct.

When the youngster activated his Bloodcore and simultaneously unleashed both bloodlines, he entered the hybrid fishman form.

In this state, his features bore a certain resemblance to the saint corpse.

His training in this form stemmed from a desire to maximize his combat effectiveness underwater.

The concentration of the Surging Wave fishman bloodline remained relatively low. For now, the hybrid fishman form undoubtedly possessed greater combat prowess than the single fishman bloodline form.

Though the hybrid form also brought the drawback of accelerated Bloodcore magic depletion, the fishman youngster currently faced no shortage of Bloodcore magic.

The only regret was that the fishman youngster still couldn't properly utilize his Berserk talent.

More accurately, he couldn't avoid the drawback of losing his sanity when using it.

"I've already returned the Ice Heart Headband to the Snow Bird Harbor City Lord. The Cold Heart Ice Bag that Bu Quan gave me can keep me lucid, but it has specific trigger conditions and doesn't work at all with the Berserk talent."

The fishman youngster fretted over this, finding no immediate solution.

He ended today's training early, as the first practical deployment of the precision eel drillers was imminent.

True to his silver level Alchemy Master status, Zhong Yong far surpassed Cang Xu and Zi Di in alchemy mastery. After considerable effort, he successfully crafted the first batch of precision eel drillers.

These drillers were deployed in secret.

The fishman youngster and Zhong Yong entered the cockpit together, intently watching the precision eel drillers through the magic display.

These slender, microscopic alchemy automatons were only the size of an adult's finger. Released from the Deep Sea Monster Fish, they swiftly swam to the surface of the cave wall.

The wall was covered in pearls.

Selecting a relatively weak spot, the drillers commenced their work.

The spiral drills on their heads spun at full speed, generating considerable noise.

To the fishman youngster's relief, the slumbering saint level sea beast remained undisturbed.

Once the drillers penetrated the cave wall, their tails fully embedded, the drilling noise actually diminished.

Zhong Yong remarked, "I can now confirm the walls of the Ocean Mother Nest absorb sound waves. This explains why our dual-eared detectors failed to detect external intelligence earlier. Simultaneously, the walls significantly dampen the noise generated by the precision eel driller's operations."

“Success is within reach.”

The fishman youngster replied, “Let's hope so.”

Zhong Yong returned to the alchemy cabin to resume his work.

As the fishman youngster prepared to head back to the landing pod to restart his training, the tower spirit alerted him midway: "Captain, the divine artifact, Mermaid Fairy Tale, is behaving erratically. The cause is unpredictable. You should investigate immediately!"

The fishman youngster froze in shock.

What trouble was the Mermaid Fairy Tale divine artifact stirring up now?

He remembered clearly how it had exhibited suspicious disturbances when the Deep Sea Monster Fish had been inexplicably transported here before.

Chapter 629: Cang Xu – I am a One Man Army!

The fishman youngster was suddenly informed by the tower spirit that the divine artifact, Mermaid Fairy Tale, had undergone an unusual change.

The anxious youngster hurried to the cabin, only to discover this anomaly was actually a blessing.

“The amount of Pearl Bubbles increased!” The fishman youngster stood before the divine artifact, his eyes widening in astonishment, even though his fishman eyes were already quite large.

The Mermaid Fairy Tale was the divine artifact of Charming Blue Goddess. In appearance, it was a large, fan-shaped seashell.

Within the half-open shell lay a sea of iridescent pearl bubbles.

They were uniformly sized, completely transparent inside as if hollow, their outer layer a spherical foam. Yet these pearls could be grasped by hand and felt surprisingly hard.

The survivors had obtained this divine artifact from the control tower beneath Mystifying Monster Island.

They had repeatedly used Pearl Bubbles to construct the Deceptive Disguise Formation, harnessing the divine artifact's power more effectively.

The Deceptive Disguise spell provided immense assistance to the survivors.

Without this spell, without this artifact, the survivors would never have reached their current position. It could be said that the Dragon Lion Mercenary Corps owed its present scale largely to this divine artifact!

However, the Mermaid Fairy Tale produced Pearl Bubbles at an increasingly slower rate, and in ever-dwindling quantities.

When the survivors first obtained this divine artifact, the great shell was filled with layers upon layers of pearl bubbles. Now, only a thin layer remains at the bottom of the shell.

The yield of Pearl Bubbles has plummeted.

The survivors' consumption of this god level material grew ever more frequent, showing no signs of abating.

Zi Di, Cang Xu, and others expressed deep concern over this, repeatedly warning the other survivors.

The reason for the decline in pearl bubble production was simple, and all survivors understood it—the Mermaid Fairy Tale contained the mystical power of the Charming Blue Goddess. It was precisely the continuous depletion of this divine power that enabled the divine artifact to produce Pearl Bubbles.

However, the divine power was like water without a source; it diminished with every use. As the divine power dwindled, so did the yield of Pearl Bubbles.

This scarcity was one reason survivors urgently sought redemption from the Empire.

With the divine power of the Charming Blue Goddess unable to be replenished and Pearl Bubbles dwindling with each use, the day would soon come when it could no longer aid the survivors.

Without this shield, the survivors will be easily exposed to prophecies, divinations, or similar means. At that point, the immense wealth they possessed would compel the Sheng Ming Empire to act against them. The justification for such action would be noble and righteous, after all, they killed Father Jia Sha on the Mystifying Monster Island.

The dragonman youngster even secretly slew the Flame Dragon King!

Yet now, within the great shell before the fishman youngster, two layers of pearl bubbles have already formed.

“The Mermaid Fairy Tale is merely a divine artifact; it doesn't generate divine power on its own.”

“Divine power emanates from the deity itself.”

“The increased pearl bubble production indicates the divine power reserves within the Mermaid Fairy Tale have risen.”

“How is the divine power within the artifact replenished?”

The fishman youngster sank into deep thought.

His mind naturally turned to the Ocean Mother Nest.

"Previously, pearl bubble production had been steadily declining. The most significant difference now compared to before is that the Deep Sea Monster Fish is situated within an Ocean Mother Nest."

“Could it be... that there is Charming Blue divine power here?”

“Why would Charming Blue divine power be here?”

“Is it precisely because of the Charming Blue divine power that the Mermaid Fairy Tale divine artifact suddenly reacted, bringing us all here through teleportation?”

“Could this artifact possess an autonomous consciousness that replenishes divine power?”

As the fishman youngster pondered this, he distinctly sensed: the Deep Sea Monster Fish's teleportation seemed fundamentally different from the fishmen's arrival.

“We must unravel the secrets of teleportation.”

“If the artifact itself offers only limited clues, then the fishmen are our breakthrough.”

"How I wish we could soon uncover what lies beyond the Ocean Mother Nest! But I must await the findings of the precision eel drillers."

“Ah, if only I could pierce through this barrier directly.”

“There was indeed such an opportunity once, one that could have granted me this ability... Why didn't I think of it back then?”

The fishman youngster sank into reflection, his heart filled with regret.

The so-called opportunity he recalled involved absorbing ghosts, allowing his Bloodcore to record their life essence. Once a certain threshold was reached, the fishman youngster could also use the Bloodcore to transform himself into a ghost.

It was possible.

Long ago, through the joint investigation of Cang Xu and Zi Di, the youngster had discovered that the Bloodcore could absorb the life essence of the undead, recording their bloodline.

Bloodline, at its core, was essentially life information.

Undead life was a unique form of existence, driven by negative energy rather than the positive energy that sustained most living beings.

But the youngster did not do so.

"At that time, Cang Xu controlled only a small number of ghosts. Had I absorbed them using the Bloodcore, it would have significantly weakened both his and Zi Di's combat strength."

“No, that was merely an excuse to deceive myself.”

“Truthfully, the reason I never considered it was my rejection of the undead.”

The fishman youngster engaged in profound self-reflection.

As a normal living being, he had constantly reminded himself not to discriminate against any form of life. Yet, influenced by the positive energy within him, he inevitably felt aversion toward the undead as a natural part of being a living creature.

He had performed remarkably well, interacting with Cang Xu and Zi Di without even noticing this trace of aversion and resistance stirring within him.

Yet now, in his profound self-examination, the fishman youngster suddenly understood.

He could accept Cang Xu and Zi Di because of their close connection to him. It was precisely this bond that prevented him from discriminating against or rejecting them based on their fundamental nature as living beings.

But the dragonman youngster had never once considered transforming himself into an undead.

Nor had Zi Di or Cang Xu ever informed him about this possibility.

“If I could transform into a ghost, perhaps I could pass straight through the cavern walls and reach beyond the Mother Nest.”

The fishman youngster's regret came too late.

With neither Cang Xu nor Zi Di by his side, there were no ghosts for his Bloodcore to devour.

“Zi Di must journey to the Ice Sculpture Capital.”

“Cang Xu... where are you now?”

Cang Xu stood atop the ghost ship's quarterdeck.

An underwater battle raged below.

Countless ghosts darted through the inky depths, while Xiao Wang Zi commanded skeleton archers on deck, unleashing relentless ranged attacks.

Cang Xu remained shielded within a protective circle.

Floating around him were countless skulls of the fallen.

Their foe this time was a school of lanternfish.

These rare deep-sea creatures possessed both light and water attributes.

While water-attribute racial magic were manageable, it was the light-attribute racial magic that dealt devastating damage to the undead forces.

“This is the toughest battle since we entered the White Hair Trench!” lamented the undead ronin Ku Feng.

He charged single-handedly into the lanternfish swarm, displaying the prowess of a silver level fighter.

Other necromancers had grown exponentially stronger through these days of combat. Though Ku Feng was a Fighter, his strength had also surged dramatically.

Wielding bone blades in both hands, he hacked relentlessly, charging in and out. Each time he unleashed a battle skill, it invariably resulted in the gruesome demise of a magic beast lanternfish.

“This is Ku Feng's true combat prowess!”

"During the battle of Two Eyes Island, he never fought at full strength. This level of combat power would undoubtedly rank among the elite within the silver level community."

As Cang Xu observed the battle, his mind continuously formed assessments.

Cang Xu possessed considerable insight.

Whether from his past as a noble steward or his adventures after the Mystifying Monster Island, he was well-versed in the current state of the silver level community, enabling him to make accurate judgments.

Necromancy magic - Soul Healing.

Cang Xu's mind stirred, and he cast the spell directly.

A silver level death head beside him immediately emitted magic fluctuations.

But it took a full thirty seconds before the Soul Healing Spell was successfully released.

Ku Feng received the healing, his wounds rapidly closing. He glanced back gratefully at the ghost ship, thinking: "With Xu Jia's healing, I have almost no worries. This battle was truly exhilarating!"

Once transformed into undead lifeforms, most healing methods in the world became ineffective.

Forcing them could even inflict counter damage on the undead. After all, most healing methods were designed for living beings.

The undead and the living represent two extremes.

Necromancy magic - Flying Bone Shield.

Necromancy magic - Ghost Slip.

Cang Xu cast spells continuously, tending not only to the undead ronin Ku Feng but also to Xiao Wang Zi's skeleton archers and the ghost army on the periphery.

His spellcasting was remarkably effortless, requiring no manual action—merely mental control over the death heads surrounding him.

These skulls had been modified by him, each engraved with one or more necromancy magic, serving as crude, simplified grimoires.

Soon, the lanternfish swarm suffered heavy losses. Led by their gold ranked leader, they broke out of the ghost fog's domain and fled in disarray.

Jian Gui did not pursue with the ghost ship.

The White Hair Trench featured complex terrain teeming with countless magic beasts. Pursuing them would make the ghost ship a prime target. The risks of a reckless chase were too great.

“Thanks for the spell support,” Ku Feng said, returning to the ship and expressing his gratitude to Cang Xu.

Xiao Wang Zi's skeleton archer also benefited from this. Clutching the back of his head, he said, "Xu Jia, I'm so jealous of you. I've never seen anyone cast spells as effortlessly as you do."

“You've gotten yourself a great inheritance, you know?”

“That Flying Head Spell Doctor Sui Shi gave you is seriously powerful.”

“Absolutely.” Jian Gui materialized, wholeheartedly agreeing.

After Cang Xu imprinted the magic model onto the death heads, this marked their first combat deployment.

How effective was it?

Just look at the reactions of Jian Gui, Xiao Wang Zi, and Ku Feng beside him.

“Who knew those death heads could cast spells!” Jian Gui approached Cang Xu, staring at the heads in awe.

The ability to cast spells fundamentally transformed the value of the death heads!

“This must be the true power of Flying Head Spell,” said Xiao Wang Zi, “Xu Jia, you alone can now function as an entire squad of magicians.”

Chapter 630: Will You Sell the Flying Head Spell?

The battle against a school of lanternfish made Cang Xu the star of the group.

The necromancers gathered around Cang Xu, their gazes filled with a mix of curiosity and a faint intensity.

Xiao Wang Zi asked curiously, “Xu Jia, how did your death heads suddenly gain the ability to cast spells? Why haven't I seen you use them before?”

Cang Xu explained that only after engraving magic models into the death heads could they wield corresponding magic.

Xiao Wang Zi understood, reexamining the skulls with renewed understanding. He praised, “So the Flying Head Spell has such ingenious applications?”

Jian Gui pondered, "A creature's skull serves as its cognitive base and the material foundation for spirit power. That must be why death heads can be imprinted with magic models."

"If that's the case, then different death heads should be able to imprint varying numbers of magic models. The higher the intelligence level of the skull's former owner, the more magic models that skull should be able to imprint."

Jian Gui briefly analyzed the situation, then glanced at Cang Xu with a questioning look.

Cang Xu felt a slight chill in his heart and could only nod, “That's right. Exactly.”

Jian Gui was a direct descendant of the orthodox necromancers, possessing profound knowledge of necromancy magic. This allowed him to analyze many things at a glance.

Cang Xu already had extensive practical experience. This experience indeed proved that Jian Gui's analysis was completely correct.

He used numerous undead materials to craft the death heads. He also consciously inscribed magic models onto different types of death heads for comparison.

At this stage, the death heads bearing the most magic models came from the sharp-clawed water monkey.

Sharp-clawed water monkeys themselves held a low status in the White Hair Trench, occupying the middle to lower reaches of the food chain.

Yet compared to other sea beasts, they possessed notably higher intelligence.

The difficulty of engraving magic models onto water monkey skulls was relatively low, yielding a higher success rate.

Cang Xu offered a wry smile to the other necromancers. "Engraving magic models onto these skulls is extremely challenging. What you see are only the finished products. The failed skulls have all been scrapped, their number is five or six times that of the successful ones."

Cang Xu was not exaggerating.

For he had been engraving them within the ghost ship's hold.

Jian Gui could merge with the vessel itself. It was likely that Jian Gui had long observed him engraving these magic models.

Hiding this was pointless; honesty felt far more straightforward and sincere.

Xiao Wang Zi stroked his chin with slender fingers, pondering, "So, these skulls are essentially spellbooks themselves? Imprinting magic models into them is just like crafting individual spellbooks, right?"

“Following that logic, the success rate of engraving magic models should correlate with the necromancer's alchemy expertise, right?”

Xiao Wang Zi paused, turning his gaze toward Cang Xu.

Cang Xu could only nod slightly once more.

Xiao Wang Zi's tone grew increasingly astonished: "If these dead heads are all spellbooks, then the cost of producing spellbooks is ridiculously cheap! That must be the greatest advantage of the Flying Head Spell. The stronger a necromancer's alchemy skill, the lower the cost of crafting spellbooks."

Cang Xu had resolved to remain silent.

In this single battle alone, he hadn't said much, yet both Jian Gui and Xiao Wang Zi had glimpsed many of the mysteries of the Flying Head Spell.

Jian Gui added, “Cost is merely one of its advantages.”

He turned to Xiao Wang Zi: "Death heads are far more practical than spellbooks. Suppose you had countless spellbooks, could you cast them all simultaneously? That's clearly impossible."

“But a necromancer can command these death heads to cast spells in unison.”

Upon hearing this, the undead ronin Ku Feng also wore a look of astonishment.

Even without being a necromancer, he grasped the value of the Flying Head Spell from these words.

Cang Xu sensed trouble and hurriedly explained, “These death heads are far inferior to proper spellbooks. They have many drawbacks.”

"Even if I engrave the magic models onto them, once the death heads cast spells, the model wears down. The more they're used, the more they wear down, until they can no longer cast spells. But that's not the worst part."

“Those skulls that have had magic models engraved but have had them erased from overuse, they can never have new magic models engraved again.”

Xiao Wang Zi listened and suddenly understood: “I see. It's like a piece of paper, once you've scribbled all over it, you can't erase it cleanly. It becomes unusable.”

“Though these death heads are undead entities, not fully formed magicians.”

“No,” Jian Gui corrected, “To be precise, they're merely unfinished products.”

Xiao Wang Zi nodded in agreement, “I also believe these death heads have significant room for improvement.”

This was precisely why Doctor Sui Shi had disseminated the Flying Head Spell.

He intended to release foundational knowledge, allowing diverse users to forge unique paths of advancement through varied experimentation.

Xiao Wang Zi continued: “In their current state, they aren't truly spellbooks, more like bundles of consumable scrolls.”

“But then again, most spellbooks are consumable too, magicians simply replenish materials during use to offset the wear.”

Cang Xu sighed again, “Truthfully, these death heads pale in comparison to regular ghosts, skeletons, or zombies.”

“Their combat capabilities are far weaker.”

“Even if they could be organized into magician squads, couldn't ordinary necromancers do the same?”

"Mass deployment of skeleton archers forms an archer legion; concentrated use of ghosts creates a ghost army. The most classic example is the corpse mage, any squad of corpse mages is essentially a magician squad, isn't it?"

“You're quite right.” Jian Gui continued, “But I believe death heads hold significant advantages over conventional undead lifeforms.”

“If my guess is correct, the mental burden they impose on necromancers should be far less than that of ghosts, zombies, and the like.”

"Moreover, if we're to organize a magician squad, we must be extremely selective. Among the undead lifeforms we create, only a minority possess spellcasting abilities. And even fewer possess identical spellcasting abilities."

“Without shared spells, achieving economies of scale is difficult.”

“Death heads, however, cast spells with remarkable ease and can actively choose which magic models to imprint.”

“For a necromancer to command these death heads in spellcasting is far easier and more precise than managing a magician squad formed from conventional undead.”

Cang Xu was rendered speechless.

Jian Gui's gaze was piercingly sharp, truly worthy of an undead orthodoxy.

He stared at Cang Xu and suddenly asked, “Is this Flying Head Spell for sale?”

At these words, Cang Xu's spirits soared.

Wasn't this precisely the response he'd been hoping for?

Chapter 631: Cang Xu - I am a Pitiful Person

Cang Xu had been executing a plan all along.

That plan was to fully demonstrate the value of the Flying Head Spell, then use this bargaining chip to strike a deal with Jian Gui, thereby obtaining a higher level undead inheritance.

Now, Cang Xu had succeeded.

A mere death head held no interest to Jian Gui. But once it was imprinted with a magic model and gained casting capability, Jian Gui proactively inquired about its price.

Cang Xu steadied his composure, immediately bowed to Jian Gui, and politely presented his request.

Yet Jian Gui shook his head without hesitation.

“Xu Jia, you are unqualified to receive the intermediate undead inheritance.”

"It's not that I personally refuse to sell it to you, but rather the rule set by our founder. This is the principle and bottom line of our undead orthodoxy! Not just me, even the Twelve Saints must strictly adhere to it."

“I'm sorry, I cannot trade you the intermediate undead inheritance. These are non-negotiable.”

“And if I were to impart it to you in the future, there would be only one possibility: that you have earned the right to receive the intermediate undead inheritance!”

As for what qualifications?

Cang Xu had already inquired about them thoroughly back on Weeping Pine Island.

First, Cang Xu's own undead talent must be sufficiently strong, with the minimum standard being a gold level bloodline. Second, Cang Xu must prove his loyalty. He must complete at least three tasks to gain preliminary recognition from the undead lineage. It was like joining a bandit gang—one must submit a pledge of allegiance.

At this moment, Xiao Wang Zi interjected: “Xu Jia, I also want this Flying Head Spell inheritance.”

“But don't sell it too expensively.”

“The fact that Doctor Sui Shi was willing to pass this technique to you proves it's merely an elementary undead inheritance.”

“Expecting to trade an elementary inheritance for an intermediate one? That's simply unacceptable. You're truly a master negotiator, worthy of the Wisteria Merchant Alliance. We're all on the same side, this isn't how business should be done among us."

Ku Feng added, "Frankly, we don't need to buy this specifically from you, do we? Doctor Sui Shi already possesses this legacy. It's just more convenient to acquire it from you."

Jian Gui remained silent. He merely stared impassively at Cang Xu.

A chill gripped Cang Xu's heart.

In this moment, he found himself targeted by everyone else!

Unanimously, the others pressed Cang Xu, attempting to drive down the price.

Cang Xu suddenly realized: though he'd fought alongside these men through life-and-death battles, when it truly mattered, he remained excluded.

Even an undead ronin like Ku Feng spoke for others.

Cang Xu had successfully demonstrated the value of the Flying Head Spell yet faced immense obstacles in the actual transaction!

If Zi Di were here, she would undoubtedly be eloquent and persuasive, extolling the value of the Flying Head Spell tenfold and engaging in tough negotiations with the other party.

But Cang Xu was not Zi Di.

He lacked such exceptional oratory skills for business negotiations.

Years of working and living as a noble steward had cultivated Cang Xu's profound political acumen.

Cang Xu sensed trouble brewing.

He quickly gathered his thoughts. With a bitter expression, he said, “Thank you for enlightening me. You're both correct, my asking price was indeed excessive.”

He openly admitted his mistake.

Then he sighed deeply: “I'm truly sorry, but I'm... simply too poor.”

“As I gradually grasped the true value of this Flying Head Spell inheritance, my feelings became complicated.”

“Had I not staked nearly my entire body and fortune on it, I would have been overjoyed!”

"To this day, I still owe you all a debt. To be perfectly honest, interacting with you has been a tremendous burden. I truly wish to repay what I owe you."

Cang Xu's voice was desolate, his expression painfully genuine. His words left everyone else speechless.

Xiao Wang Zi suddenly realized they had just been ganging up on Cang Xu.

Ku Feng fell silent, a pang of guilt stirring within him. After all, he had witnessed Cang Xu's former misery firsthand. It had to be said, Cang Xu was a pitiful man.

Then, Cang Xu fixed his gaze on Jian Gui: “Lord Jian Gui, since you desire this inheritance, what more is there for me to say?”

“I can only entrust this legacy to you. After all, even this body I now possess is a gift from you!”

Jian Gui flinched in surprise, then without a moment's hesitation, flatly rejected Cang Xu's proposal.

His reaction was entirely within Cang Xu's expectations.

Jian Gui had always been the undisputed leader of the undead, displaying great grace and magnanimity, willing to mentor necromancers. Of course, this was only when his core interests remained unchallenged.

If he were to accept Cang Xu's offering now, it would look as though he forced Cang Xu to surrender it, that would be utterly distasteful.

Especially since the Flying Head Spell wasn't Cang Xu's exclusive possession, if Jian Gui desired it, he could acquire it from others.

A moment's analysis would reveal: Doctor Sui Shi couldn't possibly have entrusted this legacy to only Cang Xu!

Cang Xu breathed a silent sigh of relief.

By playing the pity card, he had defused this minor storm.

His lamentations once again stirred sympathy in Xiao Wang Zi and the others. They recognized Cang Xu as a pitiable figure, possessing the Flying Head Spell was both his fortune and his misfortune.

The group's covetousness toward the technique significantly diminished.

After cleaning the battlefield and distributing the lanternfish-related spoils, everyone returned to their quarters.

Yet a weight settled in Cang Xu's heart.

His plan had succeeded and failed.

The Flying Head Spell had ignited others' desires, yet the price offered was far from satisfactory. Cang Xu could never exchange it for an intermediate undead inheritance.

“Who would've thought Jian Gui would be so principled about this!”

"According to him, even the Twelve Saints couldn't violate this principle. This might not be merely Founder You Shen's favor or command, it could be a magic contract they themselves established and signed."

“Sigh. Though I averted the crisis this time, the problem remains unresolved.”

“As long as I alone master the Flying Head Spell while other necromancers do not, their craving for it will never fade.”

They merely suppressed their craving temporarily out of deference and pride."

On the other hand, Cang Xu had to concede that Xiao Wang Zi's argument during the price negotiation held merit.

This precious knowledge of Flying Head Spell wasn't Cang Xu's exclusive possession.

Even if they couldn't purchase it from him, Jian Gui could still negotiate with Doctor Sui Shi to acquire it from him.

According to intelligence from the Underworld River battle, the fact that Doctor Sui Shi, a full-fledged saint level being, could provide immediate assistance indicated a remarkably harmonious atmosphere among the orthodox necromancers.

Cang Xu fully understood this point.

Orthodox necromancers upheld the principle: Preserve their humanity! Though necromancy revolves around negative energy, necromancers must not be corrupted by it.

Thus, when they first met on Weeping Pine Island, Jian Gui felt a strong affinity for Cang Xu. This stemmed largely from observing that Cang Xu had undergone certain undead modifications to his body yet had not fully embraced the undead path.

Cang Xu did not recklessly pursue power; instead, he constantly strived to maintain the balance between life and death, preserving his humanity.

Among necromancers, the orthodox lineage descending from You Shen possessed the most profound humanity.

In a world where every faction, large and small, hunted necromancers, what would orthodox necromancers, those retaining humanity and immune to negative energy corruption, do?

They would unite tightly, maximizing their chances of survival.

Today you will help me, tomorrow I will help you.

Thus, during the battle in the Underworld River, Jian Gui summoned the aid of the saint level Doctor Sui Shi without incurring any costs afterward.

It must be said that while following Jian Gui's actions exposed Cang Xu to immense risks and genuine peril, the rewards were substantial.

The Flying Head Spell was merely one such gain. The greatest reward came from the invaluable intelligence Cang Xu gleaned about necromancers through their daily interactions.

Through careful analysis of this intelligence, Cang Xu gained precise insight into the overall situation of the necromancer faction and his own personal standing.

“I'm currently on the ghost ship, having demonstrated the value of the Flying Head Spell, yet I haven't commanded a high price.”

“If I possessed Zi Di's negotiating skills, I might easily drive up the price.”

"No, in truth, even if I could do that, I still couldn't purchase the subsequent inheritance I desire from Jian Gui. Let alone the various secrets concerning the memory crystal."

Thinking of the latter, Cang Xu felt another wave of headache.

He had once tried to probe indirectly about the contents of the intermediate undead inheritance. Jian Gui didn't strictly guard this information.

It was as if Jian Gui couldn't reveal the specific contents of the book to Cang Xu but could tell him what the table of contents contained.

Cang Xu was almost certain: whether it was the soul aspect, corpse aspect, or bone aspect, none of the intermediate undead inheritances touched upon the subject of memory crystals. He also speculated: It was highly likely that knowledge related to memory crystals could only be found within the advanced undead inheritance of the soul aspect!

“My previous plan was a bit too naive.”

"Acquiring this knowledge is extremely difficult. After all, for a magician, knowledge is the greatest treasure!"

Yet Cang Xu harbored no regrets about his choice, even though the only genuine part left on his entire body was his skull.

“If all else fails, I'll have to sell the Flying Head Spell to others at a ‘discount’.”

“If I persistently refuse, it might create friction with others, hindering future operations.”

“Acquiring the undead knowledge, I seek will likely be an ultra-long-term project.”

After confronting this reality, Cang Xu settled his mind, scarcely leaving his quarters. He continued crafting death heads, inscribing undead magic models upon them.

He soon discovered that lanternfish heads were unsuitable for death heads.

This likely stemmed from the lanternfish's light attribute.

Light attribute wasn't neutral; it had a strong affinity with positive energy. It was much like the relationship between the dark attribute and negative energy.

Chapter 632: The “Traitor” Betrays Again

Necromancy magic harnessed pure negative energy, so they differed from dark magic.

Of course, lanternfish heads could still be crafted into death heads, though the success rate was far lower than with other skulls.

After consuming all his lanternfish heads, Cang Xu had only managed to create over twenty lanternfish death heads.

When he attempted to inscribe necromancy magic onto the fish heads, he discovered the engraving difficulty for undead magic models had also increased significantly. Moreover, the limited space meant only a few spells could be inscribed onto a single fish head.

By the time Cang Xu had five spell-casting lanternfish heads, he received some good news.

More accurately, the ghost ship's reconnaissance efforts had finally yielded results.

They had discovered the whereabouts of Qi Ling!

More precisely, the remains of a water ghost under Qi Ling's command.

Qi Ling commanded an army of water ghosts that had distinguished themselves during the Weeping Pine Sea Battle. When the scout ghost sent out by the Ghost Ship discovered it, only the legs of this water ghost remained.

The scout ghost brought the leg back to the ghost ship, where Jian Gui immediately cast a spell to confirm it bore Qi Ling's magic aura!

“We've finally found her!” Xiao Wang Zi exclaimed joyfully.

Ku Feng remarked, “Necromancers can only control ghosts within a certain range. Since the water ghost is here, it means Qi Ling must be nearby too.”

Jian Gui breathed a sigh of relief.

Their purpose in coming to the White Hair Trench was to rescue Qi Ling, the “traitor.”

But truthfully, Jian Gui wasn't certain if Qi Ling was even in the White Hair Trench.

Her presence here would be ideal. If not, the necromancers could still save her fishman tribe, freeing them from Lady Sea Snake's control.

Qi Ling cared most for her former clan. Once freed, her greatest burden would lift.

The remnants of the water ghost's leg slowly dissipated, vanishing into nothingness.

The ghost ship returned to where the leg had been found, using that point as a base to explore the surrounding area.

Soon, they uncovered new clues.

From the lingering traces of battle, they deduced that a small school of sardines had surrounded and killed the water ghost.

The sardines' racial magic inflicted damage on ghosts.

This particular water ghost, clearly a scout sent out by Qi Ling, had been surrounded by the sardine school. Outnumbered and overwhelmed, it met a gruesome end.

Its leg drifted with the ocean currents to a nearby location.

Armed with this new lead, Jian Gui dispatched countless ghosts to scour the surrounding area.

Subsequently, the group gradually gathered numerous valuable clues.

Finally, within a submerged mountain hollow, they discovered Qi Ling's fishman clan.

“Qi Ling must be nearby. She worries most about her people,” Xiao Wang Zi murmured. “Even though they've hurt her many times!”

Then, compelling evidence confirmed Xiao Wang Zi's hunch.

The group discovered Qi Ling's scouting water ghosts.

Not just one.

These water ghosts were scattered around the fishman tribe, concealed among the rocks.

Were it not for the faint aura of undead energy they emitted, the necromancers would have struggled to detect them.

The necromancers grew cautious.

They feared alerting the enemy prematurely and preferred to quietly pinpoint Qi Ling's exact location.

To conceal their presence, Jian Gui stationed the ghost ship at a distance while personally leading Xiao Wang Zi and his companions on foot.

Yet after searching covertly for a long time, they still hadn't found Qi Ling.

"Where on earth is she hiding? We've practically turned over every rock nearby." Xiao Wang Zi's patience was the most limited.

“The White Hair Trench has complex terrain. Perhaps there are hidden passages within the cliffs, or even internal caverns?” Ku Feng speculated.

Cang Xu pondered, "It's odd. If Qi Ling is nearby, then Lady Sea Snake shouldn't be far either. Unless Qi Ling breaks free from control, or Lady Sea Snake is overly confident in her methods... But if I were merely a pirate and water magician, I wouldn't presume to control a necromancer."

“Remember, the necromancer faction is a new one.”

Cang Xu voiced his opinion, believing Lady Sea Snake was most likely here too.

“If she's here, then where's her ship?” Jian Gui frowned.

Her flagship during the Weeping Pine Sea Battle had left a deep impression on him.

Xiao Wang Zi shrugged. "We already scouted the sea surface, didn't we? It's as empty as a field, nothing but water and wind."

The group faced a massive obstacle.

They simply couldn't detect Qi Ling, nor Lady Sea Snake who might be right here.

“Actually, this problem can be solved with just a little time.” Cang Xu's words instantly drew everyone's attention.

He didn't keep them in suspense, stating directly, “If we can't find Qi Ling, we can track those water ghosts.”

Ku Feng shook his head. "Even I know that ghost reconnaissance doesn't require returning to report. Necromancers can cast spells to see through a ghost's vision and access its memories."

Cang Xu smiled. "That's precisely the tracking method I'm referring to. We can capture a water ghost alive in secret. While Qi Ling casts spells to excavate the water ghost's memories, we employ tracking spells to pinpoint her exact location. I recall this spell being part of the intermediate soul aspect inheritance."

Jian Gui nodded. "Indeed, it's called the Soul Trace Spell. Its primary function is to discern the location of undead beings through subtle traces. Necromancers who master it often use it to discover and capture powerful or unique spectral entities within the Ghost World."

"It can also be used now to locate Qi Ling. After all, necromancers are undead beings themselves. When she casts undead spells to access the water ghost's vision or memories, that constitutes an undead trace. However, I cannot guarantee success, as I rarely employ this spell."

After hearing Jian Gui's explanation, Xiao Wang Zi and Ku Feng instantly understood.

Where could one find numerous powerful, unique undead beings?

The Ghost World, of course.

Exploring a plane like the Ghost World required a necromancer to be at least saint level; gold level was too risky.

Back then, Doctor Sui Shi had discovered Jian Gui's distress signal while exploring the Ghost World.

Following Cang Xu's suggestion, the group captured a bronze level water ghost alive.

This action carried inherent risk.

It risked alerting Qi Ling.

After escorting the water ghost aboard the ghost ship and examining it, Jian Gui made a new discovery.

When he reappeared on deck, he announced, “Good news.”

Xiao Wang Zi beamed. "So, the Soul Trace Spell worked? Tell me, where exactly is Qi Ling hiding?"

“In the sky,” Jian Gui replied.

"What?!" Xiao Wang Zi strained to widen his eye sockets, well, all he had left were eye sockets.

“I see,” Ku Feng realized.

Jian Gui continued, “There's always been a white cloud on the sea surface. The Fluffy Cloud is hiding within that cloud.”

“The Fluffy Cloud?” Cang Xu questioned.

Jian Gui knew about them: "It's the Powerless Pirates. They're also part of the Fire Beard Pirates. It seems they were invited here by Lady Sea Snake. That's the intel Qi Ling provided."

This surprised the others.

Xiao Wang Zi asked uncertainly, “Lord Jian Gui, you've made contact with Qi Ling?”

“That's right. Qi Ling anticipated our arrival and rigged the water ghost. Through the water ghost, we can now communicate with her and exchange basic intelligence. That's the good news I mentioned!" Jian Gui declared.

It truly was excellent news!

Qi Ling's “betrayal” stemmed from having her vulnerabilities exploited, she had no choice. During the Weeping Pine Sea Battle, she clearly held back.

She also anticipated that Jian Gui and the others would come to rescue her, so she preemptively tampered with the scouting water ghost.

Cang Xu speculated: Perhaps her trust in Jian Gui stemmed from being summoned by him and serving as his alchemy assistant for many years, fostering deep familiarity.

Now, Qi Ling had secretly established contact with Jian Gui. With an “eye” within the enemy's ranks, the situation for the necromancers immediately turned favorable.

“The Fluffy Cloud is hiding within the clouds. How can we strike?” Xiao Wang Zi fretted.

“It's difficult,” Jian Gui shook his head, “My ghost ship cannot fly yet.”

Cang Xu's heart stirred. He keenly discerned a secret in Jian Gui's words, could the ghost ship actually fly?

“I'm useless in aerial combat,” Ku Feng admitted, “Only the ghost army can maintain combat effectiveness.”

“Exactly,” Xiao Wang Zi lamented, clutching his massive head. "My skeleton archers pose no threat to the Fluffy Cloud. My skeleton magicians are too few, and worst of all, my ramhorn skeleton turtle fleet is utterly useless!"

Among his skeleton forces, the most formidable were those ramhorn skeleton turtles. Their current numbers had more than doubled since the initial expansion.

Cang Xu advised, “We must lure them into the sea.”

“Mm-hmm, exactly!” Xiao Wang Zi nodded vigorously, “Luring them to the ocean floor would be ideal, then my heavy cavalry might finally prove useful.”

Ku Feng added, “We operate in the shadows while they're exposed. We can take our time strategizing.”

Jian Gui shook his head. "The pirates aboard the Fluffy Cloud aren't our only enemies. According to Qi Ling's intel, another pirate crew will arrive here for reinforcements in the coming days."

“The Heroism Pirates.”

“But according to the plan, they must first dock at Flower Skirt Island for several days.”

“So, we don't have much time left.”

“We'd better lure them down here soon!”

But how?

Xiao Wang Zi pondered for a moment, then suddenly looked up: “I've got it!”

His head jerked up too forcefully, and with a crack, his skull separated from his neck.

His round, skull-like head bounced onto the deck.

Xiao Wang Zi's body scrambled over, crouched down, and reattached his head: “We can attack Qi Ling's clan!”

“That way, we'll lure those pirates down!”

Seeing everyone fall silent, Xiao Wang Zi pressed on: “Why do you think they're hiding in the clouds?”

“I don't believe they're lying in wait for us.”

“They're probably threatening and exploiting Qi Ling's clan to hunt sea beasts on a massive scale.”

"If we strike at the clan now, they'll be shocked and come after us. They'll think we're here for revenge—after all, Qi Ling betrayed us!"

Chapter 633: The Low IQ Small Prince

After hearing Xiao Wang Zi's meticulously crafted plan, the others remained silent for a long while.

Receiving no response, Xiao Wang Zi pressed, "What do you think? It's a brilliant idea, right?"

The other three exchanged quick glances.

Cang Xu looked at the smug Xiao Wang Zi and suddenly understood a past incident involving him.

From what Cang Xu had heard from others, when Xiao Wang Zi first encountered Jian Gui and the others, he repeatedly found himself in peril, only to be rescued time and again by Jian Gui. It could be said that without Jian Gui, Xiao Wang Zi would have been finished long ago.

At the time, Cang Xu found it somewhat strange.

Why did Xiao Wang Zi keep getting into trouble? Even with his covert protection, surely the merchant ships of his own kingdom wouldn't be so vulnerable?

Perhaps one or two close calls were unavoidable.

But if one acted cautiously, for example someone like Cang Xu, they wouldn't repeatedly find themselves in peril.

Cang Xu had once darkly speculated that this might be a tactic Jian Gui employed to win over Xiao Wang Zi. Because of this conjecture, Cang Xu hadn't probed further into Xiao Wang Zi's past experiences.

But now, Cang Xu felt that previous conjecture was likely his own overthinking.

Given Xiao Wang Zi's intelligence, encountering multiple crises was entirely expected!

Threatening Qi Ling's fishman tribe to provoke the pirates in the sky into descending to war...

That wasn't a strategy at all, it was downright foolish.

First, if the necromancers voluntarily exposed themselves, they would instantly lose the advantage of operating in the shadows.

Second, would Qi Ling even agree? She cared so deeply for the fishman tribe that she returned to protect them even after becoming a necromancer, even when it meant leaving the Ghost Ship. The moment the fishmen were threatened, Lady Sea Snake blackmailed her, effectively controlling her. Would someone like that ever consent to this plan? Absolutely not!

Third, even if Qi Ling agreed, execution would be nearly impossible. A misstep would backfire spectacularly, becoming a catastrophe.

While necromancers wield broad control over their minions, precision remains elusive. Xiao Wang Zi and his allies might show restraint, but armies of skeletons and specters offer no guarantee each undead will stop short of killing the fishman.

Moreover, living creatures' aversion to the undead ran deep in their bones.

The fishman tribe's reaction would be nearly impossible to control.

The best case scenario would be the fishmen fleeing. In fact, there's a high probability they'd resist and attack.

If the latter occurred, the necromancers would face a severe predicament.

If they engaged in combat, their minions could not stop short of killing, inevitably inflicting casualties among the fishman. What would Qi Ling think? If casualties mounted, Qi Ling might well turn to hatred and betrayal!

If they didn't fight, the only option was retreat. What would the pirates think upon witnessing this? The necromancers' wavering resolve clearly showed they had no desire for vengeance against the fishman clan. Why? It's simple, they're undoubtedly mindful of Qi Ling's feelings. If they care about Qi Ling, they must be trying to save her. Perhaps they'd already made secret arrangements with Qi Ling!

Finally, would this scheme truly lure the pirates down?

Not necessarily.

Lady Sea Snake's gold level sea serpent was already dead, and she herself was only silver level. The captain aboard the Fluffy Cloud, also leader of the Powerless Pirates, Ruan Tang Ren, was likewise only silver level.

That's why Lady Sea Snake also invited the Heroism Pirates. Their leader possessed gold level cultivation.

Facing the undead horde, even if Jian Gui and ghost ship don't show themselves, Lady Sea Snake and Ruan Tang Ren would likely cower aboard their ship, unable to move.

Anyone with half a brain would connect the undead army to Jian Gui's crew.

After all, Jian Gui was gold level!

In short, Xiao Wang Zi's plan was utterly foolish. Rather than implementing it, a direct assault would be far more effective.

Cang Xu remained silent, saying nothing. He wouldn't provoke Xiao Wang Zi for no reason.

Ku Feng also kept his mouth shut, as if he hadn't heard Xiao Wang Zi's words at all.

Xiao Wang Zi's gaze was fixed primarily on Jian Gui.

Jian Gui stepped up to Xiao Wang Zi, reached out, and stroked his round, smooth skull. "Not a bad plan. You clearly put a lot of thought into it. But maybe don't make plans anymore."

"Ah?!" Xiao Wang Zi froze.

“Besides this plan, what other ideas do you have?” Jian Gui asked Ku Feng and Cang Xu.

Ku Feng shrugged, indicating he'd just follow Jian Gui into battle with his sword. He wouldn't think about anything else.

“Who knew Ku Feng could be so cunning,” Cang Xu thought to himself, feeling the pressure mount.

He pondered, "As things stand, our understanding of the enemy remains limited. At the very least, we still don't know why Lady Sea Snake is here, why she invited the Powerless and the Heroism Pirates, and why her pirate ship is nowhere to be found."

“Since we've already established communication with Qi Ling, why not ask her?”

Cang Xu's words sounded simple, yet they clearly conveyed his stance.

He wanted Qi Ling to devise a plan, with Jian Gui and the others providing support.

Qi Ling's widespread deployment of water ghost scouts would certainly not escape Lady Sea Snake's notice. The magic fluctuations generated when communicating with water ghosts to gain vision and memories were intense.

Therefore, Lady Sea Snake must have consented to Qi Ling's reconnaissance.

What Lady Sea Snake didn't know was that Qi Ling had tampered with the water ghosts' bodies.

Since Qi Ling possessed reconnaissance capabilities, it was more effective to make the pirates wary, prompting them to misjudge the situation and launch an attack from the sea.

"Hmph! What's the point of asking that traitor? She should be grateful that we came to rescue her and just wait obediently for help!" Xiao Wang Zi was somewhat annoyed.

After hearing Cang Xu's words, Jian Gui sighed again, as if to say: Finally, someone who understands.

He nodded slightly: "I cannot communicate with Qi Ling for too long, nor too frequently. The more times and the longer the duration, the easier it is to be exposed."

“Still, these matters should indeed be clarified first.”

“I'll contact her in due time.”

Cang Xu remained unaware of the nature of this communication method. He speculated it might be part of the intermediate soul aspect inheritance. But considering Jian Gui's principles, Qi Ling likely hadn't been taught knowledge of this caliber.

Cang Xu recalled: Qi Ling had once served as an alchemy assistant, spending many years at Jian Gui's side.

“Perhaps this is some sort of alchemy method?”

Soon after, Jian Gui reappeared and informed the others: “I've inquired.”

“Lady Sea Snake's familiar has perished. She came here specifically to search for a gold level sea serpent to re-establish a familiar contract.”

“To this end, she enlisted the Heroism Pirates. With their gold level captain leading the charge, they can handle a gold level sea serpent beast.”

"Simultaneously, she invited Ruan Tang Ren. Though only silver level, he possesses proven combat experience. He can counter gold level entities, rendering them powerless, limp, and unable to fight."

“Ruan Tang Ren will weaken the gold level sea serpent's abilities, making it easier for the Heroism Pirates Captain to capture it alive.”

"As for Qi Ling, only Lady Sea Snake knows her true identity. The other two are unaware she's a necromancer. Lady Sea Snake disguised her. In peacetime, Qi Ling handles reconnaissance of the ocean."

"Naturally, Lady Sea Snake and her crew also conduct surveillance. But while they hide within the clouds, the water ghost has the greatest advantage."

Xiao Wang Zi listened with delight: “If that's the case, we can capture a gold level sea serpent and lure them into the sea to strike!”

Jian Gui replied: “Three days ago, they already identified a suitable target.”

“That's precisely why Lady Sea Snake summoned the Heroism Pirates for backup.”

This arrangement made perfect sense.

The Heroism Pirates was a gold level force, while Lady Sea Snake was merely silver level. Especially after losing her gold level sea serpent, her confidence had waned considerably.

It was unlikely she could afford to keep a gold level expert constantly by her side, waiting endlessly for the right target to appear.

The Heroism Pirates naturally had their own affairs to attend to.

Just as Bu Quan, despite being valued by the Alchemy Guild, wouldn't have a gold level member assigned to secretly guard it at all times.

The value of gold level power determined the difficulty and cost of enlisting their services.

“Then why don't we just take action against that sea serpent?” Xiao Wang Zi called out again.

The more he schemed, the more he revealed himself to be a fool.

Upon hearing this, Cang Xu dismissed the foolish plan without hesitation.

Attacking a gold level sea serpent? Lady Sea Snake would never come down.

With so many sea beasts in the White Hair Trench, finding another gold level sea serpent wouldn't be difficult.

Even if Jian Gui and ghost ship hadn't appeared, the undead army dared to target a gold level sea serpent. How could the silver level Lady Sea Snake and Ruan Tang Ren risk venturing into the sea?

Cang Xu pondered silently: “This intelligence is a mixed blessing. It all depends on how Jian Gui chooses to act.”

The ghost ship could not fly, but Jian Gui was gold level, and a necromancer at that.

In truth, if Jian Gui moved now, it would hold a significant advantage against the enemies aboard the Fluffy Cloud.

If the situation dragged on, allowing the leader of the Heroism Pirates to join the fray, things would become far more complicated.

But Jian Gui chose not to take the risk of acting directly.

After a brief discussion, Jian Gui made a decision: to lie in wait around the targeted sea serpent, carefully set the stage for battle, and wait for the right moment to strike.

Cang Xu understood Jian Gui's hesitation.

His concern was simple and obvious; it was that gold level Shadow Assassin.

Qi Ling didn't know this person, but that didn't mean they weren't here.

Near the waters of Flower Skirt Island.

At the base of an uninhabited, barren isle, Lan Zao deftly opened the chamber door and swam out into the sea.

All around was utter stillness.

Every cell in Lan Zao seemed to be laughing, a constant surge of exhilaration and joy welling up within him.

After declining Bing Xiao's recruitment offer, he had repeatedly traveled here via teleportation for training.

Sometimes Shuang Tu accompanied him.

Sometimes he came alone.

Lan Zao preferred solitude.

During these times, he would immerse himself in the sea, letting the water envelop him from all sides.

He would remain motionless, drifting silently through the depths.

He loved the sea.

This affection predated his bloodline awakening; it had always been there.

He was born and raised in a coastal fishing village.

Even as a child, he loved diving into the sea, holding still as the currents carried him in any direction, or slowly sinking while gazing through the water's glow at the vast, azure sky above.

Such a pure blue, without a trace of impurity.

The sky's blue usually seemed cold and distant, high above. But filtered through the seawater, this shade of blue became soft, elegant, melancholic, and approachable.

Lan Zao loved this blue most of all. Immersed in this deep blue, he could forget himself, forget life's petty troubles, dissolve all worries and frustrations.

Looking back now, everything had its origin.

Back then, though his bloodline hadn't awakened, the Fish Bladder bloodline had already flowed within him.

All the signs were there from the start.

Chapter 634: Lan Zao’s Future

A pristine sky, an azure sea.

Lan Zao swam freely through the ocean, startling schools of fish into scattered flight.

After his bloodline awakened, his appearance underwent significant changes.

With every stroke, the webbing between his fingers and toes provided immense propulsion. Simultaneously, his arms, thighs, and calves bulged with powerful muscles, allowing him to surge forward great distances with a mere flick of his wrist or swing of his leg.

Before his bloodline awakened, Lan Zao had been a seasoned sailor for years, possessing swimming skills far surpassing ordinary humans.

Now, his aquatic prowess had surged exponentially. Though he couldn't quite match the fishman who had lived in the sea their entire lives, he was not far off.

“This is the power of bloodline...” Lan Zao's heart brimmed with awe.

Over these days, he had truly grasped the weight of the word “bloodline.”

He now understood completely why the entire world placed such immense value on bloodlines, a truth unchanged throughout history.

Simply because bloodline was truly that crucial!

The mere alteration of his bloodline had granted Lan Zao extraordinary enhancement. His aquatic prowess had shattered his previous limits. The cost?

Merely eating, drinking, and undergoing some training each day.

“Of course, the meals Shuang Tu provided were top level magic delicacies, crafted specifically for my needs.”

“The training regimen he designed for me was also tailor-made. I could genuinely feel how incredibly efficient these exercises were!”

“Thank goodness I'm part of the Dragon Lion Mercenary Corps, otherwise, I wouldn't receive such preferential treatment.”

“It proves beyond doubt that following the commander was my most correct, wisest decision!”

Lan Zao developed a fundamental trust in allies like Shuang Tu, feeling fortunate for his circumstances and having his suspicions toward Zi Di fade considerably.

All of this only deepened his admiration and obedience toward the dragonman youngster.

The misunderstandings surrounding him and Zi Di, stemming from the bloodline purification potion he consumed, along with a series of fateful encounters, had shifted Lan Zao's inner convictions.

The nightmares of that time had long faded from Lan Zao's memory.

The migration of his deepest beliefs occurred silently.

Even Lan Zao himself remained unaware.

Lan Zao suddenly retracted his limbs, lying completely flat. Without moving a muscle, he still sped through the sea, his pace only slightly slower than before.

He was riding the currents.

Just as winds in the sky created stable air currents that skilled birds used to extend their flight time and distance, the ocean, filled with water, also formed currents.

Some currents were vast and persistent, but most were minute and fleeting.

Lan Zao now resembled a skilled bird, harnessing a transient current to glide effortlessly through the depths.

“What a marvelous sensation!”

Though far from his first experience, Lan Zao still felt profound delight and wonder.

He could sense the currents, every single one, as long as it fell within his range of perception.

This was transcendent perception; a talent derived from his racial magic - Fluid Perception.

The fishman youngster had gained four racial magic from his Surging Wave fishman bloodline: Healing, Affinity Aura, Mental Flogging, and Mind's Eye.

Lan Zao also drew two racial magics from his Fish Bladder bloodline. One of these was Fluid Perception.

As the name implied, it granted perception of flowing objects.

It was much like humans sensing wind direction by perceiving air currents. Yet for ordinary people, discerning wind direction proved difficult unless facing strong gusts. Trained rifle snipers and archers possessed this skill. They often moistened their fingertips with saliva to heighten their sensitivity to air currents.

Fluid Perception was this ability but elevated to a transcendent level.

Even in everyday situations, Lan Zao could sense certain fluid movements.

When he concentrated his spirit on this perception, his range expanded severalfold, and his senses became far more precise.

Lan Zao drifted forward with the ocean currents.

He remained almost motionless, as if lying on an invisible flying carpet in the sea, effortless and utterly comfortable.

When the currents he rode shifted slightly, he would subtly adjust his posture to maintain his blend within the flow.

These micro-currents were ever-changing.

Yet with his extraordinary gift of current perception, Lan Zao effortlessly allowed him to become almost one with the sea's rhythm.

He cherished this sensation.

Each time he swam freely in the sea, a faint, boundless joy would well up from the depths of his being.

Every training session in the ocean was not arduous for him, but rather filled with pure enjoyment.

He looked forward to the upcoming underwater training with anticipation.

Lan Zao closed his eyes, the six black lines on each cheek opening and closing.

These were his newly formed organs, fish gills, created after his bloodline awakened.

The gills closed at least six times faster than before, allowing Lan Zao to rapidly draw ample air from the sea.

This air filled his fish bladder through his trachea. This too was a new organ, the most defining feature of the Fish Bladder bloodline.

The fish bladder rapidly expanded, imparting buoyancy to Lan Zao.

As buoyancy grew, he broke free from the ocean currents' grip and shot upward toward the surface.

In his mind, Lan Zao recalled what Shuang Tu had once told him: “Once the Fish Bladder bloodline concentration reaches 30%, a transcendent can consciously control the air concentration within their body.”

"Therefore, you can compress the air within your fish bladder. This is also the source of the projectiles for the Air Cannon battle skill you're beginning to cultivate."

"What you must do now is compress all the air within your body, absorb as much air as possible to reach your limit, and dissolve it into your bloodstream. By doing so, you will enhance your blood circulation and significantly boost your physical capabilities."

“You need to replace your battle qi technique. The Water Grass battle qi technique you mastered is no longer suitable for you.”

This was the core of Lan Zao's current training regimen.

Through rapid breathing, he replenished his body with vast amounts of air, consciously accelerating blood circulation to elevate his physical prowess.

Shuang Tu had provided him with extensive training materials for a Fish Bladder bloodline transcendent. These documents contained explicit descriptions of this state, the Maximum Air State.

In contrast, the initial state where the fish bladder held no stored air, so subtle Lan Zao couldn't even perceive it, was termed the Minimal Air State.

Lan Zao had now entered the Maximum Air State.

As he neared the surface, he abruptly opened his eyes.

Typically, fishman lack eyelids, but Lan Zao was not a fishman; he retained this bodily structure. When he opened his eyes, however, his eyelids retracted to their absolute limit, becoming nearly invisible.

Lan Zao flicked his arms, exerting no force with his legs. His entire body propelled upward like a leap, bursting through the sea surface.

The sky was clear and cloudless.

Sunlight bathed his smooth, pale blue skin, making countless droplets glisten like diamonds.

Amidst the surging waves, Lan Zao did not fall directly. He spread his arms wide, like a flying fish unfurling its fins, allowing him to glide through the air.

At 30% Fish Bladder bloodline, the bearer could control their own air concentration. This allowed Lan Zao to rapidly adjust internal and external pressure, preventing injuries from rapid ascent and resulting pressure imbalances.

At 60% Fish Bladder bloodline, the bearer gained the ability to freely ascend and descend in water, along with the racial magic, Water Flow Perception.

Above 95%, they could float mid-air. Though the floating height was limited, they gained the racial magic, Air Current Perception.

At 100%, the floating height increased significantly, and the related perception talent upgraded to Fluid Perception.

Hovering above the sea surface, Lan Zao glided over five meters before plunging back into the water with a splash.

As he plunged, he extended his arms forward and pressed his legs together, striving to form a straight line.

His smooth skin minimized the splash upon entry. Once submerged, he tucked his forearms close to his head and kicked his legs like a fishman's tail, accelerating his speed.

After swimming for a while, he leapt back out of the water, gliding through the air.

Countless droplets of spray, illuminated by the sunlight, sparkled brilliantly, seeming to dance and laugh with Lan Zao.

He was practicing another racial magic - Levitation.

Thanks to the enhancement from his bloodline, the distance Lan Zao could glide through the air was increasing at a visible rate.

“What a thrill!” Lan Zao cheered inwardly.

As he glided through the air, suspended between sea and sky, he felt an elf-like agility and the vastness of nature. When he plunged back into the water, the soft sea embraced and caressed his entire body, like a gentle mother tenderly stroking her beloved child.

Upon his fifth leap into the sky, Lan Zao's life level underwent a transformation.

At this moment, he ascended from bronze level to iron level!

“I've advanced!”

Lan Zao's heart trembled, tears streaming down his face.

He had long reached the peak of the bronze level, held back by his innate limitations. But the awakening of his bloodline had shattered those limits, propelling him far beyond. The bronze level was no longer his ceiling.

He had advanced.

Both his life level and battle qi cultivation had soared to iron level.

Though Shuang Tu had informed him, and his bronze level battle qi had taken on an iron sheen days prior, when this moment finally arrived, Lan Zao was still shaken to his core, overcome with emotion!

Tears fell from the sky, mingling with the spray of waves along his gliding path.

As he plunged into the water, the vast ocean embraced him like a mother, wiping away his tears and offering gentle solace, as if welcoming a wandering son home at last.

How many years have passed?

The freshness and innocence of youth had long faded from his face.

The reverence and naivety of young adulthood had been ground away by life's hardships, leaving no trace.

Fate's merciless torment had once beaten him into a shadow of his former self.

The shackles of guilt weighed heavily on his heart. He had contemplated suicide yet lacked the courage to act.

The instant he plunged into the sea with iron level battle qi, his eyes opened beneath the force of the currents, revealing scenes from his past.

Once, he had curled up in the sea, soaking himself to escape monotony and frustration.

Once, he had labored on the deck, drenched in sweat, the scorching sun lashing him like whips, his salt-crusted skin stinging with pain.

Once, he lay on a lonely boat, arms raised, fingers splayed wide. Through the gaps between his fingers, he gazed at the midnight stars, weeping silently: "Bronze is my limit! Damn it, damn it all..."

Once, he lay in the desert, drinking blood from the corpse of a loved one to quench his thirst!

Flash after flash of the past streaked before his eyes, finally settling on the figure of the dragonman youngster.

“My life, my... future!” Suddenly, the visions vanished before Lan Zao's eyes, leaving them crystal clear.

Boom!

Without warning, a massive golden battle qi hand tore through the air, slamming downward.

His Fluid Perception instantly alerted Lan Zao to danger. With agile reflexes, he dove deeper into the sea.

But in the next instant, the giant hand split into two, one on each side, crashing into the sea and pursuing relentlessly.

Lan Zao couldn't outrun the giant hands and was soon cornered.

The giant hands seized Lan Zao and flung him back onto the ship.

He slammed onto the deck.

He looked up and saw a pirate flag first.

The Heroism Pirates!

Chapter 635: Battle Qi Change

Lan Zao lay sprawled on the deck, thoroughly disoriented from the fall. The back of his head, which had slammed against the deck, throbbed with pain.

The first thing he saw in his field of vision was a pirate flag.

The black pirate flag fluttered in the wind. At its center was a white skull, its mouth wide open in a roar.

Radiating from the skull's gaping maw were concentric white circles, resembling the violent shockwaves generated by its roar.

“The Heroism Pirates!” The answer flashed instantly through Lan Zao's mind.

Among all the survivors, he was arguably the most familiar with pirate identities.

Having served as the boatswain of the Hog's Kiss for years, he made his living on the seas and knew every pirate by name.

He recognized the Heroism Pirates, for they weren't just a gold level pirate force, they were also members of the Fire Beard Pirates.

Pirates who joined the Fire Beard Pirates were never ordinary folk.

Lan Zao's gaze shifted downward. Following the pirate flag along the mast, he spotted a massive figure.

Gritting his teeth against the pain, he rolled over swiftly and stood up, glaring at the man seated beneath the mainmast.
